**B. The request, mirroring `requestDocking()` line for line.** `SpaceWorld::hailTarget()` is written against `space_world.cpp:2308-2341` deliberately, so that a drive which knows one knows the other:

- no ship selected → `say("Comms", "No ship selected to hail.")`;
- beyond `kHailRange = 20'000.0` m → out of comms range. **The same 20 km a station is hailed from**, so the player learns one number rather than two;
- **hostile → they refuse, in their own voice.** Standing is read through `playerAttitudeName(factionIndex)`, the exact call `dock_request` is handed, so "who will talk to me" and "who will let me dock" can never drift apart;
- **already hailed → they repeat themselves, verbatim.** This is the anti-slot-machine rule: one `bool` plus the composed reply held on `ShipPilot`, so re-hailing replays what was said rather than re-rolling for a better answer.

Otherwise the hail is queued — `m_pendingHail` plus a seeded roll, drained by `GameContent` next frame — exactly as a dock request is.

**C. `pilot_hail`, the sixth hook of that shape, with one deliberate difference from `dock_request`.** C++ enumerates what the pilot could know: their name, role, faction name, the player's standing and attitude, whether a market tip is available, whether a place tip is available, and one seeded roll. Lua composes through one of three builders, each validated:

- `sol.hail_reply(message)` — words only, no tip;
- `sol.hail_tip_market(message)` — C++ then picks and validates the market and calls `recordMarket`;
- `sol.hail_tip_place(message)` — C++ then picks and validates the site and calls `addBookmark`.

⚑ **Lua composes the words; C++ picks the fact.** In `dock_request` the script chose the berth, because a berth is one of four interchangeable integers and a script cannot get it meaningfully wrong. **A tip is a claim about the galaxy.** A hook that could name the market or the position could bookmark a point in interstellar space, or record prices for a station that does not exist, and the player would have no way to tell a bug from a lying pilot. So the hook decides **what kind of tip and how it sounds**; the engine decides **which one**, from what that pilot could plausibly know.

**D. What a pilot knows lives in the engine, as a pure function, so it can be tested.** New `engine/sim/include/sol/sim/pilot_tips.hpp` — the shape 8r gave `docking.hpp` and 8q gave the fog rule, for the same reason: `game/src/` has no test suite, and a selection rule that cannot be asserted headlessly will be verified by screenshot forever.

- **A Trader knows a price.** Choose a market within `kIntelJumpRadius` hops of the current system, **preferring one the player has no memory of at all, then the stalest one they have** (`SurveySim::remembered` / `isStale` answer both). Snapshot it with `recordMarket`, exactly as `buy_intel` does, so the tip lands in the Trade tab's "elsewhere" column and the galaxy map's trade overlay **with no new UI whatsoever**.
- **A Fighter or a Patrol knows a place.** Choose an **unresolved signal** from `signalsFor` in a system the player has at least Charted, preferring this system and then the nearest, and drop a bookmark on it with `label = kTipLabel` so the map can tell a rumour from a place the player wrote down themselves.
- **A pilot with nothing to say says so, and that is a real answer** rather than a failure path. A neighbourhood the player has already surveyed and traded across should run out of rumours; a game that always has one more tip is a game where the tip means nothing.

**E. The scriptless default**, per the rule the other five follow: with no scripts at all a hostile pilot refuses and everyone else answers with a flat line plus whatever tip their role affords. `init.lua` gets an authored `pilot_hail` so that a trader sounds like a trader and a patrol sounds like a patrol. **Every line drops the speaker's name** — 8r's hard-won rule, since the comms panel's sender column already says who is talking and the repetition is what overran the panel.

**F. No UI code changes, which is the payoff of 8r having built the channel general.** The reply arrives through `say(pilotName, text)` on the existing ring. The one addition is a prompt chip — `[Y] HAIL` when a ship contact is selected and in range — drawn by the same `game_ui.cpp` path that already builds `[G] REQUEST DOCKING` from live bindings.

**G. Console, because the assertion comes before the screenshot.** `sol.hail()` performs the real hail on the current selection; `sol.hail_target(namePart)` selects then hails, so a drive need not cycle contacts blind; `sol.tips()` prints what the player has been told — bookmarks at `kTipLabel` and market memories with their ages. This is 8q's and 8r's rule that the console assertion is built first and pixels are spent only on what is genuinely visual.

**Save v10, unchanged.** The hailed flag and the held reply ride on `ShipPilot`, which is not serialised for spawned ships (8l's precedent). Bookmarks and market memory are **already** in the save at v10 and a tip writes fields that already exist, `label` included. `settings.toml` gains one binding row, which is additive — a file without it reads the default, per 8k's rule that a missing key means "use the default".

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **A comms screen or a dialogue tree** — the three-line ring is the surface, and a conversation UI is a larger item than this one. **Pilots hailing the player unprompted.** **Any economic or reputation effect from talking** — no standing gain, no paying for a tip, no haggling; that is a trade system wearing a comms hat. **Missions from pilots** (rumour → contract), which is `MissionSim`'s business and needs an offer source. **Voice, portraits, or pilot names beyond the display name a contact already carries.** **Hailing stations for anything but docking** — 8r owns that channel. **Persisting which pilots have been hailed** across a jump or a save, which would need spawned ships serialised to mean anything. **NPC-to-NPC chatter.** **Tips about anything that is not a market or a site** — no "this system is dangerous", no faction gossip, because neither has a store to land in and a tip the game cannot record is a line of text pretending to be information.

**Exit**: selecting a ship contact and pressing `Y` puts a reply from that pilot, by name, on the comms panel; a trader's tip writes a market memory that then shows in the station Trade tab's "elsewhere" column and on the galaxy map's trade overlay for a station the player has never docked at; a fighter's or patrol's tip drops a labelled bookmark that appears on the radar, in the target cycle and on the system map, and Autopilot flies to it; re-hailing the same pilot repeats the same words and grants no second tip; a hostile pilot refuses in words; hailing with nothing selected, or with a ship out of range, says so rather than doing nothing; a pilot with nothing left to tell says so; `sol.hail()`, `sol.hail_target()` and `sol.tips()` track every one of those transitions; new `sim.unit` coverage for the tip-selection rules (the unknown-then-stalest market preference, the unresolved-site preference, and the empty case); save format unchanged at v10 and a `settings.toml` with no `hail_target` row still boots with `Y` bound; all 8 suites green, zero warnings.

*(Verified live 2026-08-18, seed 1701, 1280×720 @ UI scale 1.0, across four drives — a hail drive, a place drive, a panel/key drive and a settings-upgrade drive. Every exit criterion met. 13 files, sim 103 → **109**, save unchanged at **v10**, **36 actions** (`hail_target`, default `Y`).*

***The conversation works and the console carries it.** `sol.hail()` on a neutral Solar Navy freighter 203 m out answers `comms: You: Hailing Freighter (Solar Navy).` then `comms: Freighter: Prices were worth the run at Saria Alpha, Saria.`, with `pilot tip: market 8 (Saria Alpha, Saria)` in the log and `sol.tips()` reading **`price 8: Saria Alpha, Saria - 3 s old` / `0 rumour(s), 1 market(s) remembered, 1 pilot(s) hailed here`**. **The repeat rule is proved by the counter, not by the words**: re-hailing printed the identical sentence and `sol.tips()` still said `1 market(s) remembered` — the second hail granted nothing. A hostile (an unaffiliated spawn, player-hostile by the pre-8b rule) answered the authored `Wrong channel. Break off.` and left the count at `1 market(s) remembered, 2 pilot(s) hailed here`. At 40.1 km — twice the hail range — `Freighter (Solar Navy) is out of comms range.`, and the `[Y] HAIL` chip disappears from the prompt row on its own.*

***A place tip becomes a real destination, which is the whole of 8i's one-slot argument re-run.** A patrol in Saria named a site and `sol.tips()` read `place 1: Rumour in Saria`; `sol.bookmarks()` `1: Rumour - 544566 km away`. Then all four surfaces answered: `sol.target("Rumour")` selected it and the HUD readout showed **`* Rumour  544566.0 km`**; the radar drew it; `sol.system_map()` — the real fill — printed **`bookmark  * Rumour - 544566 km - bookmark`**; and `sol.autopilot(2000)` logged `Autopilot: flying to '* Rumour' (arrive 2.0 km out)` with the ship closing at 566 km/s. One `addBookmark` call bought four things, exactly as claimed.*

***The `Y` binding is real and survives an upgrade.** The key itself (not `sol.hail()`) produced the whole sequence with the console hidden and the overlay off. Against a hand-written pre-8s `settings.toml` carrying `jump = "K"` and no `hail_target` row, `sol.bindings()` answered **`HAIL[Y] JUMP[K]`** — the `K` is the negative control that separates "the default was used" from "the file was never read". Quitting through the pause menu exited **0**, so nothing in the frame complained. All **8** suites green on a clean rebuild, zero warnings, and `build/dev/bin/` left with no `settings.toml`, `imgui.ini` or `world.sav`.)*

**What driving it changed about the plan.** Two defects, and neither was visible from the code.

**The first pilot hailed named the station 83 m away.** `chooseMarketTip` ranked purely by unknown-then-nearest, and the nearest market is the one you are parked next to — so a trader's one answer was spent telling the player about a board they could read out the window. `hops == 0` is now skipped, with the reason beside it: **that is a window, not a tip.** It is also the rule `buyMarketIntel` has followed since 8g for its own reason, so the exclusion is the same fact stated twice rather than a new policy. Worth noting the asymmetry that survived: a *place* tip still prefers the current system, because a site is hidden until scanned even when it is local, whereas a market is not.

**⚑ "Freighter (Solar Navy)" overran the comms sender column and overwrote the message beside it** — the panel read `Freighter (Solar Navy)s were worth the run at Saria Alpha, Saria.`, with the name printed straight through the word *Prices*. `addTextInBox` does not clip to its box: **the same fact 8i recorded about `ui.selectable` and 8k about `ui.label`, now found a third time**, and this is the first phase to actually fix it rather than route around it. 8r sized `kSenderWidth` at 116 px against station names, which fit; a pilot's name carries its faction (Phase 8b) and does not. Fixed in **both** the places 8r's own comms fix landed: the cell now `pushClip`s, because **a column is a budget and it gives way — the message is the content**; and the sender is reduced to a callsign (`radioName` strips the parenthetical), because **the target panel already prints the faction twice, an inch to the right**. That second half is 8r's own rule — drop what the neighbouring column already says — applied one item later to the panel 8r built.

**Known gaps.** **A tip is a fact, not a promise**: nothing verifies the pilot was telling the truth, because nothing in the game can lie yet — a deception system would need the tip stores to hold provenance, and they hold none. **Only two kinds of thing can be told**, a market and a site, because those are the only two stores a tip can land in; "this system is dangerous" and faction gossip have nowhere to go and were named out of scope rather than printed as text pretending to be information. **Who you have hailed does not persist** across a jump or a save, so re-entering a system re-arms every pilot in it — a consequence of spawned ships not being serialised, and the same trade 8l made for its assist window. **The tip selection is deterministic**, so two traders met in the same place name the same market unless one of them has already been taken; the seeded roll reaches the hook's *wording* but never the fact. **`kTipLabel` is set and nothing reads it but `sol.tips()`** — the map draws a rumour with the same glyph as a bookmark the player dropped, so the category tier is recorded and not yet shown. **A rumour bookmark is named `"Rumour"` for every site**, so two in one system are distinguished only by position. **Ambient traders are coarse-sim and never enter the bubble**, so in practice the only pilots in hail range are patrols, raiders, and whatever the console spawns — the trader branch of `pilot_hail` is the least likely to fire in a real session, which is a Phase 7 caveat surfacing rather than a new one. And **`kHailRange` is shared with docking by construction** so the player learns one number, which means it cannot be tuned for conversation separately from clearance.

#### Phase 8t — Audio ✅ (completed 2026-08-18)

**From the roadmap, not from a playtest** — the backlog was empty and the user chose to build rather than play. It closes **§5 standing question 5** (the audio decoder dependency), open since the plan was first written, and it is the **first change to the AGENTS.md §5 dependency table since the project began**: the user approved a vendored ogg decoder, recorded in `docs/decisions/009-audio-decoder.md`.

**Diagnosis (measured from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **§2.10 promised a stubbed interface "so gameplay code can post sound events into a void". It was never built.** There are zero occurrences of `audio` in `game/src`, and **zero occurrences of `audio` or `sound` in `docs/gdd.md`** — so there is neither an interface to fill in nor a design to implement. This item writes both, and the design half is why three questions were settled with the user before a line of it.
- **The layering is already determined by the CMake graph and is not an open question.** `sol::assets` links `sol::platform` links `sol::core`; `sol::renderer` links `sol::rhi` + `sol::assets`. A new **`sol::audio` linking `sol::assets` is the same shape as the renderer** — cooked data in, a device out — and needs no new rule. **The WASAPI backend needs no architectural exception either**: AGENTS §4 already puts platform-specific code in the platform layer, and the two sanctioned exceptions it lists (`engine/rhi/src/win32`, `engine/ui/src/win32`) exist precisely because those two are *not* in it. Audio is the ordinary case.
- **The cooker takes a new source type in four lines.** `tools/cooker/src/main.cpp:187-216` is a flat extension dispatch over one source directory — `.png → .stex`, `.gltf → .smesh`, `.font → .sfont` — and mtime staleness (`isUpToDate`) comes free with it.
- **Every source asset in this repo is generated.** `tools/scripts/gen_assets.ps1` (27 KB) writes all twelve files under `assets/`; there are no hand-authored assets and nothing over 1 MB. **Audio content has to come from somewhere, and this is where the other twelve came from.**
- **`CombatEffects` is the shape the game-side emitter should copy, and its call sites are already the trigger points.** `combat_effects.hpp:15-47` is cosmetic-only, absent from the save, and `clear()`s on a system change; it is spawned from exactly four places in `space_world.cpp` — `4361` and `4373` (mining cuts), `4489` (a hit), `4506` (a destruction). A sound belongs at all four, beside the particles that already fire there.
- **⚑ The listener already exists and was built to be shared.** `ViewFrame` (`space_world.hpp:317-330`) carries camera position, orientation, virtual screen size and a `valid` flag, pushed once per frame by the frame loop and **held rather than passed** — its own comment says why: the click-pick and the console pick must answer against *the same view* "or the verification is testing a different game than the one on screen". A 3D mix is a third consumer of that argument, so positional audio inherits a solved problem rather than re-deriving a camera.
- **`Settings` is four scalars plus the binding table** (`menu_screens.hpp:39-44`), persisted beside the save so a settings change never risks the run; a volume is one more scalar, and 8k's rule that **a missing key means "use the default"** already covers an upgrade from a pre-8t file.
- **The def system and the binding table each take a new entry by pattern** — `data_defs.hpp`'s strict-schema types, and `content.cpp:2060+` where every `sol.*` function registers.
- **What does not exist is exactly five things**: a device, a mixer, a sound asset format, any sound content, and a way to ask for a sound. That is the whole item.

**A. The dependency, and why it lands in the cooker rather than the runtime.** stb_vorbis-class ogg decoding is **approved** and vendored under `third_party/stb_vorbis/`, on the terms `third_party/CMakeLists.txt` already states for Lua and ImGui: never linked against `sol_options`, default warnings, never modified in place. **It links to the cooker only.** The scope chosen for this item is one-shot cues, and a one-shot does not need streaming — so `.ogg` is decoded at cook time and `.saud` carries PCM, which keeps the shipping binary decoder-free and makes the approval buy the thing it is actually for: **the freedom to drop a real recorded `.ogg` into `assets/sounds/` instead of being locked to what a PowerShell synthesiser can produce.** The `.saud` header carries a `codec` field from day one so that moving the decoder into the runtime for streamed music is additive and needs no second approval.

**B. `.saud`, the fourth cooked format.** `SoundFileHeader` joins `formats.hpp` beside mesh/texture/font — magic `SAUD`, `kFormatVersion 1`, channel count, sample rate, frame count, `codec` (PCM16 for now), then interleaved frames. `assets::loadSound` joins `loadMesh`/`loadTexture` in `asset_loader.hpp`, synchronous like both of them. The cooker imports **`.wav`** (an in-repo RIFF PCM reader, the same in-repo-importer rule that gave us the PNG and glTF readers) and **`.ogg`** (through the vendored decoder), both to the same output. **The authored sample rate is kept rather than normalised at cook time**: pitch jitter (§E) already forces the mixer to step through a source at a fractional rate, so device-rate conversion is the *same* mechanism and not a second one — normalising at cook time would buy nothing and lose the source. **A 3D voice requires a mono source**; a stereo asset plays 2D, and a positional def pointing at a stereo asset is a load error, per the strict-schema rule the defs already follow.

**C. The device: `sol/platform/audio.hpp`, WASAPI behind it.** Open/close, a queried output format, an underrun counter, and a render callback wanting N frames of interleaved float stereo. `engine/platform/src/win32/audio_win32.cpp` runs shared mode, event-driven, on **its own thread** — the game is vsync-bound with ~5.7 ms of idle per frame (8n/8o), so it *could* mix on the frame loop, but a mix that depends on frame pacing turns any hitch into an audible click, and the buffer must be fed whether or not the game is drawing. **No output device must degrade to silence, not to a failure** — a machine with no sound card still boots the game.

**D. `sol::audio`: a bank, a voice pool, and a listener.** New `engine/audio` module. A fixed voice pool with **stealing in which the quietest voice loses, oldest breaking the tie** (a bound on voices is what stops a fireball with forty fragments from swallowing the mix) — the spec first said *age then gain*, and that is backwards: the loudest voices are the near ones, so ageing out a close explosion to keep a distant one is exactly the wrong trade. **An incoming sound quieter than everything already playing is dropped rather than admitted.** Then one-shot playback, and 3D positioning: **distance attenuation plus a stereo pan law, taken against the `ViewFrame` listener.** The game thread never touches a voice — it pushes immutable play requests into a single-producer ring the mixer drains, and publishes the listener as an atomic snapshot. **⚑ A one-shot is positioned where it was fired and does not track its emitter.** That is the simplification the chosen scope buys: without it, every voice needs an entity lifetime, and emitters outliving their entity is where audio systems get genuinely hard. **Exactly one looping voice exists** — the player's own engine, non-positional because it is always at the listener, its gain driven by throttle. Generalising loops to arbitrary entities is out of scope and named below. New **`audio.unit`** suite, the **9th**, asserting the attenuation curve, the pan law, voice stealing under pressure and mix correctness — all headless, because a rule verified only by ear is a rule that will be re-verified by ear forever.

**E. `[[sound]]` defs, so a cue is data.** A new strict-schema type in `DefDatabase` — `id`, cooked asset name, gain, pitch jitter, max concurrent instances, rolloff distance — following `[[weapon]]`/`[[commodity]]` exactly, so it hot-reloads and mod-layers like every other def and a cue can be retuned without a rebuild. Base-game `game/data/sounds.toml` names every cue.

**F. The game side, and `sol.play_sound`.** A `GameAudio` emitter shaped like `CombatEffects` — cosmetic, absent from the save, cleared on a system change — fed the listener from the existing `ViewFrame`. Cues at the four combat sites above, plus weapon fire, docking and clearance, UI clicks, and the alarm states the HUD already computes. **`sol.play_sound(id [, x, y, z])` registers beside the other bindings**, which is what lets `campaign.lua` and 8s's `pilot_hail` be *heard* rather than only read — the payoff for choosing data-driven addressing over hardcoded call sites. Console probes: `sol.sounds()` lists the loaded bank, `sol.audio()` reports device state, active voices, steals and underruns.

**G. Settings, and the content.** `masterVolume` and `effectsVolume` on `Settings`, persisted in `settings.toml` and driven by sliders on the existing Settings screen. Content is synthesised by `gen_assets.ps1` into `assets/sounds/` — noise, envelopes and filters for weapons, impacts, explosions, thrusters, docking, UI and alarms — **so the item ships audible rather than as a silent engine waiting on assets**, and any one cue can later be replaced by a recorded `.ogg` file-for-file. The generator must leave the existing twelve assets **byte-identical**, the check 8m established.

**Save v10, unchanged.** Nothing about audio belongs in the save: the bank is derived from defs, voices are transient, and volume is a setting, which is deliberately not save state. `settings.toml` gains two keys, additive under 8k's missing-key rule.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **Music and streaming** — the decoder sits in the cooker for exactly this reason, and adding a streamed track later is additive. **Looping world emitters** (station hum, field noise, other ships' engines) and anything else needing per-entity voice lifetime; the player's own engine loop is the single exception and is hardcoded as one voice. **Reverb, occlusion, filtering, doppler, HRTF or any DSP beyond gain and pan.** **Surround output** — stereo only. **Voiced dialogue.** **A separate music volume**, which would be a slider governing nothing. **Audio hot-reload of the cooked `.saud` payload** (defs hot-reload; the samples do not). **Runtime device change** — unplugging headphones mid-run is not handled beyond degrading to silence. **Positional audio for the player's own weapons**, which are at the listener by construction.

*(Verified live 2026-08-18, seed 1701, 1280×720 @ UI scale 1.0, across one standalone device probe and four drives — a bank/probe drive, a combat drive, a settings-layout drive and a persistence drive. 24 files, **9 test suites** (`audio.unit` is new at 15 tests; cooker 13 → **19**, assets 33 → **35**), save unchanged at **v10**, `settings.toml` +2 keys.*

***The device was proved before anything was built on it.*** *A throwaway probe linked against `sol_platform` alone opened the real endpoint and rendered a 440 Hz tone: `WASAPI shared, 48000 Hz, 2 channels, 1056 frame buffer (22.0 ms)`, **152 callbacks producing 1.520 s of audio in 1.5 s of wall clock**, zero underruns, clean close. The 0.02 s of overshoot is the priming buffer plus the last partial callback, which is the instrument agreeing with itself. Doing this first is what kept the mixer's bring-up from being a two-variable debug.*

***The played counter is the instrument, because audio cannot be screenshotted.*** *Cold boot logs `audio: 9 cue(s) loaded`; `sol.sounds()` lists all nine with no `(missing)` marker. `sol.play_sound("sol.explosion")` walked `before=1` → `play=true` → `after=2`, and the negative control answered `play_sound: no cue 'sol.nope'` / `bogus=false` — which is what separates "the counter moved" from "play_sound always returns true". **That `before=1` is itself evidence**: one cue had already fired, the UI click from pressing New Game.*

***A firefight walks it and nothing complains.*** *Spawning an inert `sol.shuttle` and shooting it apart took the counter **1 → 49 (three bursts) → 91 (destroyed)**, with `'sol.shuttle' destroyed` in the log and **`0s 0d 0u` — zero stolen, zero dropped, zero underruns — throughout**. Docking then added **exactly one** cue (91 → 92): the chime. A jump to Saria left the voice count back at 1, so `stopAll` tore the old system's voices down and the engine loop re-armed rather than leaking or staying dead.*

***The volume sliders close the whole loop.*** *Clicking a quarter and three-quarters along the two tracks wrote **`master_volume = 0.248` / `effects_volume = 0.748`** on leaving the screen; a second boot drew them back as **0.25 / 0.75**, and `sol.audio()` reported **`vol 0.25/0.75`** — which is what proves the value reached the *audio system* and not merely the settings struct. Both quit paths exited **0**, so nothing in the frame complained.*

***The ogg path works and is the one thing with no committed regression test.*** *A real 105 KB stereo Vorbis file cooked to **269,188 frames at 44,100 Hz**, with the `.saud` size reconciling exactly against its own header (24 + frames × channels × 2 = 1,076,776 bytes). That file is deliberately not committed — see the gaps.)*

**What building it changed about the plan.** Three corrections, and all three came from writing the thing rather than from running it.

**The spec contradicted itself about sample rates.** It said cook-time resampling to one engine rate "so the mixer never resamples per voice", and it also gave every cue pitch jitter — which makes the mixer step through a source at a fractional rate no matter what. Normalising at cook time would have destroyed the source and bought nothing, so the authored rate is stored and one linear-interpolating step serves both device-rate conversion and pitch. A test pins it: 96 frames at half the device rate stretch to exactly 12 blocks, pitch 2.0 gives 6, pitch 0.5 gives 24.

**Voice stealing by "age then gain" is backwards.** The loudest voices are the near ones, so ageing out a close explosion to keep a distant one is the wrong trade. Quietest-loses with oldest breaking the tie is what shipped, and an incoming sound quieter than everything already playing is dropped rather than admitted.

**⚑ The Settings panel's height was hardcoded for four rows, and this item adds two.** `buildSettingsScreen` sizes its panel as `kTitleHeight + rowHeight * 4 + kButtonHeight * 2 + spacing * 7`; the two volume sliders would have pushed *Controls…* and *Back* out through the bottom of the panel it is drawn in. **This was caught by reading the layout before writing the drive, not by the drive** — which is the 8q/8r lesson arriving a third time from the other direction: read the code first, and it tells you what your change breaks. A comment now names the two numbers that must move with a row.

**Known gaps.** **Nobody has heard this but me, and the cues are synthesised** — noise, envelopes and swept sines from `gen_assets.ps1`, which read as retro rather than cinematic; any one of them can be replaced file-for-file by a recorded `.ogg`, and that freedom is the whole point of the approved decoder. **The ogg path has negative unit coverage only**: there is no encoder in this repo or on this machine, so its positive case rests on a one-off cook of a file that is deliberately not committed. **The no-device degrade path is written and logged but never exercised**, because this machine has an endpoint — the same shape as 8o's `timestampValidBits == 0` gap. **The engine loop's throttle response has no headless assertion**: the voice count is 1 whether the throttle is 0 or 1, so it is verified by construction (it reads the same `m_appliedInput` the thruster plumes do) and by ear. **No music, no streaming, no looping world emitters, no reverb/occlusion/doppler/HRTF, stereo only** — all named out of scope above, with the `codec` field as the way back in. **The cooked payload does not hot-reload**, only the tuning. **Voices are a flat 32 with no per-category budget**, so a large enough fight could in principle crowd out a UI click — quietest-loses makes it unlikely rather than impossible. **`activationsThisFrame` collapses every widget press in a frame to one cue**, which is right for a click and would be wrong for anything that could legitimately fire twice. **The roster is nine cues**: undocking, jumping, scanning, mission events and the whole comms channel 8r/8s built are still silent. **A one-shot does not track its emitter** (deliberate), and **a device unplugged mid-run is not handled** beyond falling silent.

**Exit**: the game makes sound on a cold boot with no settings file; firing, taking a hit, destroying a ship, cutting rock, docking, and clicking a UI button are each audibly distinct; a ship destroyed off to one side is quieter and panned to that side, and a kill at 100 m is louder than the same kill at 5 km; the engine loop follows the throttle; both volume sliders move what they claim to and survive a restart; `sol.play_sound` fires a cue from Lua and `sol.audio()` reports voices, steals and zero underruns across a sustained fight; a machine or a run with no output device boots and plays silently rather than failing; `sounds.toml` retunes a cue's gain on a script reload without a rebuild; the existing twelve generated assets regenerate byte-identically; new `audio.unit` coverage for attenuation, pan, stealing and mixing; save unchanged at v10; all **9** suites green, zero warnings. **The last criterion needs the user's ears** — the same caveat Phase 4 carried for flight feel and Phase 6 for the dogfight, and audio is the first thing this project has built that **a screenshot cannot verify at all**.

#### Phase 8u — War & Territory: Contested Systems and Borders That Move ✅ (completed 2026-08-18)

**From the roadmap, not the backlog** — the playtest board was empty and the user chose this over a play session. It pays a debt named twice: Phase 8b scoped itself as **"dynamic relations on fixed borders … territory never changes hands (border shifts join a later war/conquest item)"**, and §4's sketch has carried "territory/border shifts deliberately deferred to a later war item" since. This is that item.

Scope decided with the user, from four questions asked against what the code makes cheap: **a contested state that resolves into a flip** (not an instant tip, not a second decision verb); **player agency through missions** (a new objective kind, not kills alone and not a pure background sim); **announced on comms and the map**; and **nothing forced on a player standing in a system that changes hands** — the consequences that already exist simply start applying.

**Diagnosis (measured from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- ⚑ **The roadmap's own cost estimate for this item is wrong, and by about 4× in the cheap direction.** It said ownership meant "a save version bump plus **~46 read sites** (32 in `space_world.cpp`, 14 in `content.cpp`)". Those counts are of the *string* `factionIndex`, and it names two unrelated things: **`ShipPilot::factionIndex`** (`space_world.hpp:147`), which is a *ship's* allegiance, and a Lua binding **parameter** of the same name. In `content.cpp`, **zero** of the 14 are ownership reads. This is the third consecutive item whose written estimate was refuted by five minutes of reading — after 8q's regenerated marker source and 8r's "~30 `isDocked()` call sites" — and the rule those two wrote down is now three for three.
- **System ownership is read through one accessor.** `SpaceWorld::systemOwnerFaction(systemIndex)` (`space_world.hpp:576-581`) has **11 call sites** — 7 in `space_world.cpp`, 3 in `content.cpp`, 1 in `map_ui.cpp` — plus **one direct bypass** at `space_world.cpp:1963` and **two reads** inside `FactionSim::raidCandidates` (`faction_sim.cpp:115`, `:134`). **Make the accessor answer dynamically and all eleven sites are correct without being touched.**
- **`FactionSim` already stores per-system state of exactly the shape this needs.** `m_raidIntensity` and `m_lastRaider` are `m_systemCount`-sized vectors, initialised from the galaxy (`faction_sim.cpp:41-42`), decayed in `step()`, and written and read whole by `save`/`load` (`:284-345`). Ownership is a third vector of that shape, in the sim that already holds the war machinery. **`Galaxy` stays `const`.**
- ⚑ **The war is already flyable and it already leads nowhere.** `spawnAmbientPilots`' raid-incursion branch (`space_world.cpp:1975-1985`) spawns the last raider's wing in-system whenever `raidIntensity >= 0.5` and the raider is not the owner, scaled by intensity and capped at 3. **A player can already fly into a raid in progress, kill the raiders, and change nothing whatsoever.** This item is largely about giving that fight a consequence.
- **`commitRaid` already does everything except move the map** (`faction_sim.cpp:166-187`): it drains the victim's markets through `Economy::raidSystem`, pushes the pair's relations down by `raidRelationHit`, refreshes the war flag through the same hysteresis, bumps `m_raidIntensity[target]` and records `m_lastRaider[target]`. A contest is that pressure made persistent and directional.
- **A flip is *felt* for free, because ownership already drives six live consequences**: station catalog gating (`stationSells`, `:385`), standing credit for survey sales (`:1155`), refining (`:1789`) and trade (`:2713`, `:2731`), docking rights against a hostile owner (`:2267`), clearance revocation (`:2635`), mission-board posting (`content.cpp:2764`), the dock-request dispatcher (`content.cpp:2601`), and the galaxy map's owner colour (`map_ui.cpp:357`). **No new consequence has to be invented for a border to matter.**
- **The map already treats ownership as knowledge.** `map_ui.cpp:355-360` sets `hasOwner`/`ownerColor` only when the system is `visited`, under a comment saying a system you have only heard of from a gate does not tell you whose space it is. **The fog rule for a moving border is therefore already written** and this item must not undermine it: a flip in a system you have never visited is not news you are entitled to.
- **`bountyCandidates` is the precedent for a contest candidate** (`missions.hpp:86-92`, `:141-143`): a per-system sim reading — raid intensity, paired with the clan that caused it — enumerated in index order and handed to Lua as the raw material for a contract. A contest is the same kind of reading with a stronger claim attached.
- **The comms channel is general by construction and no *system* has ever spoken on it.** `say(from, text)` pushes onto a 3-line ring at 8 s per line (`space_world.cpp:2319`, `kCommsLines = 3` at `space_world.hpp:457`); 8r built it deliberately general and 8s used it, but **every line the player has ever seen was a reply to something the player did**. A border moving is the first thing in this game with a reason to speak unprompted.
- **Capitals are not stored.** `generateGalaxy` picks them locally (`universe.cpp:252-256`) and `Galaxy` keeps only `systems`, `links` and `clans`; `ClanSpec::homeSystem` is documented as "lowest-index member system". So a faction's home is **derivable** from the founding claim rather than needing a new `Galaxy` field.
- **What does not exist is four things**: dynamic ownership, a contest, a way for a mission to be *about* one, and any announcement. That is the whole item.

**A. `SystemSpec::factionIndex` becomes the founding claim; `FactionSim` holds who has it now.** The generated plan stays the plan — it is regenerated from the seed on load (`space_world.cpp:4808-4812`) and must not be mutated, or the galaxy stops being a pure function of its seed. `FactionSim` gains `m_systemOwner`, initialised from `galaxy.systems[s].factionIndex`, and:

- `[[nodiscard]] std::uint32_t systemOwner(std::uint32_t system) const;`
- `SpaceWorld::systemOwnerFaction` delegates to it, so the eleven call sites are correct by delegation;
- `space_world.cpp:1963` stops reading `spec.factionIndex` and asks the accessor — **the one bypass, and it is the presence spawn, i.e. exactly the site where reading the stale value would put the wrong navy in the sky**;
- `raidCandidates`' two reads become `m_systemOwner[s]`, so a faction can raid onward from ground it has taken and can be raided on ground it has lost. **The reach BFS seeds from held territory, so conquest moves the front by construction.**

**B. The contest, and what moves it.** Two more per-system vectors beside the ownership one — `m_contestAttacker` (`kNoFaction` when quiet) and `m_contestPressure` (0..1):

- **A raid opens or feeds a contest.** `commitRaid` already knows the attacker, the victim and that they are at war; it adds `contestPerRaid` to pressure. A raid by a *different* faction against a system already contested by someone else does **not** hijack it — the standing attacker keeps the claim unless the newcomer's raid arrives while pressure has decayed below `contestFloor`, at which point the contest is quiet and open. One attacker per system, because two would need a three-way resolution rule for a case a 60 s decision cadence makes rare.
- **Pressure decays** on the same exponential `step()` already runs for raid intensity, with its own half-life. **A siege nobody sustains lapses**, which is the defender winning by attrition and needs no separate code path.
- ⚑ **The player pushes back by killing the attacker's ships in the contested system, and only the player's kills count.** `space_world.cpp:4630-4650` already reports every kill with the victim's faction, the current system, and 8l's assist window; one call beside `recordShipKill` is the whole hook. **NPC-versus-NPC kills deliberately do not count**: nothing in this game simulates a war's attrition, so crediting ambient dogfights would be inventing state the sim does not have — the same ruling 8r made about berth occupancy. The honest claim is "**you** turned this back", not "the front line is simulated".
- **Above `contestThreshold` the system is publicly contested** — that is the state the map, the missions and the comms lines see. Below it the pressure is just weather.
- **At 1.0 it flips**, and the contest clears with the new owner's claim at rest.

**C. What can never flip, and why the game would break otherwise.** A faction's **home system — the lowest-index system holding its founding claim**, the rule `ClanSpec::homeSystem` already states — is not contestable. Without it a faction can be erased from the map, and a faction with no territory is not a cosmetic loss: `content.cpp:2764` returns early with no board for an ownerless station, catalogs gate on the owner's def id, `raidCandidates`' multi-source BFS degenerates to empty, and every standing the player has built with that faction becomes unspendable. **A faction that cannot be killed can still be beaten back to one system**, which is all the drama this needs and none of the collapse.

**D. Presence: the war you can fly into.** The incursion branch already spawns the attacker's wing off raid intensity. It gains a contested form: while a system is contested, the attacker keeps a wing there sized off **pressure** rather than intensity, and the owner's patrol wing is reinforced rather than replaced. **This is the difference between a number on a map and a thing the player meets** — and it is the same `spawnWing` lambda with different arguments, not new spawning machinery.

**E. `ObjectiveKind::Hold`, the fifth member, and the contract that uses it.** The user chose missions as the agency surface, so the contest gets a mission shape rather than only a meter:

- **`ContestCandidate { system, attacker, owner, pressure, jumps }`** beside `HaulCandidate` and `BountyCandidate`, enumerated by `MissionSim::contestCandidates(galaxy, factions, fromSystem, out)` in index order within `candidateReach`. A candidate exists when the system is contested **and the board's owner is a party to it** — a station owned by O will not pay you to help A, and one owned by A within reach will.
- **`ObjectiveKind::Hold`**: *system S is held by faction F when its contest resolves*. **One member covers both directions** — defence is `F = owner`, an assault contract is `F = attacker` — and it needs exactly one new report, `MissionSim::notifyContestResolved(system, winner)`, called where the sim already drains faction events. It is checked, not polled, so it costs nothing per tick.
- ⚑ **A `Hold` mission that loses its contest does not charge the standing penalty.** Failure by expiry and failure by abandonment stay as they are; losing a battle you flew is a third thing, and the poster lost more than the player did. This is the one place 8l's recorded rough edge — *"expiry's standing penalty applies even when the contract became impossible through no fault of the player"* — has an obvious right answer, so it gets it here rather than being recorded a second time.
- `postOffer`'s procedural validation grows to accept a single `Hold` objective matching a current contest candidate, which is the rule it already applies to `Deliver` and `Kill`.
- `mission_board` gains a `contests` argument and `init.lua` composes the offer, exactly as it does for hauls and bounties. **A defence contract gets a deadline** — 8l's lesson that `deadline = 0` means immortal, and that an unfinishable contract holds one of four active slots forever.

**F. What the player is told.** Three surfaces, all of them existing:

- **Comms, when it is happening to you.** A flip in the system the player is standing in speaks on the channel 8r built: `say("Fleetcom", "Kestrel Combine forces hold this system.")`. **The sender is a short callsign** — 8s established that the sender column is 116 px, that a faction name overruns it, and that the panel prints through itself when it does. Contest *opening* speaks too, once, and is throttled the way 8r's refusal is; pressure crossing is not narrated tick by tick.
- **The galaxy map, for everywhere else** — `hasOwner`/`ownerColor` already recolour, **still gated behind `visited`**, and a contested system is marked as contested rather than silently recoloured, so the player can see a front rather than only its outcome.
- **The station Factions tab**, which already lists standings, wars and recent raids, gains recent territory changes. That is where a player who has been away catches up.

**G. Standing in a system that changes hands: nothing is forced.** The catalog changes under the player because `stationSells` is evaluated live while docked; the patrols that spawn become the new owner's; re-docking is refused if the new owner is hostile (`:2267`) and an outstanding clearance is revoked by the rule 8r already wrote (`:2635`), because that rule reads standing rather than a docking event. **The player is never ejected**, and the dock stays the safe room — `space_world.cpp:2267` says so explicitly about the death respawn, and this item is not the one to break it. The hazard is real, it is legible, and the player chooses when to face it.

**Save v11.** The faction block gains the three per-system vectors — owner, contest attacker, contest pressure — written and validated with the `m_systemCount` check the block already performs. The mission block gains the `Hold` objective's fields, which are the ones `MissionObjective` already carries (`system`, `faction`). Old saves are **rejected cleanly, not migrated**, per the v4–v10 policy.

**Console.** `sol.territory()` (every system whose owner differs from its founding claim, plus every live contest with attacker and pressure), `sol.contest(system)` (one system's contest in full), `sol.set_contest(system, attacker, pressure)` (the dev lever — **the exit criteria are stated against it**, because a real contest takes many 60 s decision cycles), `sol.flip(system, faction)` (force a resolution), and `sol.contest_candidates()` beside the existing `sol.mission_candidates()`.

**Tests.** `sim.unit` gains coverage for: ownership initialising from the founding claim and surviving save/load; pressure rising on a raid, decaying to a lapse, and resolving at 1.0; a home system refusing to be contested; `raidCandidates` following a border after a flip (the reach BFS re-seeding from taken ground); one attacker at a time; a `Hold` objective completing on a resolution in its faction's favour, failing on the other, and **charging no penalty when it does**; and `contestCandidates` enumerating only contests the board's owner is party to.

**Exit**: a contest driven to a flip changes the map colour, the station's catalog, who spawns in the sky and who will let the player dock, with the comms line arriving on the flip and the Factions tab listing it afterwards; a lapsed contest leaves ownership untouched; the player's kills in a contested system measurably push pressure down and NPC kills do not; a defence contract offered from the contested system's own board completes on a successful defence, fails without a standing penalty on a lost one, and expires normally on its deadline; a faction beaten back to its home system stops losing ground; ownership, contests and the `Hold` objective survive a save/load round trip at v11 and old saves are rejected cleanly; all 9 suites green, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** No fleet battles or simulated NPC attrition — the contest is a meter the player and the raid cadence move, not a war game. No faction elimination (§C). No player-owned territory, no claiming a system for yourself, and no third-party or multi-attacker contests. No border *lanes* — ownership is per system, and the gate graph does not change. No diplomacy verbs (no alliances, treaties or player-brokered peace); relations still move only through the events 8b defined. No station capture or destruction — a station changes owner with its system and is never removed, which is what keeps 8q's known gap about remote content being live rather than remembered from becoming visible. No war UI beyond the three surfaces in §F, and **no change to the campaign** — `ObjectiveKind::Hold` is available to authored missions but Act 2 is a separate item.

**What driving it changed about the plan.** Six drives, five things the spec did not see — and **four of the five were only visible in a screenshot**, which is 8s's rule arriving a second time:

- **⚑ The comms line did not fit its own panel, and the log said nothing about it.** *"Solar Navy forces are pressing a claim on this system."* rendered as *"…on this syste"* — clipped at the cell edge. The clip itself is correct (8s taught the cell to `pushClip` rather than print through), so the panel is *behaving*; the sentence was simply written to a budget it cannot grow, because 8r clamps that panel against the target panel. All three lines are now short enough for a ~17-character faction name, and the comment in `space_world.cpp` says so. **The console printed the full sentence happily throughout.**
- **⚑ `holds N system(s)` was appended after the faction's war list, which already overruns its column.** A major at seed 1701 is at war with all ten pirate clans, so the new fact was drawn entirely past the right edge. Moved to the front of the detail string: **short fixed-length facts first, unbounded lists last.** The same reasoning 8s applied to a sender column, applied to a detail column.
- **⚑ `init.lua` rep-gated the only war contract a new pilot will ever see.** `min_rep(5)` on a contest in the board's own system looked like the haul tier rule, but there is **at most one war contract on a board**, and standings start at 0 — so a local contest offered a contract nobody could accept, and the drive's first accept answered *"reputation too low"*. Gated on **pressure > 0.8** instead: the last stand goes to someone known, the ordinary front is open. A tier rule for one item is a lockout.
- **⚑ `setContest(system, kNoFaction, …)` wiped a live contest without queueing a resolution**, so a `Hold` objective on that system waited forever for an answer that could not come. Decay to the floor — the natural way a contest ends that way — *does* queue one, so the dev lever was producing a state the running game cannot. It now resolves as a lapse, with a `sim.unit` test pinning it. **A dev lever that can reach a state the sim cannot is not a shortcut, it is a second implementation.**
- **A `Hold` objective printed no destination and described itself as a "kill".** `objectiveDestinationText`'s switch had no `Hold` case and fell through to an empty string; `sol.objective()`'s `kindName` chain ended in `: "kill"`. Neither is a compiler error — the switch has a trailing `return {}` and the chain a fallback — and a contest has no position, so **prose is the only thing a `Hold` objective can have**. Both fixed, and the HUD and journal now carry the pressure percentage, which is the only progress bar this objective owns.

**Verified live** across six drives (rules, consequences, surfaces, missions, persistence, rejection), every one quitting through the pause menu at **exit code 0**:

- `SET true` on an ordinary system and **`HOMESET false`** on a home system, in the same drive — the positive/negative pair that separates "the home rule works" from "the lever is broken". `FLIP true` then `[territory] Sarorea: Ironstar Hegemony takes the system from Freight Guild`, with `sol.territory()` reporting `3 Sarorea: Ironstar Hegemony (3) (taken from Freight Guild (2))` and the founding claim unmoved.
- **Both comms lines on the glass, in full**: `Freight Guild is pressing a claim here.` on the opening and `Freight Guild holds this system now.` on the flip, sender `Fleetcom` inside its column. The lapse reads `Solar Navy driven off. System holds.`
- **The galaxy map draws a front**: Saria wearing an orange ring in the *attacker's* colour outside the cyan current-system ring, detail `Saria: core, Solar Navy - CONTESTED by Freight Guild`.
- **Both sides in the sky**: `7 contact(s) in Saria` — 2 Freight Guild interceptors (the attacker's wing, sized off pressure) and 5 Solar Navy (the region's 3 patrols plus the 2-ship reinforcement).
- **The mission chain end to end**: the board composed `2: Defend Saria (Solar Navy, 2267 cr, rep 5+, 1200s)` off a live contest candidate; losing it logged **`[missions] lost 'Defend Saria': the system fell (no penalty)`** with the poster's standing unchanged at exactly the `+25.0` the drive set; winning it logged `completed 'Defend Saria': +2134 cr, Freight Guild +8.0 rep` with credits 1000 → 3134.
- **The player's lever, with its own negative control**: killing a Freight Guild shuttle in contested Saria logged `contest in system 8: pressure 0.45 -> 0.40` — exactly `contestPerKill` — while killing a *Solar Navy* shuttle (the holder, not the attacker) in the same system produced **no pressure line at all**.
- **Save v11**: a moved border and a live contest at 0.49 both survived F9/F10, the comms line re-announced after the load (a loaded run has heard nothing yet), the header on disk read `11`, and a header patched back to `10` gave `world load FAILED` with the game still running and exit code 0.
- All **9** suites green, zero warnings; `sim.unit` **109 → 121**.

**Known gaps.** **The contest is a meter, not a battle** — nothing simulates attrition, so the only things that move it are the raid cadence and the player's own kills; an NPC war fought over a system changes nothing, and that is deliberate rather than unfinished (§B). *(⚑ Superseded by **Phase 8x**, which gave the sim ships that can be lost: attrition inside a contested system now presses the claim without the player firing a shot. It only ever **sustains** a claim — opening one names an attacker, and attrition has none — so a raid remains the only thing that starts a war.)* **One attacker per system**, so a three-way border is not representable. **No history**: the Factions tab lists current fronts and borders that differ from their founding claim, because nothing records *when* a system changed hands — a player returning after a long absence sees the state, not the story. **The Factions tab is not fog-gated** — it lists every contest galaxy-wide, following the existing raids block it sits under, while the map *is* gated behind `visited`; the two disagree and the tab is the lenient one. **The galaxy map's detail line clips**, and the `CONTESTED by` clause pushes the station and site counts off the right edge (8i's recorded cell-width gap, now easier to hit). **Ownership never returns to its founding claim by drift** — only by being taken back. **A flipped system's stations keep their names**, so `Lyrioa Alpha` can be held by a faction that did not build it. **`kBerthCount`, catalogs and station archetypes are unchanged by a flip** — the new owner inherits the building. **Nothing spawns to *defend* a contest the player starts winning**, and the attacker's wing does not regenerate within a visit. **The `Hold` objective has no marker** (a contest has no position) and the campaign uses none of this.

#### Phase 8v — Jump Transition: Flying the Lane Between Systems ✅ (completed 2026-08-18)

**From playtest session 6 — and it is not in that session's written report.** Session 6 returned **zero notes**, the first clean playtest the project has had. This item surfaced only when the user was offered the three remaining roadmap items and answered with none of them: *"lets develop a transition between systems when we jump so its not just an instant pop into the next system."* **That is the third time a direct question after a session has beaten the framing before it** — session 5's cockpit sign-off was the first, session 6's audio-as-placeholder ruling the second — and it is the standing argument for always asking one. A session that reports "nothing major stuck out" is not necessarily a session with no findings; it may be one nobody asked.

Scope decided with the user from three questions, each framed against what the code makes cheap. **All three answers took the more expensive option, knowingly**: **the tunnel**, not a fade and not a flash — a traversal the player sits inside; **one named duration constant tuned on evidence**, rather than a skip path designed up front; and **the trigger moves too** — flying *through* the gate replaces pressing `J` within 10 km, which makes this a gameplay item and not only a presentation one. It is the largest item since 8m, and §H sequences it so each half is verifiable before the other exists.

**Diagnosis (measured from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **The pop is literal, and it is one call.** `jumpNearestGate` (`space_world.cpp:2190-2213`) finds the nearest gate inside an activation range and calls `loadSystem` **synchronously, in the same frame**: despawn, swap `m_currentSystem`, reposition the player 500 m off the arrival gate, zero the `FlightBody`, respawn ambient wings. There is no frame in which the player is between systems.
- **⚑ There is no transition machinery of any kind to build on.** `fade` occurs in this codebase only as per-particle alpha (`thruster_particles.cpp:110`, `combat_effects.cpp:70`) and 8r's comms-line alpha (`game_ui.cpp:884`). No screen fade, no timed cinematic state, no camera rig that is not ship-anchored. This is greenfield, which is why §A puts the timing in a pure header instead of the frame loop.
- **⚑ `loadSystem` must not run mid-tick, and the codebase already says so.** `space_world.hpp:1273-1275`: *"Death respawn into another system defers to end-of-tick: loadSystem mid-tick would invalidate the pass scratch (collision slots, pools)."* `m_pendingRespawnSystem` is drained at `space_world.cpp:4607`, after `tickDocking`. **The swap point therefore has a proven pattern already in the file, and §D copies it rather than inventing one.**
- **`loadSystem` has four callers and only one is a player jump** — `initialize` (`:261`), `jumpNearestGate` (`:2211`), `jumpToSystem` (`:2858`, the console lever) and the deferred death respawn (`:4610`). **A fifth entry point does not call it at all**: `loadSave` sets `m_currentSystem` directly and calls `rebuildSystemSideData` (`:5053-5055`). ⚑ **That is 8r's recorded lesson exactly** — *"`loadSave` does not go through `loadSystem`, so transient state reset in the latter is not reset on a load"* — so the transition state must be cleared in **both** places, or a save written mid-tunnel resumes inside one.
- **⚑ Two console levers jump instantly, and 8u's rule says they cannot stay that way.** `sol.jump()` is `jumpNearestGate(1.0e30)` (`content.cpp:285-288`) and `sol.jump_to(name)` is `jumpToSystem` (`:436-439`). 8u's hardest-won lesson was that **a dev lever which reaches a state the sim cannot is a second implementation, not a shortcut** — that defect left a `Hold` objective waiting forever on a resolution the lever never queued. §F rules on both.
- **⚑ Gates are not avoidance obstacles.** `m_obstacles` is rebuilt at `space_world.cpp:2123-2130` from **stations (130 m), planets and the star** — gates are absent. Nothing steers around a gate, so "fly through it" does not fight the avoidance system. **This is the inverse of 8r's constraint**, where a berth sat at 200 m precisely because the station carried a 130 m sphere autopilot dodged, and it is why §B is cheap rather than a second geometry problem.
- **A gate's nav target has `surfaceRadius = 0.0`** (`:2092`), so `autopilotArrivalRange` gives it the general 1.5 km standoff — autopilot flies to 1.5 km short and stops. Under §B that is 1.5 km of manual flying at the end of every autopiloted jump, so the gate needs **the third arrival clamp**, after 8i's mission objective and 8r's berth. The pattern and its reason are written twice already.
- **The gate's frame is 70 m** (`kGateRadiusMeters`, `space_world.hpp:315`), shared with 8j's click-picking. That is a real drawn thing rather than an invented radius, and §B captures against it.
- **⚑ The tunnel is far cheaper than "a new shader path" implied when the question was asked.** `sky.frag` is a single `texture(uSky, direction)` sample scaled by `pc.forward.w`; `SkyRenderer::draw` already takes `intensity` as a parameter that `scene_renderer.cpp:322` feeds a compile-time `kSkyIntensity`; `sky.vert` is a fullscreen triangle at reversed-Z far. **A radial streak is N taps along a direction in the existing fragment shader plus two more push-constant fields — no new pass, no new pipeline, no new descriptor set.** The cost of this item is tuning, not plumbing, and the estimate offered to the user was pessimistic. **That is the fourth consecutive item where reading the code moved the estimate**, after 8q, 8r and 8u — this time in the cheap direction.
- **⚑ A FOV kick is a trap, and it must not be used as the speed cue.** `kCameraVerticalFov` is one value feeding the scene projection, the sky ray setup **and the HUD projection** (`scene_renderer.hpp:25`). 8m's rule is that *the dash is measured in angles and the panels in pixels*, so widening the FOV mid-jump would slide every cockpit anchor and drag the console mounts across the radar disc — the exact collision 8m spent three fixes on. **The speed reads off the streaks; the FOV is left alone** (named again under Out of scope).
- **The sim loop is already gated.** `main.cpp:708` runs the fixed loop only `if (simRunning)`, under a comment that a menu stops the clock so unpausing does not fast-forward the galaxy. **A transition that suspends the sim needs no new mechanism**, only a new reason to be in that state.
- **The HUD prompt is already state-driven.** `hud.gateInRange` (`main.cpp:963`) is `distance <= kGateActivationRange` and `game_ui.cpp:538` gates the `[J] JUMP` chip on it; 8r's dock chip already changes its verb with state (`:524-525`). A chip whose verb follows the approach is a precedent, not a new idea.

**A. The timing lives in a pure header: `engine/sim/jump_transition.hpp`.** The shape `docking.hpp` (8r) and `pilot_tips.hpp` (8s) established, and for their reason: `game/src` has no test suite, so a rule that cannot be asserted headlessly gets verified by screenshot forever. It owns **phase and time only** — no rendering, no entity access:

- `enum class JumpPhase { Idle, Tunnel, Arrive };`
- `begin(destinationSystem)`, `advance(dtSeconds)`, `clear()`;
- `[[nodiscard]] bool swapDue() const` — **true on exactly the one advance that crosses the swap point**, with `noteSwapped()` consuming it, so the caller cannot double-load or miss it;
- `warp()` (0..1 streak strength), `skyScale()` (multiplier on `kSkyIntensity`), `phase()`, `active()`, `destination()`.
- Durations come from a `JumpTransitionParams` struct with **one named constant per phase**, which is the "short now, tune later" answer: `kJumpTunnelSeconds` and `kJumpArriveSeconds` are changed on evidence after flying it, exactly as `kAssistSeconds`, `kHailRange` and the berth numbers were.

**B. The trigger: flying through the gate.** `J`-at-10 km is replaced, not supplemented.

- **Capture is the gate's own 70 m frame**, the radius the game already draws and picks against — no invented number, which is the rule 8r's berths landed on.
- **There is no speed gate.** 8r's berth needed one because a berth is a parking space; a gate is a threshold you cross, and a speed limit on it would punish exactly the approach the feature is trying to make feel good.
- **The `[J] JUMP` chip becomes an approach readout** rather than a key prompt, following 8r's dock chip: outside the frame it names the destination and the distance, and the key legend goes away because there is no longer a key. `hud.gateInRange` keeps its meaning as "close enough to be told about it" and gains a companion for "you are through".
- ⚑ **`Action::Jump` does not get deleted, and this is the one place the item touches settings.** The action is one of 36 rows in `input_actions.cpp` and it is written into every existing `settings.toml` under `[bindings]`; deleting the row shifts nothing (bindings are stored **by name** since 8k, which is precisely the property 8k built for this case) but it would leave `jump = "J"` as an orphan key that `repairDuplicateBindings` has no opinion about. **The row stays and the action becomes unused**, which is honest and reversible, and §H's first stage keeps it live as the fallback trigger while the tunnel is being built.

**C. The tunnel, in the pass that already exists.** `sky.frag` gains a warp: the sampled direction is smeared along the axis of travel, N taps accumulated and normalised, with strength driven by `warp()`. Push constants gain the **travel direction** and the **strength**; `SkyRenderer::draw` gains the two arguments beside the `intensity` it already takes; `scene_renderer.cpp:322` stops passing a compile-time constant and passes what the frame gives it. **`kSkyIntensity` becomes a default rather than the only value.** No new pass, no second pipeline, and `gpu.sky` stays the zone that measures it — which matters because 8o established the sky is the most plausible fill-rate cost in the frame, so the tunnel's cost lands in a zone that is already instrumented and already has a baseline (0.03→0.10 ms across a 7.2× pixel range).

**D. Where the swap happens.** `SpaceWorld` holds the `JumpTransition` and a `m_pendingJumpSystem`, drained **at end of tick beside the death respawn** (`space_world.cpp:4607`) and for that member comment's stated reason. `swapDue()` sets the pending index; the drain calls the existing `loadSystem` unchanged. **The pop still happens — it is simply covered**, at the moment `warp()` is at full and the starfield is unreadable. Nothing about `loadSystem` changes, which keeps the item off the four other entry points.

**E. Control, and what this ride is not.** The transition **suspends the sim** for the same reason a menu does, and advances on real frame time. No NPC can meaningfully act on a ship that is in neither system, and the alternative — a steerable inter-system space — is a different and much larger feature. **The tunnel is a ride, not a piloting segment**, and that is a ruling rather than an omission (named under Out of scope). Flight input is ignored for its duration; the pause menu still works, because a player must always be able to leave.

**F. The five entry points, and which of them animate.** Only the player's own gate crossing plays the tunnel. `initialize`, the death respawn and `loadSave` swap silently, because none of them is a journey the player took. **The two console levers are the interesting case, and 8u's rule decides them**: `sol.jump()` plays the full transition, because it is the lever a drive uses to exercise the real path and a lever that silently skipped it would be a second implementation; `sol.jump_to(name)` stays instant as an explicit **teleport**, and says so in its own output so a drive can never mistake which one it called. The distinction is stated in the binding's comment, not left to be inferred.

**G. Autopilot, and the third arrival clamp.** `autopilotArrivalRange` gains a gate case clamping arrival **inside** the 70 m frame, so "autopilot to the gate" ends with the jump rather than 1.5 km short of it — 8i's clamp for a mission objective and 8r's for a berth, a third time, and for the reason both recorded: the ship must park *inside* the thing it flew to. 8i's compaction rule is expected to cover the disengage-on-arrival case for free, and §H's exit criteria say so rather than assuming it.

**H. Build order, because this is two items in one coat.** Each stage is verifiable before the next exists, which is the answer to it being the largest item since 8m:

1. **The pure header and its tests**, with no game wiring at all — phases, the single-fire swap, the curves. Green in `sim.unit` before anything is drawn.
2. **The transition on the existing trigger.** `J` at 10 km, tunnel plays, swap covered. **At the end of this stage the feature is fully playable and the item could stop here** — which is the hedge against the trigger change proving worse in the hand than on paper.
3. **The trigger change.** Fly-through capture, the chip, the autopilot clamp.
4. **Tuning**, against the constants §A names.

**No save change — still v11.** The transition is transient state, cleared on load in **both** `loadSystem` and `loadSave` per the diagnosis. A save written mid-tunnel is not a case that can arise (the pause menu is reachable but the sim is suspended and the swap is already committed), and clearing on load makes it moot rather than relying on that argument.

**Console.** `sol.jump()` plays it (§F); `sol.jump_to(name)` teleports and says so; **`sol.jump_state()`** prints phase, elapsed, warp and destination, which is what lets a drive assert the sequence without reading a pixel — the lesson 8q, 8r, 8s and 8u all recorded in different words. `sol.gate_distance()` reports the nearest gate's range for the capture assertion.

**Tests.** `sim.unit` gains: phases advancing in order and terminating at `Idle`; **`swapDue()` firing exactly once across a whole transition**, including when a single `advance` steps clean over the swap point (the one case a naive threshold test gets wrong); `clear()` mid-tunnel leaving nothing armed; `warp()` and `skyScale()` bounded to their ranges and returning to rest; and a zero-length parameter set degrading to an immediate swap rather than a division by zero.

**Exit**: flying into a gate plays a visible tunnel and arrives in the destination system with no frame showing the swap; the same jump under autopilot ends inside the gate rather than 1.5 km short; `sol.jump_state()` reports the phases in order and one swap; `sol.jump_to` teleports instantly and labels itself; loading a save never resumes inside a transition; the death respawn and a cold boot are visually unchanged; `gpu.sky` stays within a frame budget that leaves the 6.94 ms vsync target untouched (8o's baseline is the control); all 9 suites green, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No steerable inter-system space** — the tunnel is a ride (§E), and an actual lane you fly is a separate feature with its own content problem. **No FOV kick**, for 8m's angles-vs-pixels reason in the diagnosis. **No skip path** — the user chose "short now, tune later", so if the duration proves annoying the constant moves before any skip machinery is built. **No per-gate or per-faction tunnel variants**, no gate mesh change, and no arrival cinematic beyond the settle. **No change to what a jump costs or requires** — no fuel, no charge time, no interdiction, and nothing that can refuse a jump that would have succeeded before. **The campaign is untouched.**

**What driving it changed about the plan.** Four drives, and the two corrections that mattered were both about a number, both invisible to reading, and both only findable by flying:

- **⚑ The capture radius was set to the one distance the player can never reach, and three drives said so identically before I understood why.** §B chose the gate's own drawn frame, `kGateRadiusMeters = 70`, on the reasoning that it is "a real drawn thing rather than an invented radius". It is also **solid**: a gate is a `ModelId::Cube` (base radius 1.0) at `RenderShape` scale 70, and collision radius is `base * scale.x`, so the gate carries a **70 m collision sphere**; the player's hull is the Ship model at 8 m. **The ship stops at exactly 78 m and cannot be moved closer** — twelve seconds of autopilot, then six seconds of full manual thrust, in three consecutive runs, always 78 m, always 0.0–0.1 m/s. Capturing at the frame meant capturing inside the one volume the ship is excluded from, so **the jump could never fire at all**. Now `kGateCaptureRadius = 110` in the pure header, with the 32 m of daylight asserted by a test rather than claimed by a comment. **This is 8r's berth rule arrived at from the opposite side**: there, capture had to stay outside the sphere the autopilot steers around; here, outside the sphere the hull bounces off. **The diagnosis had even checked `m_obstacles` and correctly found gates absent from it — and then treated "nothing steers around it" as "nothing stops you", which is a different system entirely.** Avoidance and collision are not the same list.
- **The autopilot clamp copied the berth's `* 0.5` without earning it.** A berth is a place to park; a gate is a doorway to cross. `steerTravel` decelerates to a **stop** at whatever range it is handed, so any positive standoff has the ship braking exactly where it should be committing. Aiming at the centre is what makes it cross with speed in hand. **Two constants copied from 8r in one item, and both were wrong for the same reason: the precedent was about parking.**
- **§D and §E of the spec contradicted each other, and only writing it revealed the conflict.** §D said drain the swap "at end of tick beside the death respawn"; §E said the transition suspends the sim. A suspended sim has no tick to drain in. The transition advances from the frame loop, immediately after the fixed-loop block and outside it, where no tick is provably in flight — which satisfies the constraint §D was actually about. **This is 8t's "the spec contradicted itself on sample rates" a second time, and both were caught at implementation rather than by review.**
- **The item introduced a prompt that lies, and fixing it was one line.** The interact row kept advertising `[J] JUMP` for the whole 2.7 s of a transition that refuses every one of its four actions. Before 8v that window did not exist, because pressing jump put you in the next system on the same frame. **A feature that adds a new duration adds a new state for every readout to be wrong in.**
- **`Action::Jump` was REMOVED, against §B, which said the row stays and becomes unused.** An action listed in the Controls screen that does nothing is exactly the lie 8k existed to remove, and `game_ui.cpp:509` already carried the argument in writing about this very chip. The orphan-key worry that motivated §B turned out to be answered already: the loader logs `unknown action 'jump' - ignored` and carries on, under a comment saying a settings file must never be the reason the game cannot be flown. **35 actions now, down from 36 — the first time this project has removed one.**

**Verified live** across four drives (rules, visual, trigger, persistence + cost), every one quitting through the pause menu at **exit code 0**:

- **The console caught the lane mid-flight**: `tunnel t=1.67 warp=0.86 sky=2.71 -> Queis`, which is the header's own curve to two decimals — `(1.67/1.8)² = 0.861` and `1 + 0.861 × 2 = 2.72`. **The instrument validating itself**, the same shape 8q's `600000 km` gate reading took.
- **Screenshot file sizes trace the whole curve without opening one** (8p's trick): 347 KB at rest → 494 → 600 → 682 → **774 at peak** → 633 → 408 settled. Streaks compress worse than point stars, so the PNG grows with the warp.
- **The real trigger, with no key pressed**: select the gate, engage autopilot, and the log reads `Autopilot: flying to 'Gate: Lyrioa' (arrive 0.0 km out)` → `jumping: Queis -> Lyrioa` → `Arrived in 'Lyrioa'`.
- **The binding is gone, with its own negative control**: `sol.bind("scan_pulse","K")` answers `Scan Pulse = K` while `sol.bind("jump","J")` answers `no such action 'jump'`, and `sol.bindings()` has no jump row — which separates "the action was removed" from "bind is broken".
- **⚑ Loading a save DURING a tunnel does not resume inside one**: F9 at rest in Lyrioa, `sol.jump()` toward Queis, F10 mid-tunnel → `after load: idle in Lyrioa`, back in the **saved** system rather than the destination. That is 8r's rule (`loadSave` does not go through `loadSystem`) paying off directly; without the explicit clear the loaded run would have finished a jump nobody started.
- **The cockpit does not move.** Through the whole tunnel the frame and all four HUD panels are where they were, which is the FOV decision holding.
- **Cost**: `gpu.sky` runs **0.04 ms at rest and 0.16 ms through the tunnel** — the 12 taps are flat for any warp above zero — against 8o's 6.94 ms vsync budget, i.e. 2.3% of a frame. Both sides of the frame stay idle and the performance question stays closed.
- All **9** suites green, zero warnings; `sim.unit` **121 → 129**.

**Known gaps.** **Nobody has played it** — the duration (1.8 s + 0.9 s) is my number, not a tuned one, and "short now, tune later" means the next verdict is the user's. **A jump is silent**: 8t's roster is nine cues and gate travel was already among the things it does not cover, so the most cinematic moment in the game makes no sound. **There is no way to abort** — crossing the capture sphere commits you, with no hold-to-cancel and no confirmation, so a gate you merely fly *near* takes you: 110 m is small against a system, but autopilot to a target sitting beyond a gate can now jump you by accident, and there is no direction test. **You never actually pass through the frame** — the gate is solid and capture happens 110 m out, so "fly through the gate" is really "reach the gate"; the visual promises an aperture the physics does not have. **The streaks converge on the ship's NOSE, not its velocity**, so drifting sideways into a gate streaks toward where you are pointed rather than where you are going. **Suspending the sim makes a jump a guaranteed combat escape** for 2.7 s of world time that never elapses — true instantly before, now a deliberate window. **`kGateActivationRange` is a stale name**: at 10 km it no longer activates anything and only decides when the approach readout appears. **The tap count is a compile-time 12** with no quality setting, and the streak is a sky-only effect — ships, rocks and stations do not stretch, so anything in the bubble sits crisp against a smeared background.

#### Phase 8w — Through the Aperture: Gates You Actually Fly Through ✅ (completed 2026-08-18)

**From playtest session 7, and it is feedback on Phase 8v within an hour of 8v shipping** — the fastest loop this project has had. Two of session 7's five notes are about 8v, and both are things five drives did not catch:

> *"i think the player should have to physically fly through the gates to travel like in x4: foundations for example you have to get right up on it that seems to help avoid accidental jumping."*
> *"during the jump animation the gate just sits there in front of the player often times which detracts from the effect."*

Both are hits on 8v's own recorded gaps (*"there is no way to abort … a gate you merely fly near takes you"* and *"you never actually pass through the frame"*), which is 8v's known-gaps list being read back as a bug report. **The timing was explicitly endorsed** — *"the timing seems good, it feels pretty quick"* — so `kJumpTunnelSeconds` and `kJumpArriveSeconds` are now **settled** and this item must not move them.

**Diagnosis (measured from the code as it stands).**

- **⚑ A gate is solid, and that is why 8v's capture radius is 110 m.** The static collision pass (`space_world.cpp:4237-4251`) gives every non-ship, non-projectile, non-ore `RenderShape` a body of `modelBaseRadius(model) * scale.x`; a gate is a `Cube` (base 1.0) at scale 70, so it is a 70 m sphere, and the 8 m hull stops the ship at 78 m. **110 m was not a design choice, it was the smallest number that could fire at all.** The note is asking for the opposite of what the physics allows, so the physics has to change first.
- **⚑ That same loop already knows how to make something non-blocking, and says why in a comment**: projectiles and ore chunks are skipped because *"bolts and loose ore never block anything — you fly through your own ore to collect it."* **A gate is the third thing you fly through**, and it joins that list rather than needing a new mechanism.
- **⚑ A gate already has a facing, and it is derivable rather than stored.** `universe.cpp:393-399` places every gate at `hub + direction * params.gateDistance`, where `direction` is the normalised bearing to the destination system on the galaxy map. So the gate's axis **is** `normalize(gate.position - hub)` — the lane it serves. `GateSpec` needs no new field, and the galaxy stays a pure function of its seed. This is the same move 8u made deriving `ClanSpec::homeSystem` from the founding claim instead of storing capitals.
