FOURTEENTH CONSECUTIVE ITEM THE REPO HAD ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN.** `drawLayerInto`'s `touched` parameter and `plot`'s `touched[index] = 1` have been there since G, for `textureLayerCoverage`. **That is what makes the hit test trustworthy**: a geometric "is the cursor inside this rect" would be a second implementation of the half-open box, the clipping, the file order and the width-2 pen, and the first to drift would select something the picture does not show.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE HONEST LIMIT OF THAT, STATED SO NOBODY OVERSELLS THE TEST: a shared walk cannot catch an error in the pixel RULES.** Assertion (1) checks every pixel of all three textures against the colour its named row would paint, and it caught six of the seven mutations — but breaking `penStart` moves the drawing and the attribution *together*, so it passes. The existing `aWidthTwoPenCoversTheColumnBeforeItsCentre` is what covers that, and it is why that test still earns its place.
- **⚑⚑ THE API's SHAPE WAS DECIDED BY A TEST RATHER THAN BY TASTE, AND THE SIGNAL WAS A CLOCK.** `textureHitTest` began as one pixel per call, which costs a full document evaluation each time — so the exhaustive test asked 65,536 of them and **took `assets.unit` from 0.13 s to over two minutes**. `textureAttribution` returns the whole map from one walk; the tool caches it and refreshes lazily **on the press that needs it**, because a drag does not re-pick. *A per-query cost that is fine for a click is quadratic for a proof.*
- **⚑⚑ THE VIEWPORT's DRAG IDIOM DOES NOT TRANSFER TO AN INTEGER DOCUMENT, AND THIS IS E4c's LESSON IN THE TIME AXIS.** `PointTool` drags on a per-frame `cursorDelta`, correct for a mesh authored in `double`. Here every write is rounded, and **rounding a per-frame delta does not compose to rounding the total** — five frames of 0.4 px round to zero apiece while the hand moved two whole pixels. There is deliberately **no `moveRow(dx, dy)`**: the caller keeps the value and cursor the gesture *started* at and sets an absolute position, which makes the wrong arithmetic **inexpressible** rather than merely discouraged. ⚑ The can-fail input **had to be built**, per E4d — no real mouse produces that path reliably.
- **⚑ DIRECT MANIPULATION IS NOT UNIFORM ACROSS THE VOCABULARY, WHICH THE STAGE's OWN QUESTION NEVER MENTIONED: only 2 of 5 ops have a 2-D handle.** `rects` and `panels` carry `x, y, w, h`; **`lines` carries a bare coordinate**, so a seam drags in one axis and the other is *dropped* rather than written somewhere harmless-looking; `fill` and `checker` have no position at all and report a layer with **no row**, which a caller must be able to tell from "the first rect" or a click on bare hull moves something.
- **⚑⚑ SEVEN MUTATIONS, SEVEN CAUGHT — AND ONE OF THEM TESTED NOTHING ON ITS FIRST FORM.** Attributing a row *after* painting it, colliding the line ordinals, keeping the first writer instead of the last, reporting row 0 for a rowless op, a seam always taking `x`, the panel writer dropping `y`, and a seam reading back the wrong axis. **⚑ The seventh's first form zeroed two variables and therefore did not COMPILE** (warnings are errors), which the harness scored as "caught by the compiler" — it was not: a mutation that does not build exercises no assertion. Re-formed as an axis *swap*, it failed two tests. ⚑ A second gap the mutations found: `settingARowPositionMovesThatRowAndRewritesNoOtherLine` passed just as happily on a writer that moved `x` and dropped `y`, because the row's line differs either way — **it now reads both axes back**.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE DRIVE FOUND THE "SAVE MOVES" TRAP A THIRD TIME, CAUSED BY THE VERY FEATURE BEING ASSERTED.** Stage G recorded that `save` moves with the preview; stage H recorded that `use measured` deletes a warning and jumps everything up 49 px. Here **the scroll-into-view added by this stage scrolls `save` up the panel** the moment a pick lands deep in a long list, so a save click calibrated on `checker.tex` (client 669) missed on `hull.tex` (client 188) and the drive reported an empty diff — which reads as "the drag did nothing" when the screenshot plainly showed `101` in the field. **Read the button off the shot after the edit, never before it.**
- **⚑ TWO SMALLER DRIVE FACTS WORTH THE NEXT SESSION's TIME.** **`PrintWindow` returns a blank white surface for a Vulkan swapchain** — it photographs as *the tool drew nothing* rather than as a capture failure; use `CopyFromScreen` over the window rect with the window topmost. And **reaching `Textures` now needs SIX headers collapsed, not stage G's four**: stage H inserted `Def rows`, and at 1:1 the preview is 56 px taller, so `Levels` must go too. Client `57,69` → `57,92` → `57,115` → `57,138` → `57,161` → `57,184`, then the section's header is at `57,207` and its **first list row 28 px below it** — clicking the header collapses the thing you were trying to use.
- **⚑ WHAT THIS STAGE DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO.** **No resize handle** — dragging a corner to change `w`/`h` needs a *screen-space* handle test that is a different rule from the document-space pick, and move comes first as E1 took one point before it took a set. No colour picked off the preview, no marquee, no multi-row selection, no new op, and **no zoom or pan**. ⚑ **And the six-header climb is now the tool's most obvious rough edge** — the panel is one scrolling column and every stage since C has added a section to it.

**⚑⚑ STAGE J — ONE CLICK AWAY — SPEC'D 2026-08-22 ON `feat/one-click-away`, AND IT IS THE ONE ITEM ON THIS PROGRAMME'S LIST THAT THE *TOOLING* FOUND RATHER THAN A PERSON.** Stage I closed by naming the six-header climb as the tool's most obvious rough edge. Every other open Forge item is a guess about what an author will want, because **no human has ever used stages G, H or I**; this one is not a guess, because the evidence is that my own drive recipes had to encode the workaround. `solproject-drive-script` records *"FOUR headers must be collapsed to reach it"* at G, the same recipe plus one at H, *"the only layout where the whole element panel plus the Report fits above the fold"* at E5, and **six** at I. **A tool whose automation must begin by dismantling its own UI has a layout defect, and the count has gone up at every stage that shipped.**

**Diagnosis (measured, not read — the numbers below come from a scratch probe that logged `ImGui::GetCursorPosY()` at each header and `ImGui::GetScrollMaxY()` once, then was deleted).**

- **⚑⚑ THE PANEL IS 2,901 px OF CONTENT IN AN 860 px WINDOW — 3.4× TALLER THAN THE THING IT LIVES IN, AND `scrollMaxY` IS 2,045.** Nine `CollapsingHeader`s, all `ImGuiTreeNodeFlags_DefaultOpen`, in one column: `Meshes` 257, `Parts` 500, `Points, edges & faces` 173, `Report` 269, `Def rows` 368, `Levels` 148, `Textures` 750, `View` 155, `Scale` 233. **Under 30% of the tool is on screen at any moment.**
- **⚑⚑ "SIX HEADERS" UNDERSTATED IT: `Textures` STARTS AT y 1,763, WHICH IS 903 px BELOW THE FOLD, AND ITS FAR END IS AT 2,513 — NEARLY THREE WINDOW-HEIGHTS DOWN.** The count of headers was the wrong unit; the distance is the fact.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE INVERSION NOBODY HAD NOTICED: THE HARDEST SECTION TO REACH IS THE BIGGEST ONE IN THE TOOL.** `Textures` is **750 px, 26% of the entire panel** — larger than `Parts` (500) and nearly three times `Report`. Stage G added a whole second document editor to a column built for one mesh, and put it seventh of nine. **The texture editor is not a section; it is a peer of everything above it.**
- **⚑⚑ THE CLIMB IS PAID ON EVERY LAUNCH, FOREVER, AND THAT IS A ONE-LINE CAUSE.** `engine/ui/src/imgui_host.cpp:47` sets `ImGui::GetIO().IniFilename = nullptr` with the comment *"no imgui.ini litter"* — so **no header state, window position or size is ever persisted**. Collapsing six headers is not a setup cost an author pays once and forgets; it is the price of admission to the texture editor, every single time the tool starts. ⚑ The setting itself is right (a tool that litters the working directory is worse), so the fix must be in the layout, not in persistence.
- **⚑ AND THE ORDER MATTERS, WHICH IS WHY THE RECIPES KEEP GOING STALE.** Collapsing a header moves every header below it, so the six clicks are position-dependent and must be issued top-down — `solproject-drive-script` records re-measured coordinates at G, H, F, E5 and I, and separately records the **"save moves"** trap **three times**, most recently caused by stage I's own scroll-into-view. *A panel this tall makes every coordinate in every recipe a function of the panel's state.*
- **⚑ THE NINE SECTIONS ARE ALREADY THREE DIFFERENT SUBJECTS, AND THE CODE'S OWN COMMENTS SAY SO.** Six describe the open `.forge` (`Parts` *"sits above the report on purpose: the numbers below are what the edit above just changed"*; `Levels` *"a level is a consequence of the mesh the Report describes"*; `Def rows` *"a ship row is a consequence of the model row above it"*). One describes the open `.tex`. Two describe neither document — they are how you are *looking* at things. **The grouping exists in prose and is expressed nowhere in the layout.**
- **⚑⚑ AND THE HONEST CONSTRAINT ON HOW THIS CAN BE VERIFIED: `forge.unit` CANNOT LINK ImGui.** Stage H recorded this when it put `missingModelRefs` in `mesh_library.cpp` rather than in the widget. **So a pure-layout change has no headless test available by construction** — this is the first Forge stage whose exit criterion cannot be a unit test, and it must be a measured before/after from the running tool instead. Stating that up front so the absence of new tests is read as a property of the change rather than as a gap.

**Decisions.**

1. **A tab bar, and the tab count is decided by arithmetic rather than taste.** Splitting Mesh / Texture / View leaves the mesh tab at 1,789 px and **929 px of scroll** — barely better than today, because `Parts` (500) and `Def rows` (368) are the two largest mesh sections and they end up together. Splitting the mesh half at the authoring/derived seam the comments already draw gives four tabs: **`Mesh`** (`Meshes`, `Parts`, `Points, edges & faces`) 1,004 px, **`Report`** (`Report`, `Def rows`, `Levels`) 859 px, **`Texture`** (`Textures`) 824 px, **`View`** (`View`, `Scale`) 462 px. **Worst-case scroll 2,045 → 144, and the texture editor arrives whole.**
2. **The nine `CollapsingHeader`s stay exactly as they are.** Only the container changes. This keeps the diff reviewable, keeps every section's comment attached to its section, and means a section that is conditional today (`Points` needs `editor.isOpen()`, `Def rows` needs `openIndex >= 0`) stays conditional in the same words.
3. **⚑ No automatic tab switching, ever.** Opening a `.tex` does not jump to the `Texture` tab and a bake does not jump to `Report`. A tool that moves under the hand is worse than one that must be steered, and stage I's *"save moves"* trap is three sightings of exactly that failure. The tab is the author's, not the tool's.
4. **The `Report` tab is 859 px against an 860 px window and that is reported as marginal, not as fitting.** One more `[[model]]` row or a third LOD level puts it over. It is honest to say three of four tabs need no scrolling and the fourth is within a pixel of the line.
5. **⚑ What this stage does NOT do.** No second window, no docking, no resizable panel, no persisted layout (that would mean reversing the `IniFilename` decision, which is a separate call about littering the working tree), and **no change to a single widget inside any section**. It moves nothing, renames nothing and edits no document.

**Exit criterion, and it is a measurement rather than a test.** From a cold launch of `forge.exe`, **the texture editor is reachable in one click with no scrolling and no collapsing**, and the flat preview is fully visible without the wheel — measured off a screenshot the same way stage I measured the preview's 1:1 scale. Re-probed `scrollMaxY` per tab must match the predicted 144 / ~0 / 0 / 0. **All 12 suites stay green and no suite's count changes**, because nothing outside `tools/forge/src/main.cpp` is touched.

**⚑⚑ STAGE J IS COMPLETE, VERIFIED LIVE, 2026-08-22 on `feat/one-click-away`.** `baeed43` spec, `5d63ce0` the tabs. **No suite's count changed** — `assets.unit` 82, `geometry.unit` 144, `ui.unit` 94, `cooker.unit` 30, `forge.unit` 10, `game.unit` 15 — twelve suites green in 89.4 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2. **No save change, no renderer change, no game change, no document touched and not one committed pixel altered.**

- **⚑⚑ THE PREDICTION HELD TO 3 px ON ALL FOUR TABS, AND THE WORST-CASE SCROLL WENT 2,045 → 145.** Predicted 1,004 / 859 / 824 / 462 of content; measured **1,001 / 856 / 821 / 459**, the constant 3 px being the tab bar costing 23 px rather than the 26 budgeted. `scrollMaxY` is **145 / 0 / 0 / 0** against a predicted 144 / ~0 / 0 / 0. **Three of the four tabs need no scrolling at all.**
- **⚑⚑ THE HEADLINE IS THE ONE PICTURE: ONE CLICK ON `Texture` PUTS THE WHOLE EDITOR ON SCREEN.** The list, the **1:1 256 px preview with its orientation marker**, `as cooked (BC1) - click a shape, drag to move it`, `checker.tex 256x256`, the file field, `save`/`undo`, the op list (`0 checker`, `1 rects`), `add op`/`delete`/`up`/`down`, and the selected op's `kind`, `cell 32`, `color_a` and `color_b` — **all of it, with nothing scrolled.** That block previously began 903 px below the fold behind six ordered collapses.
- **⚑ AND A SECOND SURFACE CAME WITH IT FOR FREE: STAGE H's `use measured 1.1584 m` IS NOW ON THE SECOND TAB.** The backlog calls that button *"the single most valuable thing to put in front of the user"*, and reaching it used to mean collapsing four headers. The `Report` tab shows the Report, the `[[model]]` row with its orange radius warning and that button, `in the game`, and the whole `Levels` chain **at once**.
- **⚑ `Report` AT 856 OF 860 IS MARGINAL BY 4 px AND THE SPEC SAID SO BEFORE THE RUN — the screenshot shows its last line (`asked for`) flush against the bottom edge.** One more `[[model]]` row or a third LOD level puts it over. Recorded as a fact about that tab, not as a fit.
- **⚑⚑ THE HONEST COST, AND IT IS A REAL LOSS OF SIMULTANEITY: `Points, edges & faces` AND `Report` CAN NO LONGER BE SEEN TOGETHER.** They are on different tabs now, where before two collapses could put both above the fold — `solproject-drive-script`'s E5 note records exactly that layout. **No capability is gone** (the numbers are one click away and the viewport still shows the mesh live), but an author watching `volume` or `closed` while dragging a point must now click. ⚑ **The obvious answer is a persistent one-line summary above the tab bar**, left unbuilt at first because which numbers belong on it is a design question the stage would have had to guess at — **and then asked for by the user, which is what settled it. Built as J2 below.**

**⚑⚑ J2 — THE SUMMARY LINE, ASKED FOR BY THE USER AND SHIPPED 2026-08-22.** `2e1b240`. One `TextDisabled` between the fps readout and the tab bar: **`320 tri  radius 1.1584 m  vol 2.939 m3  closed`**. Twelve suites green in 89.5 s, no suite's count changed, `+30` lines under `git diff -w` and nothing else touched.

- **⚑ THE FOUR FIELDS WERE PICKED BY ASKING WHAT HAS NO OTHER CHANNEL.** The **viewport** shows the shape, the bounds box and the scale references; the **part list** shows the tree; the **Points panel** shows the selection's own counts. Nothing showed what the mesh now *measures*. `radius` is there rather than `size` because it is the number a `[[model]]` row carries and the one the tool has warned about since stage C. ⚑ **`volume` is there for a measured reason, not for completeness: E5 established that a wall or a split triangle wound backwards leaves a mesh closed, manifold AND border-free — the signed volume is the only one of these four that catches it.**
- **⚑⚑ IT STATES, IT DOES NOT JUDGE, AND THAT IS NOT CAUTION — IT IS THE ONLY CORRECT CHOICE, PROVED BY AN ASSET.** The obvious design is amber for a fault. **Every candidate fault signal is true of `gate_membrane.forge`**, which the tool now reads as **`64 tri  radius 70 m  vol 0 m3  not manifold`**: it is a film, so it is not closed, it has a border loop by construction, it encloses nothing, *and* its centre fan makes it non-manifold. Phase 16's invariants handle this by excluding it **by name**, and *a status line has no business carrying an exclusion list*. So it uses the Report's own plain voice — the Report prints `closed no / border edges 32` with no colour either.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE FIRST VERSION LOST ITS STATUS ON THE BIGGEST MESH, WHICH ONLY A SECOND ASSET REVEALED.** `%.4f` and three-space columns fit `asteroid.forge` with 8 px to spare and truncated `station.forge` to **`1068 tri   radius 102.0000 m   vol 3.783e+05 m3   c`** — clipping the health word off the largest asset in the repo, i.e. hiding the status exactly where a mesh is most likely to have a problem. **`%.5g` keeps the asteroid's `1.1584` (the number Phase 14 and stage H exist for) while writing the station's `102.0000` as `102`**, and `PushTextWrapPos` makes a mesh bigger than anything committed cost a second line rather than silently lose a field. *A status line checked against one asset is a status line checked against nothing.*
- **⚑ ITS COST IS 17 px ON EVERY TAB AND IT SPENDS EXACTLY THE HEADROOM J HAD LEFT.** Measured: Mesh 1,001 → 1,018, Report 856 → 873, Texture 821 → 838, View 459 → 476. **`Report` fitted by 4 px and now scrolls by 17**; `Texture` and `View` still need none. Worst case **145 → 162**, against the 2,045 the stage started from — so the line costs about 0.8% of what the tabs bought.

**⚑⚑ J3 — BORDER EDGES ON THE SUMMARY, ALSO ASKED FOR, AND IT COST A SECOND LINE.** `ef3ceed`. The status block is now two lines: **`320 tri   radius 1.1584 m   vol 2.939 m3`** over **`closed   0 border edges`**. Twelve suites green in 89.9 s, no suite's count changed.

- **⚑⚑ THE COUNT IS NOT REDUNDANT WITH `closed`, AND CHECKING THAT FIRST IS WHAT JUSTIFIES THE SPACE IT COSTS.** The obvious objection is that `closed` already implies zero. **It does not**: `MeshAdjacency::isClosed()` asks that **every** edge carry exactly **two** faces, while `borderEdgeCount()` counts edges carrying exactly **one**. An edge with **three** faces makes a mesh **not closed with zero border edges** — so `open` on its own cannot distinguish **a hole** from **a non-manifold junction**, and the count is precisely what separates them.
- **⚑ `gate_membrane.forge` IS THE CASE THAT SHOWS IT WORKING**: it reads `64 tri / radius 70 m / vol 0 m3` over **`not manifold   32 border edges`**, where J2's single word said only `not manifold` and hid the border loop entirely.
- **⚑⚑ IT NEEDED TWO LINES, AND THE ALTERNATIVE WAS ABBREVIATIONS.** The panel holds about **51 characters**; the size fields plus all three topology facts run **55** in full words, so a single line would have wrapped **mid-field on the common case**. The other way to fit was `rad` and `brd` — **and this tool writes things out** (`welded points`, `cache misses`, `border edges`), so the break was chosen instead and grouped: what the mesh **measures** above, what it **is** below.
- **⚑ THE NO-MESH BRANCH HOLDS THE SECOND LINE (`ImGui::NewLine()`), SO THE STATUS BLOCK IS A FIXED HEIGHT AND THE TAB BAR NEVER MOVES.** A panel whose furniture shifts with state is the *"save moves"* trap from the other side, and here it would move **the one row every drive recipe clicks**.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE NUMBER TO WATCH: `Texture` NOW SITS AT 855 OF 860, WITH FIVE PIXELS TO SPARE.** Measured Mesh 1,018 → 1,035 (scroll 179), Report 873 → 890 (scroll **34**), Texture 838 → **855 (scroll 0)**, View 476 → 493. **The texture editor still arrives whole — which is stage J's headline — but the next line added to this status block is what takes that away.** Worst case 162 → 179 against the original 2,045; the whole status block costs 1.7% of what the tabs bought.

**⚑⚑ J4 — `volume` DROPPED ON REQUEST, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT REFUSED TO GIVE THE LINE BACK.** `a5dba0a`. The block is now **`320 tri   radius 1.1584 m`** over **`closed   0 border edges`**. Twelve suites green in 87.6 s, no suite's count changed, tab heights unchanged.

- **⚑ WHAT THE FIELD CARRIED, RECORDED BECAUSE THE CHECK NOW LIVES ONLY ON THE `Report` TAB.** E5 measured that a wall or a split triangle **wound backwards leaves a mesh closed, manifold AND border-free** — the signed volume was the only number in the status block that catches an inside-out extrude. It is one click away rather than gone, but it is no longer under the eye during a drag.
- **⚑⚑ DROPPING IT DOES NOT BUY THE SECOND LINE BACK, AND THE BUDGET IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED.** `ImGui::GetContentRegionAvail().x` is **350.0 px** at **7.00 px/char** — 50 characters — and the widest one-line form, `64 tri  radius 70 m  not manifold  32 border edges`, measures **351.0**. **Over by exactly one pixel.** (J3's comment had guessed "about 51 characters"; the probe replaced the guess.)
- **⚑⚑ AND THE PIXEL IS NOT WORTH CHASING, WHICH IS THE REUSABLE HALF: THAT STRING ONLY COMES CLOSE BECAUSE THE ONE NON-MANIFOLD ASSET IN THE REPO HAPPENS TO BE SMALL.** A large one — `1068 tri … not manifold … 32 border edges` — needs **371 px**. A single line would therefore fit **by coincidence of which asset is currently abnormal**, which is **J2's "a status line checked against one asset is checked against nothing" lesson reappearing in the layout instead of the format.** Two fixed-height lines stay, and the tab bar still never moves.

**⚑⚑ J5 — `radius` OUT, `volume` BACK IN, AND THE TWO ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE.** `dbe60df`. The block settles at **`320 tri   vol 2.939 m3`** over **`closed   0 border edges`**. Twelve suites green in 87.8 s, no suite's count changed, **line count and therefore tab heights unchanged** (Mesh 179 / Report 34 / Texture 0 at 855 / View 0).

- **⚑⚑ THE SLOT WENT TO THE PER-DRAG NUMBER OVER THE STATIC ONE, AND THAT IS THE PRINCIPLE WORTH KEEPING.** **`volume` is a canary that moves the instant something goes wrong**: E5 measured that a wall or split triangle wound backwards leaves a mesh closed, manifold *and* border-free, so the signed volume is the only number in this block that catches an inside-out extrude. **`radius` is a reconciliation** — authored versus measured — and it **already had a better home on the `Report` tab**, where the `[[model]]` row prints the authored value beside it, warns when they disagree, and offers stage H's `use measured` button. ⚑ *A number you watch while your hand moves belongs in the status block; a number you reconcile against a def belongs beside the def.* **So this is an improvement rather than a trade**, and the four-turn sequence J2→J5 landed on it by the user rejecting two of my three proposed fields.
- **⚑ THE WIDTH DISCIPLINE SURVIVES THE SWAP, RE-CHECKED ON THE ASSET THAT HAS CAUGHT EVERY EARLIER VERSION**: `gate_membrane.forge` reads `64 tri   vol 0 m3` over `not manifold   32 border edges`, both complete, no wrap. `%.5g` left with `radius`; `volume` keeps the Report's own `%.4g`.
- **⚑ THE DIFF IS 47 INSERTIONS AND 5 DELETIONS UNDER `git diff -w`, AND 290/248 WITHOUT IT.** The bulk is a mechanical +8 reindent of the panel block. **Three lines had to be re-wrapped** to stay inside the 110-column limit (`bounds min`, `bounds max`, the `kLevelSwitchPixels` index) and one inline cast became a named `shading` temporary; **the `kLevelSwitchPixels` expression was deliberately re-wrapped rather than simplified**, because a stage that promises to change no logic must not quietly improve any. ⚑ clang-format is not installed on this machine and the standing rule forbids reformatting a whole file, so every wrap is by hand.
- **⚑ A DRIVE TRAP WORTH THE NEXT SESSION's TIME: A POSTED `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` DOES NOT CLICK WHERE YOU AIMED IT.** ImGui's win32 backend takes the mouse position from **`GetCursorPos()`**, not from the message, so `PostMessageW(WM_LBUTTONDOWN, ..., MAKELPARAM(x, y))` presses the button at wherever the physical pointer happens to be — the first drive photographed the `Mesh` tab still selected after three clean-looking clicks. **`SetCursorPos` first, wait a frame, then `mouse_event`.** See [[solproject-drive-script]].
- **⚑ WHAT THIS STAGE DID NOT DO.** No second window, no docking, no persisted layout, and **no automatic tab switching** — opening a `.tex` does not jump to `Texture` and a bake does not jump to `Report`, because a panel that moves under the hand is the failure the *"save moves"* trap has now been recorded three times. ⚑ The summary line was the one omission the user then asked for; see J2. **⚑ And it is still unplayed, like every Forge stage since F.**

**⚑⚑ STAGE K — PULL IT APART — BUILT AND MERGED 2026-08-22 ON `feat/pull-it-apart`, FROM PLAYTEST SESSION 14's TWO NOTES.** `9183cdd` K1 grouped mesh list, `ba0eeb6` K2 ImGui → `v1.91.5-docking`, `6ecb675` K3 docking + `forge.ini`, `853f64a` K4 four dockable windows + a menu bar, `252e5c6` K5 bottom status bar + primitives toolbar, `63bb31f` the panel-visibility fix. **17 files, +8,851/−663 (the bulk being the vendored ImGui swap). 12 suites green, `dev-gpu` 2/2, no suite's count changed, the game boots clean at 0 validation messages and still writes no `imgui.ini`.**

**⚑ THIS RECORD WAS WRITTEN AFTER THE FACT (2026-08-23, during stage L), NOT AS THE WORK HAPPENED.** The stage shipped without its plan entry, which AGENTS §8 requires in the same change set; it is reconstructed from the six commit messages and is faithful to them. **The gap itself is the note worth keeping: a programme that records nine stages and skips the tenth loses the tenth, and nothing in the build catches it.**

- **⚑⚑ THE SCOPE WAS THE USER'S, AND THEY PICKED THE LARGEST OF FOUR OPTIONS.** Session 14's notes were *"the mesh selection should separate the cooked from parts… adding folders or something to be able to collapse categories"* and *"adding some additional toolbars and windows would probably help a lot with improving the workflow."* The second had a **hard constraint**: `third_party/imgui` was **1.91.5 MASTER**, with no `ImGuiConfigFlags_DockingEnable`. Offered three cheaper shapes and the vendored swap, **the user took the swap.** K5 then came from two further verbatim instructions — *"the status line should be moved from the menu down to it's own small horizontal panel at the bottom"* and *"a toolbar that has buttons to add primitives to the mesh"*.
- **⚑⚑ THE VERSION PIN WAS THE REAL DECISION: `v1.91.5` → `v1.91.5-docking`, THE SAME VERSION, SO DOCKING IS THE ONLY VARIABLE.** 1.92.x was the tempting alternative and is wrong here — it **rewrote the font atlas and changed `ImTextureID` semantics**, and the Forge casts a `VkDescriptorSet` straight to `ImTextureID` for its texture preview, so that breakage would have landed mid-swap **looking like docking's fault**. ⚑ All 16 vendored files were **byte-identical to upstream v1.91.5** beforehand (hash-checked against the release tarball), so nothing local had to be carried across, and **no call site changed**. ⚑ The swap is proved a no-op: the panel is **pixel-identical, 0 of 314,590 px**, with the same region's same-binary noise floor also 0.
- **⚑⚑ K's HEADLINE: THE PART THAT MAKES A DOCKABLE TOOL WORK IS THE PART THAT SILENTLY DID NOT, AND PERSISTENCE FAILED IN TWO INDEPENDENT PLACES THAT PRESENT AS ONE.** (1) **`DockBuilderGetNode` returns NULL before `DockSpace()` has run for the frame** — the node is materialised *from* the loaded settings by that call — so a "do I have a saved layout?" check placed before it answered **no on every launch** and the default was rebuilt over the author's. Ask **after** the call, and ask **"is the root SPLIT"**, because after that call the node always exists and its existence answers nothing. (2) Even then the saved **tab** was wrong: **ImGui focuses a window as it APPEARS** and all four appear on frame one, so the last submitted stole the selection — `ImGuiWindowFlags_NoFocusOnAppearing`. ⚑ A third, related: **`DockBuilder` sets tab ORDER but leaves the last-docked window SELECTED**, and focusing on the build frame is silently dropped because the window has never been submitted.
- **⚑⚑ THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT GENERALISES: THE DOCK STRUCTURE PERSISTING IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT THE SELECTION DOES.** Same file, two pieces of state, one broken — and **the WRITE half worked the whole time**, so inspecting `forge.ini` "confirmed persistence" while the feature did nothing. **The passing test is the one that RE-SAVES**: click, close, read the file, relaunch, close, and check it still says the same thing.
- **⚑⚑ AND IT BIT A SECOND TIME, ON A SECOND PIECE OF STATE, FOUND BY THE USER SIMPLY USING THE TOOL** (`63bb31f`): with the layout persisting perfectly, **which panels were OPEN reset every launch**. `Begin(name, p_open)` treats `p_open` as the **application's** bool — ImGui saves position, size, dock node and selected tab and **never that flag**. ***Fixing one piece of a file's contents is not fixing the file; after repairing a round trip, enumerate what else rides in it.*** ⚑ Fixed with an `ImGuiSettingsHandler` (`[ForgePanels][Visible]`) in the **same** `forge.ini` rather than a second file with a second lifetime, **registered before the first `NewFrame`** because that is when the ini loads, with the dirty mark **compared per frame** rather than raised at each of the three places a panel can close.
- **⚑ `PassthruCentralNode` IS LOAD-BEARING**: the dockspace covers the window, so without it the tool is a grey sheet over the 3D view with every orbit and point-drag swallowed. Verified by a 20-step centre drag orbiting the model (18.7% of the viewport changes) while the panel content does not.
- **⚑ THE TWO-COLUMN DEFAULT IS MEASURED, NOT TASTE. `Texture` must keep the FULL height or stage J's headline breaks** — the editor is 821 px and arrives whole, and tabbing it into a half-height node puts it back under a scrollbar, undoing the one thing about J a person had already called right.
- **⚑ BOTH BARS ARE CHROME RATHER THAN WINDOWS, AND THAT IS THE POINT: `BeginViewportSideBar` SETS `NoDocking` ITSELF**, so neither can be closed, docked, hidden behind a tab or dragged off — the property a status line needs now that **every panel in the tool can be all four**. ⚑ A side bar reports its size into the work area **for the next frame**, so both must be submitted **before** the dockspace, which is sized from what is left.
- **⚑ THE TOOLBAR IS DERIVED FROM `forgePrimitives()` RATHER THAN LISTED**, so it cannot drift from the format's vocabulary and a new primitive gets a button free. **`mesh` is the one deliberate omission** — it is what `bake` *produces*, so a button adding an empty one hands the author an invisible part; the `add part` combo still offers it. ⚑ `addPrimitive` became a `PartEditor` **method with two callers** rather than a loop body with one — *a rule applied in two places is a defect in the one nobody looked at* — and picked up a bounds check the original lacked.
- **⚑ K1's GROUPING ALREADY EXISTED, UNEXPRESSED.** `listMeshes` is four sequential `collect()` calls, so rows were **already contiguous and already tagged**; the tag moved off the label into a field and one pass finds each run's end. **21 rows, 13 cooked (62%)**, and stage-F LOD siblings mean the cooked half **grows on its own**. ⚑ The `###` on the header ID is load-bearing: ImGui keys tree state on the **label**, which carries a **count**, so cooking a new asset would silently re-open a closed section. ⚑ The draw helper lives in `main.cpp` because `mesh_library.cpp` compiles standalone into `sol_forge_tests`, which links no ImGui — **Phase 15's linkage rule applied before the promise**.
- **⚑ K5's PROOF IS ARITHMETIC**: clicking `box` on `asteroid.forge` moves the status bar **320 → 332 tri** and **2.939 → 3.939 m³**. Twelve triangles is a cube, 1.000 m³ is the primitive's `1×1×1` default — **both predictable before the click**, which is the job the block exists for.
- **⚑ WHAT K COSTS, SAID PLAINLY: THE VIEWPORT IS NARROWER** — two ~385 px columns leave ~827 px of 1,600 where the old single panel left ~1,220. That is the price of seeing `Points, edges & faces` and the `Report` at once, it is the author's to undo, and `Layout ▸ Reset layout` is the escape hatch persistence made mandatory.
- **⚑ KNOWN, RECORDED, NOT FIXED**: the **first-ever** launch draws the dock edge ~3 px higher than every launch after; launches 2 and 3 are pixel-identical (0 of 290,696), so it settles once and never drifts. ⚑ Also: **ImGui's dark `WindowBg` is alpha 0.94**, so the moving grid bleeds through an opaque-looking panel — invisible to the eye, real to a pixel diff, and not a docking bug.
- **✅⚑⚑ K IS THE MOST HUMAN-VERIFIED THING IN THIS PROGRAMME, WITH THREE SEPARATE CONFIRMATIONS**: *"layout saving and reset seems to be working"*; *"yes i saw them both at the same time"* — `Points, edges & faces` and the `Report` on screen together, **the entire justification for the docking work**; and *"looks like the panels now have their states properly saved and the layout loads how it should when I relaunch forge."* ⚑ **The layout itself is only *"liking the direction"* — directional, not a specific sign-off.**

**⚑⚑ STAGE L — THE BLENDER BRIDGE — BUILT 2026-08-23 ON `feat/blender-bridge`, AND IT IS THE FIRST SCOPE ON THIS PROGRAMME THE USER PICKED OUTRIGHT RATHER THAN FROM A LIST.** Asked what the session should be, with four options on the table including a playtest, the answer was *"add the ability to bridge with blender so we can import assets we create in blender into forge right from blender for added QoL"*. L1 the normal fix, L2 the import, L3 the inbox watcher, L4 the addon, then the re-import fix the user's own testing forced. **`cooker.unit` 30 → 31, `forge.unit` 10 → 14**; no other suite's count changed, no save, renderer or game change, and **not one committed asset altered**.

- **⚑⚑ THE DIAGNOSIS IS THAT MOST OF IT WAS ALREADY BUILT, AND THE ONE MISSING PIECE WAS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE.** `importGltf` has read Blender's format since stage D; the cooker has cooked `.gltf → .smesh` since Phase 5; `listMeshes` already lists `.gltf` and `loadMesh` already opens one; and `forgeBakePart` already turns a `MeshData` into a literal `mesh` part with `writeForge` to write it out. **So a Blender export dropped under `assets/` is a shippable game asset today with no code at all.** What it is *not* is editable: `isPartSource` admits only `.forge`, so it arrives read-only and every tool stages E–I built is unavailable on it. **The stage is the conversion, not the import** — fifteenth consecutive item this repo had already written down.
- **⚑⚑ THE ARCHITECTURE WAS DECIDED BY A GUARD RATHER THAN BY TASTE, AND THE COST OF GETTING IT WRONG IS THE WHOLE BUILD.** The obvious design drops the glTF into `assets/meshes/` beside the `.forge` it becomes. **The cooker walks the source tree with a RECURSIVE `listFiles` into ONE FLAT output directory keyed on the file STEM**, so `ship.gltf` and `ship.forge` both cook to `ship.smesh` — and stage D's collision guard **does not skip the pair, it aborts the entire cook**. Measured rather than assumed: putting the probe glTF beside its own `.forge` gives `1 output collision(s); nothing cooked`, **exit 1**, with all 21 other assets uncooked. **The inbox is therefore `blender-inbox/` at the repository root, outside `assets/`**, and the glTF is **transport**: once converted the `.forge` is the source, which is exactly what `gltf.hpp` already argued when it called export *"an INTEROP action, not a pipeline step"*.
- **⚑⚑ L1 IS A DEFECT THE BRIDGE MADE REACHABLE, AND IT HAD BEEN SITTING UNDER A COMMENT ADMITTING IT.** `gltf.cpp` transformed normals by the node matrix itself — *"Rotation part only; assumes no non-uniform scale on normals (v0)"* — which is wrong the moment a node scales unevenly. **Nothing in the repo could tell**: every mesh in `assets/meshes/` is a `.forge`, and the generator that wrote the last `.gltf` never scaled a node. **A Blender scene breaks it in one keystroke** (`S` `X` `2` writes straight into the node). Fixed to the inverse transpose. ⚑ **The can-fail input had to be BUILT — E4d's pattern again — and the case is chosen so no tolerance can straddle it: a 45° normal under a 2× stretch in X reads `(0.4472, 0.8944)` correct and `(0.8944, 0.4472)` wrong, THE SAME TWO NUMBERS EXACTLY SWAPPED.** ⚑ **And the unit-length check still passes on the mutant**, so it is the *ordering* that carries the assertion — a length assertion alone would have been a test that could not fail.
- **⚑ ONE PART PER NODE, NOT PER PRIMITIVE, AND `importGltf` BECAME A MERGE OVER THE NEW WALK RATHER THAN A SECOND ONE.** A glTF mesh's primitives are its **material** split and this engine draws one texture per mesh, so they are one object to everything downstream. The cooker still wants one buffer and the Forge wants a tree; **two traversals would be two answers to what a node is**, which is the trap this programme has already paid for twice (the glTF/`.forge` pair, and the shipped-mesh script).
- **⚑⚑ THE MERGE RULE IS WHAT MAKES THE BRIDGE USABLE MORE THAN ONCE, AND THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION SILENTLY DELETES WORK.** Re-importing **matches parts BY ID** and replaces a match **whole** — geometry *and* placement — keeping only the `id`, the `parent` and the comment trivia above it. Parts the author added in the Forge are **left alone**. ⚑ The placement is dropped **deliberately**: the node transform is already baked into the vertices, so a preserved placement is a **silent double transform** rather than preserved intent. ⚑ Blender's names are sanitised (`Fin.001` → `Fin_001`), and an all-separator name still has to produce something legal because **an empty id fails `parseForge`** — the import would write a file it could not read back.
- **⚑⚑ THE EXIT CRITERION IS ONE ASSERTION AND IT HOLDS AT EXACT FLOAT EQUALITY: the mesh the GAME gets out of the imported `.forge` is the mesh the COOKER would have got out of the glTF directly** — all the way through `writeForge` → `parseForge` → `buildForge`, not a tolerance. **Two mutations, two caught**: keeping the placement fails the double-transform check, and appending instead of matching by id fails four assertions at once.
- **⚑ A POLL RATHER THAN AN IPC, AND IT COSTS NO NEW PLATFORM SURFACE.** `fileModificationTime`, `listFiles`, `createDirectories` and `deleteFile` were all already in `sol::platform`, so noticing a drop needs nothing Windows-shaped above the platform layer (AGENTS §4). Throttled to ~2 Hz; a socket would buy latency against a human alt-tabbing out of Blender. ⚑ **The FIRST poll only takes a census**: a file already in the inbox at launch was imported in an earlier session, and re-importing it would undo whatever was done in the Forge since.
- **⚑⚑⚑ THE MERGE'S BASE IS THE OPEN DOCUMENT, NOT THE FILE — AND THIS IS THE ONE THING L GOT WRONG FIRST, FOUND BY THE USER IN MINUTES.** The first version read the `.forge` from disk and asked `editor.dirty()`, refusing if the target had unsaved edits. The user imported, added a part in the Forge, re-sent from Blender, and **their part was gone**. ⚑⚑ **TWO DEFECTS, AND THE FIRST IS K5's: `PartEditor::addPrimitive` NEVER SET `m_dirty`.** `draw()` sets it from its own `changed` flag at the tail, which covered the `add part` combo because the combo lives inside `draw()` — but K5 gave the method a **second caller**, the primitives toolbar in `main.cpp`, outside all of that. **Fourth sighting of *a rule applied in two places is a defect in the one nobody looked at*, and K5's own comment on that method warned of exactly this while moving only the PLACEMENT rule in.** ⚑ Side finding: **`Reload` had therefore been discarding toolbar-added parts since K5**, nothing to do with Blender. ⚑⚑ **THE FIX IS NOT "ALSO SET THE FLAG": *the file on disk is not the document, it is the document as of the last save*,** so the merge now takes the **editor's live doc** as its base whenever that file is open — correct whether or not anything remembered to mark it dirty — and is **adopted as ONE UNDOABLE EDIT** (`PartEditor::adoptDoc`) rather than re-opened, so the import is not the single edit in this tool a person cannot take back. ⚑ **Auto-opening the result is NOT the "save moves" trap stage J avoided** — the author pressed a button in Blender *in order to* see it here.
- **⚑⚑⚑ AND THE METHOD LESSON, WHICH IS THE MOST REUSABLE THING IN THE STAGE: MY OWN LIVE CHECK OF THE MERGE PASSED AND PROVED NOTHING, BECAUSE IT SET UP THROUGH THE FILE.** It added the surviving part with `Add-Content` onto the `.forge` — **the one way of adding a part that puts it exactly where the buggy code was already looking.** *When a test builds its fixture through the same channel the code under test reads, it cannot observe a channel mismatch.* Set the fixture up the way a **user** does. ⚑ Now proved by an A/B on one drive reproducing the reported sequence (send → click `box` **on the toolbar** → send): fixed reads `0 added, 2 replaced, 1 kept` with the box in the file, mutant reads `0 added, 2 replaced` without it. ⚑ Note the two *failure* modes — a missed click and a broken merge — are indistinguishable from each other and both distinguishable from success, so a success run proves both and a failure run would need a second witness.
- **⚑ THE ADDON FILTERS ITS EXPORT ARGUMENTS AGAINST THE OPERATOR'S OWN RNA RATHER THAN NAMING THEM.** Blender's glTF exporter argument list moves between versions and an unknown keyword is a hard error, so one file works across versions instead of carrying a per-version branch. ⚑ Its pinned settings are all forced rather than chosen: **+Y up** (the engine is Y-up), **apply modifiers** (an unapplied Bevel exports as the cage), **no Draco** (this repo has no decompressor, so a compressed file fails at *read*), **GLB** (self-contained, and unlike embedded `.gltf` never deprecated), and no materials/animation/skins because nothing downstream reads them.
- **⚑⚑ LIVE PROOF IS THE WHOLE LOOP AGAINST A REAL BLENDER 5.2.0 LTS, DRIVEN HEADLESS, WITH THE FORGE ALREADY RUNNING.** Exporting a two-object scene lands as `bridgeprobe.forge` with 2 parts and opens itself. **56 triangles is the proof the modifier was applied** — a beveled cube is 44 and an unapplied cage is 12, so 12 + 44 = 56 where an unapplied export would read 24. ⚑⚑ **AND THE Y-UP CONVERSION HAD TO BE MEASURED SEPARATELY, BECAUSE A BOUNDING RADIUS CANNOT SHOW IT: `radius` is a sum of squares and comes out 3.028 whichever axis carries which.** The bounds do show it — a fin authored at Blender Z 2.5 with scale `(3, 1, 0.25)` arrives at **x ±1.5, y [2.375, 2.625], z ±0.5**. *A number that is invariant under the error you are checking for is not a check.*
- **⚑ WHAT STAGE L DID NOT DO.** **No round trip back to Blender** — `exportGltf` exists and could be wired to a button, but a `.forge` that has been edited here carries a part tree a glTF cannot hold, and `gltf.hpp` has said so since stage D. **No material or texture import**: a mesh's texture is its `[[model]]` row's business, which is stage H's. **No `[[model]]` row written on import** — an imported mesh still needs stage H's `create [[model]] row`, and the freighter's flight deck already recorded that the three values that button cannot infer are the ones that matter. **No armature, no animation** — Phase 9's out-of-scope list is unchanged.
- **✅⚑⚑ IT HAS MET A PERSON, AND THE DIRECT QUESTION PAID FOR THE EIGHTH TIME.** The volunteered report was *"the bridge seems to be working as expected for now"* — the same shape as session 13's *"seem to be working properly for now"*, which had hidden total non-contact. **One direct question** — did you send the same asset twice, with a part added in the Forge in between? — **produced the re-import defect above.** ⚑ **What is still unexercised: a scene with MANY objects.** One part per Blender object is the choice this stage is least sure of, it has been driven at two, and a forty-object scene may recreate the very problem stage J solved.

**E. Direct mesh editing.** Select and move vertices, edges and faces; extrude, split, merge two objects into one. This is where the tool stops being parametric and becomes a modeller, and it is the largest and least certain stage: it needs picking (precedent exists — `sol/ui/pick.hpp` and `game/src/target_pick.cpp`), transform gizmos, and undo. **The source-of-truth rule it needed is settled at the D checkpoint above — hand-editing bakes, per part — and the format that rule needs is BUILT** (`ForgePrimitive::Mesh` + `forgeBakePart`, proved against the asteroid), so what E inherits is a decided rule and a working bake, not an open question. **Its clearest motivating case is `ship.forge`**, where moving one shared corner means editing four parts by hand.

**F. Optimise and LOD.** Weld, dedupe and decimate exposed in the tool with before/after counts and a side-by-side preview at the distance the LOD is for. **⚑ This stage must also add LOD selection to the renderer or it authors dead data** — `RenderInstance` names one model today and the impostor path is celestial-only.

**G. Textures.** The three procedural textures are the regression set, as the five meshes are for B. Panel grids, wear and faction liveries; BC1 is what the cooker already produces and R8 is reserved for glyph coverage.

**H. Def rows.** Author a whole ship or station as content — mesh, model row and `ships.toml`/`stations.toml` row — validated against the strict schema that already exists in `data_defs.cpp` rather than against a second one written in the tool.

**Tests.** `geometry.unit` (new, the 10th suite) carries B: welding is idempotent and tolerance-respecting; adjacency is consistent after every operation; merging two solids preserves volume-by-triangle-signature; decimation is monotonic in target count and never produces a non-manifold edge from a manifold input; and **the five shipped meshes round-trip through the new path unchanged**, which is the assertion that would fail if any of it were wrong. `assets.unit` gains the `[[model]]` schema, including rejection of a row naming a mesh that does not exist. `cooker.unit` gains the `.forge` importer at D. **The tool's own UI is verified by running it and saying what was seen**, per AGENTS §7 — there is no suite for a viewport, and pretending otherwise is how `game/src` ended up with 21,000 untested lines.

**Exit (programme).** A person who is not the author can open `tools/forge`, build a ship out of blocks, adjust it by hand, see it lit at real scale beside a station, generate its LODs, save it, and fly it in the game without writing a line of C++ or PowerShell. **Exit (first slice, A–C).** A new mesh authored by any means reaches the game through a def row; the mesh operations are proved headless; and the viewer shows any of it at real scale.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No animation, no skinning, no rigging** — nothing in the engine has a bone and adding one is a renderer programme of its own. **No material system**: the mesh pipeline is one texture plus a scalar emissive, and a tool that authored PBR channels would be authoring for a renderer that does not exist. **No UV unwrapping** beyond what the primitives generate per face; the existing meshes are box-mapped by construction and an unwrapper is a research project. **No change to `.smesh`** in A–C — the format already stores `uint32` indices and the vertex layout the pipeline wants. **No asset hot-reload into the running game** in the first slice; the cook-and-relaunch loop is what exists and the viewer is what removes the pain the hot-reload would have removed. **And no new third-party dependency**: mesh import, geometry, decimation and image encoding are all written in this repo, per AGENTS §5.

### Phase 10 — You Are Here: Three Surfaces That Do Not Know Where The Player Is ✅ (completed 2026-08-19, playtested 2026-08-19)

**From playtest session 8's notes 1, 2 and 3**, taken together. The session returned **six** notes, the biggest haul in the project's history, and — for the third time running — **not one of them was about the mechanics of the phases it was the first flight of.** Nobody complained about 8x's traders, 8y's avoidance or 8z's discovery fog, and the thing the hand-over flagged as most likely to read as broken (an empty sky with nothing prompting `R`) drew no comment at all. Notes 4 and 5 (faction personalities, coherent world gen) and note 6 (the gate membrane) stay on the board.

**The three are one phase rather than three because they are the same complaint from three angles: the game knows where the player is and three of its surfaces do not say so.** The map points at whatever was last clicked, the ship arrives pointing back at the door it just came through, and a name too long for its box is cut off mid-word with nothing to say it was. **Phase 9's stages E–H are not abandoned** — they are behind this on the project's own standing precedent that player-found work outranks tool work.

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change; three of the backlog's estimates moved and one of them was wrong in a direction that would have shipped a bug).**

- **Note 1 — the selection is reconciled against the LIST and never against the SHIP.** `map_screen.cpp:624-627` resets `state.selectedSystem` to `panel.currentIndex` only when it is *out of range*, so after a jump it keeps pointing wherever the player last clicked. 8q made the System tab follow the Galaxy tab's selection (`main.cpp:1244` feeds `mapPanel.viewSystem` from it) and **nothing makes either follow the ship.** `MapScreenState` is owned across frames by `main.cpp` and **holds nothing that remembers which system the player was in last time the screen was built**, so "has it changed?" is a question the screen cannot ask today. That one remembered int is the whole stage. The manual answer already exists and is the shape to copy: the System tab's **"Show Current"** button (`map_screen.cpp:735-740`) does by hand exactly what this note asks the game to do on its own.
- **⚑ Note 2 — `loadSystem` never writes `orientation` at all, and the comment directly above it says it does.** *"Arrival point: off the gate we came through, facing the playfield"* (`space_world.cpp:3229-3230`); the code writes `position`, `previousPosition` and `previousOrientation` (`3247-3249`) and **the facing half has never existed.** Reproduced live at the checkpoint: autopilot through the Queis→Lyrioa gate and the ring is dead ahead filling the view, then **one press of `W` sends you straight back through it**, arriving in the same state, so it repeats. The user's own hypothesis — autopilot staying engaged and turning the ship back — is disproved by `space_world.cpp:3221`, which already clears it inside `loadSystem`.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE HELPER THE BACKLOG NAMED IS THE WRONG ONE, WHICH IS THE ONE ESTIMATE THAT MOVED IN THE EXPENSIVE DIRECTION.** `facingRotation` (`space_world.cpp:110`) takes the model's **+Z** onto an axis — it exists to turn the **gate slab**, which is thin on Z, to face its lane. **The ship's nose is −Z.** The helper that is actually wanted is `lookAlong` (`space_world.cpp:2243`), *"shortest-arc rotation taking the ship's nose (-z) onto `direction`"*, already used three times for trader, miner and paced-hauler headings. Taking the backlog's two-line fix at its word would have arrived with the **tail** pointed at the playfield: the same defect with an extra 180° on it, and it would have looked deliberate.
- **⚑ THE CHECK THAT MAKES WRITING ORIENTATION THERE SAFE: `loadSystem` HAS FOUR CALL SITES AND A SAVE LOAD IS NOT ONE OF THEM.** New game (`:279`), the jump swap (`:3356`), the dev teleport (`:4084`) and the death respawn (`:6307`). A snapshot load calls **`rebuildSystemSideData` alone** — *"used alone after a snapshot load, which already carries the statics"* (`space_world.hpp:1342-1343`) — so setting a heading in `loadSystem` cannot clobber the one a save carried. That is the question a two-line change to a shared function has to answer before it is two lines.
- **⚑ AND THE TRAP INSIDE THE TWO LINES: THE RENDER INTERPOLATES THE ROTATION.** `nlerp(transform.previousOrientation, transform.orientation, alpha)` (`space_world.cpp:6565` and `:6591`). Line `3249` copies the **old** orientation into `previousOrientation`, so writing the new heading above it makes the arrival frame visibly swing the ship through the entire turn. Both fields must be set to the same value — which is exactly what the dev `park` lever already does (`:1744-1745`) and what every puppet spawn does (`:2463-2464`, `:2707-2708`).
- **⚑ AND THE DIRECTION IS ALREADY COMPUTED ON THE LINE THAT PLACES YOU.** `arrival = gate.position + normalize(hub - gate.position) * 500.0` (`:3236`) — the offset that puts the ship in front of the gate is the same unit vector that should turn it. The fix reuses a value one line up rather than deriving a new one.
- **Note 3 — the measure exists, and the backlog's estimate was wrong in the cheap direction on that half.** `assets::Font::measureWidth` (`font.hpp:44`) is already used four times in `game_ui.cpp` for chip sizing and is covered in `font_tests.cpp`. `map_screen.cpp` also already carries a local `clipped()` (`:112`) that pushes a clip round a label — and it is used for the marker row's **detail** column one line below the offending call (`:558`), so **the right-hand cell of that row clips and the name cell does not.**
- **⚑ WHAT IS GENUINELY MISSING IS THE HOVER, AND IT IS MISSING BY ONE RETURN VALUE.** `UiContext::selectable` (`context.cpp:461-478`) computes `interaction.hovered`, uses it to tint the row, and then returns only `interaction.activated`. **The fifth "a widget does not clip to its box" in this repo** (8s's comms sender was the first actually fixed, the Forge's catalog warning the fourth) is therefore two small additions rather than one large one.
- **⚑ AND THERE IS NO LATE DRAW LAYER, BUT `endFrame()` IS ONE.** `DrawList` batches strictly in call order (`selectBatch` extends the batch matching the current texture and clip), so a tooltip drawn during the list is overdrawn by the map beside it. **`UiContext::endFrame()` already runs after every widget** and the clip stack is empty by then, so geometry added there lands on top **by construction** rather than by a z-order the DrawList does not have. That is the cheap half of what the backlog priced as the unknown.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **The map snaps only when the current system actually changes** — never on open, never per frame. A player browsing the galaxy must not be fought by their own ship. (2) **Arrival faces the playfield hub, not merely "away from the gate"** — at a gate they are the same direction and the hub is the one the comment already promised. (3) **A fresh start faces its home station**, and the hub fallback faces the hub. (4) **The ellipsis and the tooltip are `sol::ui` facilities, not `map_screen` ones** — this is the fifth instance of the same flaw and the fix belongs where the next one will look for it.

**A. The map follows the ship.** `MapScreenState` gains one remembered int (the `panel.currentIndex` it last reconciled against). `buildMapScreen` snaps `selectedSystem` to it and clears `selectedMarker` when it changes, **before the tab branch**, so it holds whichever tab is up and whether or not the map happened to be open at the moment of the jump — the first build after a jump is what sees the change. The existing out-of-range guards stay: a stale index and a changed system are different failures. **Clearing the marker matters as much as the snap** — `selectedMarker` indexes the new system's list, and the guard at `:687-690` only clamps it, so a same-length list would leave the selection pointing at a different object with no visible change.

**B. Arrive facing the playfield.** `loadSystem` sets `transform.orientation = lookAlong(...)` and `previousOrientation` to the same value, from the hub vector already computed for the arrival offset. The death respawn's pad override (`:6308-6320`) gets the same treatment in the same change, because it moves the ship *after* `loadSystem` has faced it. **Exit is a real autopilot crossing, never `sol.jump()`** — see the method note below.

**C. A name that does not fit says so.** Three additions to `sol::ui`: hover exposed off `selectable`, a label that ellipsizes to its box through `Font::measureWidth`, and a deferred tooltip flushed in `endFrame()`. `map_screen`'s marker list and system list both use them; nothing else changes, because the two map lists are the reported surface and the station screens were signed off in session 5.

**Tests.** `ui.unit` carries C, which is the only headless half: ellipsis of an empty string, a string that fits, a string one glyph over, and a box too narrow for even the ellipsis; the tooltip's geometry appears after every widget's; and hover is reported for the row under the cursor and no other. **A and B have no suite and are honest about it** — `map_screen.cpp` and `space_world.cpp`'s arrival block are `game/src`, which has no test target, so both are verified by flight and by saying what was seen, per AGENTS §7.

**⚑ The method rule this phase inherits, and it is the session's own most expensive finding.** **`sol.jump()` and a real gate crossing arrive with DIFFERENT HEADINGS.** The dev teleport keeps whatever heading the player already held, so the gate ends up *behind* them (forward thrust took gate distance 500 m → 1.5 km); a real crossing keeps the **crossing** heading, so the ring is dead ahead. **The stage-D drive recorded "a crossing leaves the gate behind you" on the strength of `sol.jump()`, and that note sat wrong in the handoff until flying refuted it.** Any assertion about arrival state in this phase must come from an autopilot crossing. That is 8u's rule — a dev lever that reaches a state the sim cannot is a second implementation — meeting arrival.

**Exit.** An autopilot crossing into a system arrives with the playfield ahead and the gate astern, and `W` flies you inward rather than back through it; a fresh game faces its home station; opening the map after a jump shows the system you are standing in on both tabs, while browsing to another system and staying put leaves the selection alone; a marker name longer than its cell reads with an ellipsis and gives its full name on hover; all 10 suites green, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No change to the jump mechanic or 8w's aperture crossing** — this phase changes what you are pointing at on arrival and nothing about how you get there. **No re-tuning of the 500 m arrival offset**, which is 8w's number and is not what the note is about. **No roll control**: `lookAlong` is a shortest-arc rotation, so the bank angle on arrival is whatever that arc gives — the same treatment every trader and miner already gets, and a thing to *look at* in the drive rather than to design around in advance. **No ellipsis pass over the rest of the game's widgets** — the facility lands in `sol::ui` so the next one is cheap, but only the two map lists are the reported defect. **No tooltip anywhere else**, and no hover delay: it shows while the cursor is on the row, because a delay is a feel decision with no complaint behind it.

**⚑ PHASE 10 IS COMPLETE** (`963b0e5` spec, `5e65ec1` A, `7486764` B, `4e7b366` C). `ui.unit` **84 → 87**. No save change, no sim change, six files.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE ONE ESTIMATE THAT WAS WRONG IN THE EXPENSIVE DIRECTION WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A BUG, AND ONLY READING THE HELPER CAUGHT IT.** The backlog's two-line fix named `facingRotation`, which takes the model's **+Z** onto an axis — it exists to turn the **gate slab**, which is thin on Z, to face its lane. **A ship's nose is −Z.** Taking the note at its word arrives with the **tail** toward the playfield: the same defect with an extra 180° on it, and it would have looked deliberate. The right helper, `lookAlong` (`space_world.cpp:2243`), was already in the file and already used three times for trader, miner and paced-hauler headings. **A two-line change is exactly the size at which nobody re-reads the helper.**
- **⚑ THE TWO QUESTIONS A CHANGE TO A SHARED FUNCTION OWES, both answered by reading rather than by hoping.** (1) `loadSystem` has **four** call sites — new game, the jump swap, the dev teleport, the death respawn — and **a save load is not one of them** (a snapshot load calls `rebuildSystemSideData` alone), so writing a heading there cannot clobber one a save carried. (2) The render **nlerps** `previousOrientation` to `orientation`, so writing only the new heading swings the ship visibly through the whole turn on the arrival frame; both ends of the tick are set, which is what the dev `park` lever and every puppet spawn already do.
- **The direction was already on the line above.** `arrival = gate.position + normalize(hub - gate.position) * 500.0` — the vector that *places* the ship is the vector that should *turn* it, so the fix reuses a value rather than deriving a second one that could drift from it.
- **⚑ NOTE 3 GOT CHEAPER ON BOTH HALVES THE BACKLOG PRICED AS UNKNOWN.** The measure exists: `assets::Font::measureWidth`, used four times in `game_ui.cpp` for chip sizing and covered in `font_tests.cpp`. And the missing "layer above everything" is **`UiContext::endFrame()`** — the `DrawList` batches strictly in call order and has no z-order, so *drawn last* **is** on top, and endFrame already runs after every widget with an empty clip stack. **There was no layer to add, only a place to draw.**
- **⚑ THE FIX WENT INSIDE `selectable` RATHER THAN AT THE CALL SITE, AND THE CALLER LIST IS WHY.** There are three in the whole game: the two map lists that are the reported defect, and one station-screen cell whose labels are two characters long and can never trip it. **A row whose text runs out through its own edge is always a defect, and a row whose text was hidden always owes the player the whole of it** — neither is a caller's judgement, so the elision and the tooltip both live in the widget. This is the **fifth** time this repo has met "a widget does not clip to its box" and the first time the fix landed where the next one will look for it.
- **Truncation walks whole codepoints, and the ellipsis is three periods.** Cutting on a byte boundary orphans half a UTF-8 sequence, which decodes to U+FFFD and draws as `?`, so a long name would come out looking *corrupted* rather than *long*. `U+2026` is exactly the glyph a subset atlas leaves out, and the font falls back to `?` for anything it was not baked with — so the ellipsis that says "there is more" would have said "something is broken".
- **⚑ A DRIVE LESSON THAT COST THREE RUNS, AND IT IS ABOUT PAUSE PARITY.** The 8z method is *pause first, then type*, and it is right — but the drive has to **know** whether it is paused, and Esc is not a reliable toggle. After `Run-Console` the console's `InputText` holds keyboard focus and **eats the next Esc**; a defocus click at client (640,347) is the **Save Game** button, whose dialog eats it instead; and (900,620) is over the **3D view**, so it selects a nav target and cancels autopilot. Any of the three desynchronises the parity, and the failure is silent and *looks like working output*: the command still reaches the console and prints correctly, while its letters simultaneously fire `r`/`t`/`y`/`b` into a live game. **The fix is to stop toggling and click the pause menu's own Resume button** — a mouse click on a widget, deterministic in both states, and a no-op that merely lands in the 3D view if the game was already running.
- **⚑ AND AUTOPILOT IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT FOR A 500 M GATE.** `sol.autopilot(100)` engages cruise and overshot the ring by **2,280 km**; the crossing has to be flown manually, which is the recipe the 8w and stage-D drives already recorded. Any click in the 3D view also cancels it (*"Autopilot: cancelled by manual input"*), so a drive that defocuses the console by clicking the world has silently disarmed the thing it was about to measure.
- **⚑ A HOVER CANNOT BE PHOTOGRAPHED THROUGH THE DEV OVERLAY.** The first tooltip shot hovered exactly the right row and showed nothing: the cursor was over the ImGui perf overlay, which covers client x 20..560 for the full height, and the game gates its own mouse on `imguiHost.wantsMouseCapture()`. **F1 hides the console and F2 must be pressed TWICE** (Full → Compact → Hidden) before a game-UI row is both visible and hoverable.

**Live proof, all three stages, three clean runs each exiting 0 through the pause menu.** **B, flown rather than teleported**: `sol.jump()` for setup only (Lyrioa → Queis, parked `gd=499`), then turn about and thrust — `jumping: Queis -> Lyrioa`, a real aperture crossing — arriving at `gd=843`, and **six seconds of forward thrust took the gate from 843 m to 2,026 m.** The press of `W` that used to send the player straight back through now flies them into the playfield. **A**: the map opened before the jump reads *"Lyrioa - visited, you are here"*; reopened after the jump with no other interaction it reads *"Queis - visited, you are here"* over Queis's own markers, where the stale selection would have kept showing the system just left. **C**: a bookmark named *"Deep Survey Outpost Kappa Nine Relay"* draws as `* Deep Survey ...` inside its cell and gives the whole name in a tooltip under the cursor, above everything. All 10 suites green in 82 s; `dev-gpu` 2/2.

**Known gaps.** **A and B have no headless test** and cannot cheaply get one: `map_screen.cpp` and `loadSystem`'s arrival block are `game/src`, which has no test target, so both rest on the drive above. **The tooltip has no delay and no wrap** — it shows the instant the cursor lands and draws on one line, so a name longer than the screen is wide would be clamped against the edge rather than wrapped; no name in the game is close. **`labelElided` is used only by `selectable`**: `label` itself still draws past its box, so the other four widgets that have met this defect are unfixed and only the reported one is closed. **Nothing faces the ship on undock**, which is unchanged behaviour — `undock()` has never written an orientation, so you leave a berth pointing wherever you docked. **A death respawn's heading is now the station it woke at**, chosen to match the fresh-start rule rather than because anything asked for it.

**⚑ PLAYTEST VERDICT (session 9, 2026-08-19) — TWO OF THE THREE CONFIRMED BY A HUMAN, THE THIRD STILL UNSEEN.** *"flying through the gate feels much better now"* and *"system map updates to new system now which feels much better"* close stages B and A: both were reported by a player who was not looking for them, which is the only evidence this project counts for a feel change. **Stage C has no verdict** — *"didnt get an opportunity to see how the labels show up on the system screen when too long on this run"* — so the ellipsis and the tooltip rest on `ui.unit` and on one drive, and the player has said they will report next session. **Do not promote that silence to a sign-off**; it is the standing rule at the top of this section and it applies here in the ordinary way. Merged to `dev` as a fast-forward after the verdict.

### Phase 11 — How Long Until We Get There: An ETA On The Target Readout ✅ (completed 2026-08-19)

**From playtest session 9 (2026-08-19), the session's only note**: *"the panel that shows our destination and distance and relative speed should also show the ETA if we're flying at the target."* The same session closed Phase 10's stages A and B and left C unseen. **This is the smallest player-found item the project has taken**, and reading it still moved the scope, for the sixth consecutive item.

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **The panel is `drawTargetReadout` (`game_ui.cpp:439`)**, top-right on the glass. Four things stacked: the name (26 px), `faction [attitude]` (20 px, conditional), one row carrying **distance left and closing speed right** (`:473-481`, 24 px), and three ship meters (conditional). Its height at `:447` is a **sum of those terms**, so a new row is one addend and one block — the panel already grows and shrinks by 20 px as the faction line comes and goes, so a varying height is this widget's normal behaviour and not a new risk.
- **⚑⚑ THE "RELATIVE SPEED" IS NOT RELATIVE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE JOB.** `main.cpp:976` computes it as `dot(shipState.velocity, targetDirection)` — **the player's own velocity projected at the target, with no reference to the target's**. `TargetInfo::velocity` (`space_world.hpp:404`) has existed since the contact readout and the HUD has never read it. So sitting still while a trader leaves at 90 m/s reads **`+0 m/s`**, while the comment one line above the draw says *"Closing is signed: negative means the gap is opening"* (`game_ui.cpp:478`). **An ETA divided by that number would be wrong in exactly the case an ETA is most wanted** — chasing something that moves — so the ETA cannot be built without fixing it.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE CORRECT FORM IS ALREADY IN THE CODEBASE, IN THE SYSTEM THAT FLIES YOU THERE.** `space_world.cpp:5543` reads `const core::DVec3 targetVelocity = target.isShip ? target.velocity : core::DVec3{};` and `:5547` tests arrival with `length(state.velocity - targetVelocity) < 25.0`. **Autopilot has always known the target moves; the HUD is the one that was never told.** That line is also the idiom to copy rather than invent: `DVec3` zero-initialises (`vec.hpp:80-82`) so subtracting unconditionally would be *correct*, but `velocity` is documented **"ships only"** and the explicit `isShip` guard is what says so at the point of use.
- **The distance shown is a SURFACE distance, not a centre distance.** `main.cpp:883-884` subtracts `target.nav.surfaceRadius` before clamping. **The ETA must divide that same number**, or the panel shows a distance and an ETA that disagree with each other — which is a worse defect than having no ETA at all.
- **A duration formatter already exists, inline and unnamed.** `game_ui.cpp:841` prints the mission deadline as `%d:%02d`. The ETA would be the **second** duration on this HUD, which is exactly when the third parameter of the "five times a widget overflowed its box" lesson applies: **make it a helper the moment there are two**, beside `formatDistance`, `formatSpeed` and `formatClosing` in the same anonymous namespace, and let the mission line use it too.
- **⚑ `decisions/005` SETS THE RANGE THE READOUT HAS TO BE LEGIBLE IN, AND IT IS TIGHTER THAN THE DISTANCES SUGGEST.** *"No time compression… a typical in-system leg lands in the tens of seconds and a long one stays under a few minutes."* Cruise is **5,500 km/s** (`flight.hpp:41`), so even the ~900,000 km hauls the drives keep measuring are **~164 s at cruise**. **Seconds and minutes are the whole design range**; anything past an hour means the player is not meaningfully flying at the thing, and printing `847:19` would be answering a question nobody asked. **This makes the ETA an instrument pointed straight at the number `005` tunes against** — the first surface in the game that shows whether that budget is actually being met.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **The closing speed becomes genuinely relative** — the existing readout is fixed, not merely divided into. (2) **The ETA row is always present while a target is selected and reads `--` when the gap is not closing**, rather than appearing and vanishing: a row that pops in and out as the sign flips would flicker, and *"you are not getting closer"* is worth saying out loud beside a closing speed already coloured as a warning. (3) **The ETA is the shown distance over the shown closing rate — naive, at the current rate.** It is deliberately **not** autopilot's own remaining-to-standoff figure (`autopilotArrivalRange`, `space_world.cpp:5441`), because the panel's two numbers must be consistent with each other and with what the player can see; an ETA that disagreed with the distance beside it would read as a bug. (4) **Format follows the mission deadline**: `m:ss` under an hour, `1h 04m` under a day, `--` beyond.

**A. The number.** `FlightHud` gains `etaSeconds` beside `targetDistanceMeters` and `closingSpeedMetersPerSecond`. `main.cpp` fixes the closing speed to `dot(shipState.velocity - targetVelocity, targetDirection)` with autopilot's `isShip` guard, and derives the ETA from it and the surface distance. A non-positive or non-finite rate yields no ETA.

**B. The row.** `formatDuration` joins the three formatters in `game_ui.cpp`, the mission deadline at `:841` is rewired through it, and `drawTargetReadout` gains an `ETA` row — label left in the dim text colour, value right — with its 20 px in the height sum at `:447`.

**Tests.** **None, and this is named rather than glossed.** The formatter, the panel and `main.cpp`'s HUD fill are all `game/src`, which has no test target — the same honest gap Phase 10's stages A and B carry. Verified by flight, per AGENTS §7. **The one thing a drive can assert as text is the relative-closing fix**, which is a sim-adjacent number rather than a pixel: a stationary player with a departing trader selected must read a **negative** closing speed, and today reads zero.

**Exit.** Flying at a station, the panel counts a falling ETA that agrees with the distance and speed beside it; turning away flips it to `--` and the closing speed to a warning-coloured negative; selecting a trader that is leaving while the player sits still reads a negative closing speed, which it does not today; the mission deadline still reads as it did; all 10 suites green, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No ETA on the map or on a plotted route** — the note named one panel, and a multi-hop route ETA is a different calculation with gate transitions in it. **No autopilot-aware ETA**: modelling the brake curve would make the panel disagree with its own distance, per decision 3. **No migration of `formatDistance`/`formatSpeed`/`formatClosing` into `sol::ui`** — worth doing when something outside the HUD needs them, and nothing does. **No re-tuning of cruise**: this phase *measures* the `005` budget for the first time and deliberately changes none of it, so that the first honest reading is taken against the numbers as they stand.

**⚑ PHASE 11 IS COMPLETE** (`182e56a` spec, `0644280` A, `e97ec64` B). No save change, no test-count change, three files.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE SPEC'S OWN PLAN TO SHARE THE FORMATTER WITH THE MISSION DEADLINE WAS DROPPED, AND THE REASON IS A NUMBER THE SPEC HAD NOT READ.** The diagnosis said the ETA would be the second duration on this HUD and that the mission line at `game_ui.cpp:841` should be rewired through the new helper. Then `init.lua:303` turned out to set a Hold contract's deadline at **1200 + 600 per jump** — so deadlines routinely **run past an hour**, exactly where this ladder switches from `m:ss` to `1h 04m` and drops the seconds. **Rewiring it would have silently changed a signed-off readout with no complaint behind it.** The helper is therefore named `formatEta` rather than `formatDuration`, owns its own two "no answer" states, and has one caller. **A shared helper is only shared if the callers agree about the edges, and the edges are the part an estimate never looks at.**
- **⚑ THE LOAD-BEARING HALF WAS THE HALF NOBODY ASKED FOR.** The note asked for an ETA; the defect was underneath it. **A readout labelled as a closing rate had never read the target's velocity**, so every moving target reported the player's own approach and nothing else. Autopilot has guarded `target.isShip` and flown against a relative velocity since it learned to chase a hauler — **the correct expression was already in the tree, one file away, in the system that acts on the same question.** The HUD had simply never been told. **When a number looks wrong, check whether some other system already computes it correctly before deriving a second one.**
- **A naive ETA falls faster than the clock, and that is right.** Measured: `0:57` at `+1499 km/s`, then `0:14` at `+2349 km/s` **fourteen seconds later** — the rate it divides by is still rising through cruise spool-up, so the estimate improves as the drive spools. It is optimistic early in a leg and pessimistic under braking. That is what "at the current rate" means and it is what keeps the number consistent with the two above it.
- **⚑ AND THE FIRST READING OF THE `005` BUDGET SAYS THE BUDGET IS BEING MET.** `decisions/005` promised legs of "tens of seconds… a long one under a few minutes" and nothing in the game had ever displayed one. An 85,741 km leg to `Lyrioa I` read **57 seconds**. The decision has been honoured for four phases without anyone being able to see it.

**Live proof (one clean run, exit 0 through the pause menu).** Autopilot at the planet `Lyrioa I`: **`85741.3 km` / `+1499 km/s` / `ETA 0:57`** — and 85,741.3 ÷ 1,499 = **57.2 s**, so the row agrees with itself to the second. Fourteen seconds on: `+2349 km/s` / `ETA 0:14`. On arrival: **`22962.4 km` / `+0 m/s` / `ETA --`**. **The relative-closing fix, which is the one thing a screenshot can prove and the console cannot**: with the flight panel reading **`0 m/s`** for the player, an ambient `Shuttle (Solar Navy)` 607,060.1 km out reads **`-61 m/s`** in warning orange, and the console-spawned shuttle at 4,710 m reads **`-214 m/s`**. **Both read `+0 m/s` before this phase**, because the player was the only thing with a velocity in the expression. All 10 suites green in 82 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.

**Known gaps.** **No headless test, and `formatEta`'s ladder is mostly unexecuted.** `game/src` has no test target, so the function rests on the drive — and **the drive only ever exercised the `m:ss` branch and the `--` branch.** The **hour form and the past-a-day ceiling have never run**, in a test or in flight, and the NaN guard has never been reached. That is the weakest part of this phase and it is cheap to close the day anything moves these formatters into `sol::ui`. **The ETA is naive by decision**, so it is wrong in the two places a brake curve lives: optimistic while cruise spools up, pessimistic while autopilot sheds speed into its standoff. **It counts to the surface, not to autopilot's arrival range**, so a leg ends with a few seconds still on the clock while the ship is already parking. **Nothing else in the game shows an ETA** — not the map, not a plotted route, not a mission's flight leg.

### Phase 12 — See Through The Gate: The First Translucency In This Game ✅ (completed 2026-08-20)

**From playtest session 8 (2026-08-19), note 6, the last of that session's six**: *"Jump gates want a semi-transparent membrane you can see yourself fly through."* Picked by the user on **2026-08-20**, after playtest session 10 confirmed Phase 11's ETA row, Phase 10's ellipsis and the Forge's viewport in one sitting. It pairs with Phase 10's stage B (arriving *facing* the playfield) and with 8w (flying physically *through* the aperture): **all three are about making a gate read as a thing you pass through rather than a place you touch.**

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **⚑⚑ THE BACKLOG'S ESTIMATE IS TRUE OF THE FILE IT NAMES AND MISLEADING AS A COST, WHICH MAKES THIS THE FIFTH CONSECUTIVE ITEM THE CODE MOVED.** The board says *"`MeshRenderer` has no blending at all — nothing matches `blendEnable`/`VK_BLEND` in it"*.
