Correct, and it stops one directory too early. **`rhi::BlendMode` (`pipeline.hpp:19-24`) has had `Opaque`, `Alpha` and `Additive` since the RHI was written**, and `GraphicsPipelineDesc` already carries `depthTest`, `depthWrite` and `cullBackFaces` as three independent booleans (`pipeline.hpp:42-45`). **The membrane pipeline is not new RHI; it is the intersection of three descriptions already in the tree** — `ui_renderer.cpp:111` supplies `BlendMode::Alpha`, `particle_renderer.cpp:74-75` supplies `depthTest = true, depthWrite = false`, and `debug_draw_renderer.cpp:70-71` supplies the same pair under a comment that states the reason outright (*"occluded by geometry, but always on top of the sky"*). **Nothing here has to be invented; it has to be combined.**
- **⚑⚑ AND THE THING THAT ACTUALLY DECIDES THE JOB IS THE PASS ORDER: THE SKY DRAWS *AFTER* THE OPAQUE MESHES.** `scene_renderer.cpp:338-344`, under its own comment — *"Sky after opaques (passes only at the far clear)"*. The sky is a full-screen pass that survives only where depth is still at the reversed-Z clear of 0. **A membrane drawn inside the existing mesh loop writes no depth — that is the entire point of `depthWrite = false` — so the sky pass would then pass the depth test across the membrane's own pixels and paint over it.** The membrane would be **invisible against open space and visible only where a hull happened to sit behind it**, which reads as *"blending is broken"* and is in fact *"the pass is in the wrong place"*. **This is the single most expensive thing in this phase and it costs nothing if it is known first.** The membrane draws after the sky and after the star.
- **⚑⚑ THE PUSH-CONSTANT BLOCK IS EXACTLY AT THE GUARANTEED LIMIT AND CANNOT GROW — AND IT DOES NOT NEED TO.** `mesh.frag`'s `Push` is `mat4 mvp` (64 B) plus four `vec4` (64 B) = **exactly 128 bytes**, which is Vulkan's guaranteed `maxPushConstantsSize`. Adding an alpha float would take it to 144 and off the guarantee. **But the block already smuggles three scalars into otherwise-dead `.w` lanes** — `modelColumn0.w` is ambient, `modelColumn1.w` is emissive, `modelColumn2.w` is sun intensity — **and `sunDirection.w` is unused** (`mesh.frag:9`). Alpha rides there for **zero growth**, following a convention the file itself establishes three times rather than a trick invented for this phase.
- **⚑ AND THAT MAKES THE OPAQUE PATH UNCHANGED BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY INSPECTION.** `BlendMode::Alpha` is premultiplied (`src.rgb + dst.rgb * (1 - src.a)`), so the shader must emit `vec4(rgb * a, a)`. **At `a = 1.0` that is bit-identical to today's `vec4(rgb, 1.0)`** — so every existing model keeps its exact output through the same shader, and the claim is provable by reading rather than by A/B-ing a screenshot. The opaque pipeline has blending disabled regardless.
- **⚑⚑ A MEMBRANE CANNOT BE A PART INSIDE `gate.forge`, AND THIS IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN STYLISTIC.** A whole `.forge` cooks to **one** `.smesh`, which is handed to **one** `m_meshRenderer.draw` call (`scene_renderer.cpp:309`) under **one** pipeline. A part cannot opt into different blend state. **So the membrane is its own asset, its own `models.toml` row, and a second instance at the gate's transform** — which is also why the flag belongs on the *model*, not on the part.
- **The def layer already takes optional per-model keys, so the flag is a row and not a C++ change.** `data_defs.cpp:519-525` reads `radius`, `avoid_radius`, `emissive` and `solid` through `optionalFloat`/`optionalBool` and then calls `rejectUnknownKeys` with the literal list. `translucent` and `alpha` are two more of exactly that shape. **This is the seam Phase 9 stage A opened on purpose** — before it, "an authored, cooked mesh had no way of reaching the game at all" — and this is the first phase to spend it on something other than a plain new model.
- **The geometry needs no new primitive.** `revolve` already exists (`forge_doc.cpp:1036-1049`) and takes a profile, segments, `u_tiles` and `cap_ends`. **A disc is a two-point profile swept about the axis**, so the membrane is authorable in `.forge` today, with no Forge work and no new builder.
- **⚑ THE MEMBRANE'S RADIUS IS NOT A TASTE DECISION, FOR THE SAME REASON THE RING'S WAS NOT.** `gate.forge`'s own header states it: the crossing test uses `kGateRadiusMeters = 70`, the ring's inner radius is `major_radius - tube_radius` = `78 - 8` = **70**, and stage D's whole point was that *"what is drawn is now what is tested"*. **The membrane must be 70 m and must say why in the file**, or the game grows a second drawn aperture that disagrees with the tested one — re-opening by hand the exact defect stage D closed.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE LIGHTING FALLS OUT WRONG UNLESS THE MEMBRANE IS EMISSIVE, WHICH READING THE TRANSFORM IS WHAT TELLS YOU.** Gates are placed with `facingRotation(axis)` where `axis` is the outward radial from the hub (`space_world.cpp:2953-2960`), and `facingRotation` takes the model's **+Z** onto that axis. A disc authored in the XY plane therefore has its **+Z normal pointing directly away from the star**, which sits at the hub. Lambert is `max(dot(normal, toSun), 0)` and `toSun` is `-axis`, so the outward face evaluates to **exactly zero** and the inward face to **exactly one**. **The side you approach on the way *out* of a system is a pure black disc.** Emissive is therefore load-bearing rather than polish — and it is already a def key and already a push-constant lane, so it costs a number.
- **The spawn is one call site.** `space_world.cpp:2960` is the only place a gate becomes drawable: `addStatic(gate.position, {1,1,1}, gateModel, facingRotation(axis))`. A second `addStatic` beside it with the membrane model and the identical transform is the whole of the spawn change. **`solid = false` is what keeps it out of the collision and avoidance sets** — `models.toml`'s gate row already documents that exact mechanism, so the membrane inherits a precedent instead of needing a new exclusion.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **A second pipeline in `MeshRenderer`, not a reuse of the particle path.** The particle pass is additive point-sprites with its own vertex format; a membrane is a lit, textured, indexed mesh that wants to be *dimmer* than what is behind it, which is alpha, not addition. Sharing `MeshRenderer`'s layout, shaders and descriptor set means the second pipeline is a `GraphicsPipelineDesc` with **three fields changed**. (2) **Translucency is a property of the MODEL, declared in `models.toml`, not of the instance.** The renderer partitions the instance list by the catalog flag. This costs nothing now and means the *second* translucent thing in this game is a def row with no C++ at all. (3) **The membrane draws after the sky and the star, before the particles** — after the sky because of the erase-trap above, before the particles so that exhaust glow reads as being *in front of* the film rather than behind it. (4) **No depth sorting among translucent instances.** The only translucent object in the game is one flat disc per gate, gates are ~100,000 km apart, and two of them cannot meaningfully overlap on screen. Sorting is the right answer the day a second translucent *kind* exists; adding it now would be machinery guarding a case that cannot occur. **Named as a known gap rather than left to be discovered.** (5) **`cullBackFaces = false` on the translucent pipeline.** You fly *through* this thing, so it is seen from both sides by design, and a one-sided membrane would vanish the instant you crossed it. (6) **The membrane does not move, pulse or scroll in this phase.** The note asks to *see yourself fly through it*; animation is a separate, arguable change and would make the first translucency's verification a judgement about taste rather than about correctness.

**A. The pipeline and the pass.** `MeshRenderer` gains a second `VkPipeline` built from the same layout and shader modules with `blendMode = Alpha`, `depthWrite = false`, `cullBackFaces = false`, exposed as `bindTranslucent()` beside `bind()`; `reloadPipeline` rebuilds both. `draw` takes an `alpha` parameter defaulting to `1.0f` and writes it to `sunDirection.w`; `mesh.frag` multiplies the emitted rgb by it and emits it as the alpha channel. `scene_renderer.cpp` partitions `instances` by the catalog's `translucent` flag — opaque drawn exactly where they are now, translucent collected into a scratch vector and drawn in a second bound block placed after `drawStar` and before the particle zone, under its own `gpu.translucent` profiler zone.

**B. The asset, the row and the spawn.** `assets/meshes/gate_membrane.forge` — a `revolve` disc of radius **70**, with a header comment carrying the same "what is drawn is what is tested" reasoning `gate.forge` uses, so the number cannot drift silently. `models.toml` gains a `gate_membrane` row with `translucent = true`, an `alpha`, an `emissive`, `solid = false` and a radius; `data_defs.cpp` learns `translucent` and `alpha` through the existing `optionalBool`/`optionalFloat` pair and both are added to `rejectUnknownKeys`. `space_world.cpp:2960` gains a second `addStatic` at the identical transform.

**Tests.** **`assets.unit` gains coverage of the two new def keys** — that `translucent`/`alpha` parse, that they default to opaque/1.0 when absent, and that an out-of-range alpha is rejected — because `data_defs.cpp` is library code with an existing suite, unlike the renderer. **`geometry.unit` gains the membrane's build**: that `gate_membrane.forge` parses and builds, and that its outermost ring of vertices sits at **exactly 70 m**, which is the assertion that stops the drawn aperture drifting from `kGateRadiusMeters`. **The pipeline itself gets no unit test and that is named, not glossed**: `MeshRenderer` needs a device, and AGENTS §7 keeps device-dependent code on live verification. `dev-gpu`'s `game.smoke` covers "it still starts and draws".

**Exit.** Flying at a gate, the aperture carries a visible film: the ring and the stars behind it are still readable *through* it, it is occluded by anything in front of it, it does not occlude the gate's own armature, and **it is visible from both sides including the outward face that lambert alone would render black**. Flying through it, the film passes over the camera and the ship comes out the far side with the membrane now behind. A screenshot A/B against `dev` shows **no change to any other model's pixels**. All 11 suites green (two of them with new tests), `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No animation, scroll, ripple or crossing flash** — decision 6. **No depth sorting** — decision 4, and it is a real gap the moment a second translucent kind lands. **No translucency on anything else**: no cockpit glass, no shields, no engine trails; this phase builds the road and drives one car down it. **No change to the crossing mechanic** — 8w's aperture test, its radius and its trigger are untouched, and the membrane is deliberately non-solid so it cannot affect flight. **No LOD or fade-with-distance**, which is stage F's problem and would author dead data today. **No second texture**: the membrane uses an existing cooked texture, because introducing a new one would drag in the PNG-writer-vs-procedural fork that stage G is deliberately holding open.

**⚑ PHASE 12 IS COMPLETE** (`c602f89` spec, `70f23b7` A, `fd7d051` B). No save change, no sim change. `geometry.unit` 93 → **94**, `assets.unit` +1.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE SPEC'S LIGHTING FINDING WAS RIGHT IN SHAPE AND WRONG IN ITS CONCLUSION, AND ONLY WORKING THE SIGN THROUGH CAUGHT IT.** The spec predicted the face you approach on the way *out* would be **lambert-black**, and made emissive load-bearing on that basis. The shape of the finding holds: the lane axis points at the hub, the star sits on it, so the disc meets the sunlight **dead-on** and lambert is all-or-nothing rather than shading across the surface. But the conclusion was wrong twice over. **The profile's normal is −Z**, which `facingRotation` maps back toward the hub — so the film is **lit**, not black. And **the fragment shader does not flip a normal for back faces**, so both faces take the *same* dot product: it is **all-or-nothing across the whole disc at once**, never lit on one side and dark on the other. Wind the profile the other way and the membrane goes black from every angle simultaneously. **The emissive floor is insurance against the winding, not a fix for a dark face**, and `geometry.unit` now asserts the sign so it cannot drift. **This is the project's own "a percentage without a sign is half a measurement" lesson arriving in a new place: the spec reasoned correctly about the geometry and then guessed the sign.**
- **⚑⚑ THE PASS-ORDER TRAP WAS REAL AND COST NOTHING, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR READING FIRST.** The sky draws after the opaque meshes and survives wherever depth is still at the reversed-Z clear. A membrane recorded in the opaque block writes no depth by design and would have been **painted over by the sky** — visible only where a hull sat behind it, i.e. invisible against open space, which reads as broken blending rather than as a misplaced pass. It was found by reading `scene_renderer.cpp:338` before writing a line, and the live overlay now shows `gpu.translucent` sitting exactly between `gpu.sky` and `gpu.particles`.
- **⚑ THE BACKLOG'S "MeshRenderer HAS NO BLENDING" WAS TRUE OF THAT FILE AND MISLEADING AS A COST — THE FIFTH ITEM RUNNING.** `rhi::BlendMode` has had `Alpha` and `Additive` since the RHI was written and `depthTest`/`depthWrite`/`cullBackFaces` are already independent fields, so the translucent pipeline is **three fields moved** on a desc that already existed, sharing shaders, layout and descriptor sets. Every ingredient was already in use somewhere — `Alpha` in the UI pass, the depth pair in the particle and debug passes.
- **⚑ AND THE PUSH BLOCK NEEDED NO ROOM MADE FOR IT.** `mesh.frag`'s constants are **exactly 128 bytes**, the guaranteed minimum, with a `static_assert` already guarding it — but the block already smuggles ambient, emissive and sun intensity into dead `.w` lanes and **`sunDirection.w` was free**. Alpha rides there, and because the shader premultiplies, **`alpha = 1.0` is the exact identity** — every opaque model's output is unchanged by construction rather than by inspection.
- **⚑ A `revolve` HAD NEVER BEEN USED BY A SHIPPED ASSET, AND ADDING ONE TO THE FOUR COMMITTED-ASSET LOOPS WAS FREE COVERAGE.** `gate_membrane.forge` joins the byte-for-byte round-trip, the part-range partition, the zero-distance-move and the bake-invariance tests, so all four met a revolve for the first time and all four passed unchanged.

**Live proof (three drives, all clean).** **The membrane renders:** from 500 m the aperture carries a visible radial film under `GATE: Lyrioa 500 m`, and the fact that it is visible **against open space at all** is itself the proof the pass survives the sky. **Translucency measured rather than eyeballed, via a def-only A/B** (`alpha = 0.00` is the analytic no-op, so it photographs exactly what is behind the film with no rebuild): the two frames are **bit-identical outside the gate — mean |ΔLum| = 0.000 over 15,000 px**, so the membrane changes only the aperture. Inside it, sampling an annulus that excludes both the HUD reticle (drawn after tonemapping) and the opaque ring, **stars behind the film stay visible and dimmed: 123.7 → 107.8, 113.1 → 98.1, 98.9 → 88.6**, with a **behind-vs-through correlation of 0.7386 where an opaque disc would read ~0**. **It does not block:** flying at it logs `jumping: Queis -> Lyrioa` / `Arrived in 'Lyrioa'` at **HULL 100%**, and **`sim.avoidance n=11` / `sim.collision.build n=11` are unchanged** with four membranes in the world — the named instrument for collision-set membership, which would read 15 if they had become solid. `sol.models()` reports `#5 gate_membrane: gate_membrane/hull r70 a70 [pass-through] [lit] [film a0.30]`, and `7 model(s)`. All **11** suites green in ~82 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.

**Known gaps.** **No depth sorting among translucent instances** — decision 4, correct today because one flat disc per gate cannot overlap another, and the first thing to fix the day a second translucent kind exists. **The membrane is static**: no scroll, no ripple, no reaction to a crossing, so it reads as a film rather than as a field. **It is a zero-thickness disc**, so it vanishes exactly edge-on — unreachable in normal flight because a gate is always approached along its lane, but real. **The renderer half has no headless test**, per AGENTS §7: `MeshRenderer` needs a device, so the pipeline, the pass order and the premultiplied identity rest on the drives above and on `dev-gpu`'s smoke. **The `alpha = 1.0` identity is argued from the shader rather than pinned by a golden image** — an A/B against `dev` cropped to a hull would close it and was not run, because the alpha-0 A/B already showed nothing outside the aperture moves. **Only the inward face has been seen by anyone**: arrival always parks the player between the gate and the hub, and crossing the aperture jumps you, so photographing the outward face needs a deliberate fly-around nobody has done.

### Phase 13 — A Galaxy That Adds Up: Coherent Worlds, Factions With Character, And A Sky With Somebody In It ✅ (completed 2026-08-20)

**From playtest session 8 (2026-08-19), notes 4 and 5 — the last two on the board.** Note 4: *"Faction personalities, leading to specialised markets and services."* Note 5: *"World generation should be coherent (no mining station where there is no rock), and ships staggered so any start system feels alive."* Picked by the user on **2026-08-20**. The backlog said from the day they were taken that they *"should be scoped together, not separately"*, and reading the code confirms why: **all three halves meet at one function, `pickArchetype`, or at one function beside it.**

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **⚑⚑ THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS `producesFrom` AND `rhi::BlendMode` BEFORE IT: EVERY FACT THIS PHASE NEEDS IS ALREADY AUTHORED, AND GENERATION SIMPLY NEVER READS IT.** `pickArchetype` (`universe.cpp:326`) is `(const GalaxyParams&, core::Rng&, Region)` — **region and nothing else**. It has no faction, no rock, no system. That is the whole of both notes: not missing information, an argument list that never asked for it.
- **⚑⚑ AND `StationDef` ALREADY STATES NOTE 5(a)'s RULE WORD FOR WORD, AS A COMMENT ON A FIELD THAT SHIPS.** `data_defs.hpp:334-339`: `producesFrom`, *"'field' means it comes out of the ground: a Mining Outpost draws ore from the asteroid fields in its own system, **so a system with no rock supports no mine**."* `stations.toml:43` sets `produces_from = "field"` on `sol.station_mine` and on nothing else. **The data already declares which archetype needs rock; the generator has never consulted it, and the downstream behaviour is already correct and tested** (`mining_tests.cpp:122`: *"an outpost with no rock under it produces nothing, which is the point"*). This is a placement decision with a pre-existing, authored predicate.
- **⚑ THE BLAST RADIUS IS ONE ARCHETYPE OUT OF FOUR, WHICH IS SMALLER THAN THE NOTE SOUNDS.** `stations.toml` holds exactly four archetypes — Agricultural, Mining Outpost, Refinery, Fabricator Yard — and only the mine names `produces_from = "field"`. Meanwhile `MiningParams::fieldCount` (`mining.hpp:105`) is `{{0,1},{1,2},{1,3}}` by tier, so **only Core systems can have zero fields**, and the mine's own `weight_core = 0.5` is exactly the weight that puts a mine in a rockless core system. **The defect has a precise home: core systems that rolled zero fields.**
- **⚑⚑ THE ORDERING PROBLEM THE BACKLOG CALLED "A LAYERING QUESTION" IS REAL, BUT IT IS NOT A DATA CYCLE — IT IS CODE OWNERSHIP, AND THE DATA SIDE IS ACYCLIC.** `MiningSim::fieldsFor` (`mining.cpp:93`) draws from `core::Rng rng(spec.seed, kFieldStream)` where `kFieldStream = 501`, and reads only `spec.seed`, `spec.region` and `MiningParams`. **It does not read stations, and nothing about a field depends on what was built in the system.** So the rock answer is computable exactly where the station decision is made — it is merely a *member of `MiningSim`*, and `MiningSim::initialize` takes an already-built `Galaxy`.
- **⚑ AND THE CALL-SITE ORDER IS A PURE REORDERING, VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `SpaceWorld::generateUniverse` builds `m_galaxyParams`, calls `generateGalaxy` (`space_world.cpp:213`), *then* fills `m_commodityIds` (`:219-222`); `initializeMining` (`:1433`) later builds `m_miningParams.ores` from `m_commodityIds` and the defs. **`m_miningParams` depends on the defs and on nothing in the galaxy**, so it can be built before `generateGalaxy` with no data moving backwards. That is the only sequencing change stage A needs.
- **⚑⚑ THE FACTION IS ALREADY ASSIGNED WHEN STATIONS ARE PLACED, SO NOTE 4 NEEDS NO REORDERING AT ALL.** `generateGalaxy` runs `claimTerritory` at **`:450`** and the `populateSystem` loop at **`:461`**. `SystemSpec::factionIndex` is therefore final before a single station exists, and `populateSystem` already receives the whole `SystemSpec`. **Note 4 is a signature change and a weight multiply, not a restructuring of generation.**
- **⚑⚑ AND THE FACTION DESCRIPTIONS ALREADY ASSERT THE PERSONALITIES THE GENERATOR IGNORES — THE SAME DEFECT AS `producesFrom`, ONE FILE OVER.** `factions.toml` says Ironstar Hegemony is an *"authoritarian **industrial** bloc"*, the Freight Guild are *"traders and haulers; **owns most stations***", the Frontier Compact are *"**settler** militias"*, the Helios Ascendancy a *"**technocratic** enclave"*. **`FactionDef` carries `aggression`, `forgiveness`, three ship rosters and a relations matrix — and not one economic key.** Five factions have had written characters since 8b and the galaxy has never built differently for any of them. Note 4 is: give that prose a number.
- **⚑⚑ NOTE 5(b) IS THE SIXTH CONSECUTIVE ESTIMATE THE CODE MOVED, AND IT MOVED THE CHEAP WAY BY A LARGE MARGIN — EVERY INGREDIENT IS BUILT AND WIRED.** `PilotRole::Trader` **already exists** (`space_world.hpp:124`). `PilotState::Travel` **already exists** and is documented as *"the same steerTravel the player's autopilot flies"* (`:134-139`). `FactionDef::shipsTrader` **already exists and is populated for all seven factions**. `spawnAmbientPilots` already has a `spawnWing(faction, roster, role, count, anchor, spread)` lambda (`space_world.cpp:2166`) — **it is simply never called with `shipsTrader` and `PilotRole::Trader`.** And decisively, **`init.lua`'s `pilot_think` already has a `role == "trader"` branch with an idle behaviour** (`:115-127`), which runs `nextLeg(ship, traderLegs, traderLeg)` over a two-leg circuit authored as *"planet-side run"* and *"back to the station approach"* (`:72-75`). **A civilian trader spawned in the ambient wing flies a station circuit with ZERO Lua changes and zero new steering.**
- **⚑ WHICH ALSO MEANS 5(b) CANNOT DISTURB THE ECONOMY, AND THAT IS THE POINT RATHER THAN A LUCKY BREAK.** `spawnAmbientPilots` is called from `loadSystem` (`:3279`) for the **current system only**, so it spawns where the player is **by construction** — which is exactly the board's own framing (*"weighting presence to where the player actually is, not re-tuning the stagger"*). These are scenery civilians, **not `TraderPuppet`s**: a puppet is a body for a coarse `EconomyTrader` and carries its cargo, route and detain/attrition hooks. Ambient civilians carry none of that and move no goods, so `economy_shipped_rates_hold_a_steady_state` cannot see them.
- **⚑ THE START SYSTEM IS "THE FIRST CORE SYSTEM WITH A STATION", BY INDEX SCAN** (`space_world.cpp:6813-6820`) — not a designed place. Combined with `kPatrolsPerRegion = {3,2,1}` (`:2200`) and everything anchored on `spec.stations[0].position` (`:2194`), a new pilot's sky is **three interceptors and nothing else**, with the nearest trader body ~900,000 km out. **The start system does not feel dead because too little was generated; it feels dead because everything generated near the player is military.**
- **⚑ AND ONE PRE-EXISTING ODDITY FOUND WHILE READING, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REDISCOVERED AS A BUG.** System seeds derive as `Rng(params.seed, kStreamContents + i)` (`:446`) with `kStreamContents = 4` and `kStreamClans = 5`, so **system 1's seed stream is the clan stream**. It is deterministic and harmless — the two consume differently — but it is a stream collision rather than a design. **Explicitly out of scope**: fixing it reshuffles every galaxy for no player-visible gain, and this phase already reshuffles enough.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **The rock rule gets exactly ONE definition.** `fieldCountFor(const SystemSpec&, const MiningParams&)` becomes a pure function in `mining.hpp`, and `MiningSim::fieldsFor` is rewritten to call it — so the count generation consults and the count mining produces cannot drift. Copying `fieldCount[3][2]` into `GalaxyParams` was considered and **rejected**: it is the "second implementation" trap this project already recorded for dev levers, and two structs holding the same tuning is how it starts. (2) **`generateGalaxy` gains an optional `const MiningParams*`, defaulting to `nullptr`**, with `universe.hpp` **forward-declaring** the type and `universe.cpp` including `mining.hpp`. There is no header cycle, and `nullptr` means "no coherence rule", so **every existing call site and every existing test compiles and passes unchanged** — the new behaviour is opt-in and the game opts in. (3) **Faction character is authored data, not a hardcoded table.** `FactionDef` gains `station_bias`, a list of `"<station_id>:<multiplier>"` strings — the exact shape `relations`, `produces` and `feedstock` already use, so the parser gains a pattern rather than an idea. A faction with no bias behaves exactly as today. (4) **Bias multiplies the region weight; it does not replace it.** A faction's character *tilts* a distribution that region tuning already shaped, so the economy's regional logic (ore in the fringe, factories in the core) survives a faction's preferences. A multiplier of 0 is a legal, deliberate "never". (5) **The coherence rule is a veto, not a re-roll.** A field-requiring archetype's weight goes to zero in a rockless system, and the roll proceeds over what remains — so a rockless system builds something else rather than building nothing, and no system loses a station it would have had. (6) **Ambient civilians are scenery and are never promoted to economy actors.** They carry no cargo, move no goods and are not `TraderPuppet`s. The day the coarse economy wants intra-system hauls is a different phase; conflating the two here would put untested ships into a steady state 8g tuned from both directions. (7) **Save version bumps to v15 with no migration**, this project's precedent. Changing archetype selection changes every system in the galaxy, so a v14 save describes a world that no longer exists.

**A. Coherence: no mine where there is no rock.** `fieldCountFor` is extracted in `mining.cpp` and `fieldsFor` reimplemented on it. `StationRule` (`universe.hpp:44`) gains `bool requiresField = false`, filled in `generateUniverse` from `station.producesFrom == "field"`. `generateGalaxy` takes the optional `MiningParams*` and `populateSystem` computes its system's field count once, before the station loop; `pickArchetype` gains that count and zeroes the weight of any rule with `requiresField` when it is zero — falling back to the unfiltered roll if that would leave no weight at all, so a degenerate ruleset still produces a station. `initializeMining`'s ore-list construction is hoisted above `generateGalaxy` in `generateUniverse`.

**B. Character: a faction builds what it is.** `FactionDef` gains `std::vector<std::string> stationBias` parsed from `station_bias` in `factions.toml`, resolved in `generateUniverse` against the station def order into `GalaxyParams::factionStationBias` — `[faction][archetype]`, defaulting to 1.0 and left empty when no faction authors a bias. `pickArchetype` gains the system's `factionIndex` and multiplies each rule's region weight by the bias before rolling; `kNoFaction` and out-of-range indices take 1.0. Biases are then authored to match the descriptions already in the file: **Ironstar Hegemony** toward Fabricator Yard and Refinery, **Frontier Compact** toward Agricultural and Mining, **Freight Guild** broad and flat with a light Agricultural lean, **Helios Ascendancy** toward Fabricator, **Solar Navy** left neutral so an unbiased faction stays covered by a shipped asset.

**C. Presence: somebody else in the sky.** `spawnAmbientPilots` gains a civilian wing — `spawnWing(owner, faction.shipsTrader, PilotRole::Trader, count, anchor, spread)` — sized by region so the civilized core is busier than the fringe, anchored off the station like the patrols and spread far enough not to read as a formation. Pirate-held systems get none: a clan system's traffic is its raiders, which it already spawns. **No Lua change** — `pilot_think`'s existing `role == "trader"` branch flies them.

**Tests.** **`sim.unit`, `universe_tests`**: a galaxy generated with `MiningParams` never places a `requiresField` archetype in a system whose field count is zero (and the same galaxy generated with `nullptr` still can, which is what proves the rule is doing the work); a faction with a zero bias on an archetype never receives one while its neighbours do; generation stays deterministic for a fixed seed; and the fallback still yields a station when every archetype requires a field. **`sim.unit`, `mining_tests`**: `fieldCountFor` equals `fieldsFor(...).size()` for every system in a generated galaxy — the assertion that pins the one-definition decision. **`assets.unit`**: `station_bias` parses, defaults to empty, survives an unknown station id as a warning rather than an error (a mod may remove an archetype), and rejects a malformed entry. **The ambient civilian wing gets no headless test and that is named**: it needs defs, a registry and a loaded system, and its behaviour is Lua's; it rests on the live drive below.

**Exit.** A console probe over a generated galaxy reports **zero mining outposts in systems with zero asteroid fields**, against a non-zero count on `dev`. Two adjacent systems held by different factions show visibly different station mixes, matching the biases authored in `factions.toml`. Flying a fresh game, the start system has **civilian ships moving near the station** in addition to its patrols, and they fly the trader circuit rather than sitting still. A `sim.unit` economy run still holds its steady state. Saves at **v15**; a v14 save is rejected cleanly. All 11 suites green, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No new station archetypes** — the four in `stations.toml` are what factions get to differ over; adding a fifth is content work with its own economy tuning. **No specialised *services*** — note 4's phrase covers markets and services, and services (refit, refuel, repair) are a station-screen feature, not a generation one; this phase makes a faction's *stations* differ, and what a station *offers* stays for a later item. **No faction-specific hulls** — all seven factions currently fly `sol.interceptor` for both patrol and raider roles, which is a ship-asset gap, not a generation one. **No change to the coarse economy, its trader count or its routing** — decision 6. **No intra-system economy legs.** **No change to `claimTerritory`, region assignment or the gate graph.** **No fix for the `kStreamContents + i` stream collision** — deterministic, harmless, and fixing it reshuffles every galaxy for nothing a player can see. **No save migration** — decision 7, and the project's precedent.

**⚑ PHASE 13 IS COMPLETE** (`43a97e5` spec, `6d26789` A, `2934b2e` B, `eef2ba2` C). Save **v14 → v15**, no migration. `sim.unit` 166 → **172**, `assets.unit` unchanged at **38** (the existing faction test extended). No renderer change.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE HEADLINE HELD THREE TIMES IN ONE PHASE, AND IT IS THE SAME DEFECT WEARING THREE HATS: THE FACT WAS ALREADY AUTHORED AND GENERATION NEVER READ IT.** (1) `StationDef::producesFrom` has stated note 5(a)'s rule **in its own comment since 8g** — *"a system with no rock supports no mine"* — and `stations.toml` sets it on the Mining Outpost alone. (2) `factions.toml` has called the Hegemony an *"authoritarian **industrial** bloc"* and the Compact *"**settler** militias"* **since 8b**, and `FactionDef` carried aggression, forgiveness, three ship rosters and **not one economic key**. (3) Note 5(b)'s entire mechanism — `PilotRole::Trader`, `PilotState::Travel`, `FactionDef::shipsTrader`, `spawnWing`, and **`init.lua`'s `role == "trader"` branch flying a two-leg station circuit** — was built, populated and wired, and the ambient wing simply never spawned a civilian. **This is the sixth consecutive item whose written estimate the code moved, and the first where it moved three separate halves the same way.**
- **⚑⚑ AND THE HONEST NUMBER IS SMALLER THAN THE NOTE, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX.** The coherence defect is real, was reachable, and is now impossible — but measured live at the shipped tuning it was **one station in 124**. Only ~12% of systems land in the Core, only a Core system can roll zero fields (`fieldCount[0] = {0,1}`), and only **3 of 80** systems came out rockless. **The note's premise was correct and its practical incidence is under 1%.** Recorded rather than dressed up: a correctness fix that closes a class of nonsense forever is worth shipping at any incidence, but nobody should expect to *see* it.
- **⚑⚑ THE ONE-DEFINITION DECISION FAILED ON ITS FIRST ATTEMPT, IN A WAY WORTH KEEPING.** The spec said `fieldsFor` would *call* `fieldCountFor`, and it cannot: `fieldCountFor` builds its own `Rng` and returns a number, while `fieldsFor` has to **advance its own stream past that same draw** and keep going into the fields. The first implementation duplicated the `countInRange` expression and papered over it with an assert — **which is exactly the "two answers to one question" the decision existed to prevent**, now with a runtime check instead of a compile-time guarantee. The fix is `drawFieldCount(rng, spec, params)`, which takes the **rng** rather than the seed. **The general form: a shared rule that sits MID-STREAM must share the DRAW, not the ANSWER.**
- **⚑⚑ THE EXPENSIVE ORDERING TRAP WAS ON THE LOAD PATH, NOT THE ONE THE SPEC LOOKED AT.** The spec correctly found that `m_miningParams` must be built before `generateGalaxy` in `generateUniverse`. It did not look at the **second** `generateGalaxy`, in `loadFrom` — where the galaxy is regenerated from the saved seed and `initializeMining()` runs **far below it**. Left alone, a *load* would have regenerated the galaxy under a different placement rule than the one that created the save: same seed, different world, and nothing would have reported an error. **A new INPUT to a generator has to be traced to every call site of that generator, not to the one the feature was written in.**
- **⚑ A PER-SYSTEM RNG STREAM MAKES AN UNBIASED FACTION BIT-IDENTICAL, BY CONSTRUCTION.** `populateSystem` draws from `Rng(systems[i].seed, kStreamContents)`, so biasing one faction cannot perturb another faction's systems. Measured: with four majors newly biased, the deliberately-neutral Solar Navy's 24 stations came back at **exactly** `21% 4% 33% 42%`, unchanged. The control was free and it is a property of the design rather than luck.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE FINDING THE PHASE DID NOT GO LOOKING FOR: THE TERRITORY SPLIT IS TOO LOPSIDED FOR THE FEATURE IT NOW CARRIES.** Measured at seed 1701: **Freight Guild holds 44 stations, Solar Navy 24, Helios Ascendancy 18 — and Ironstar Hegemony 2, Frontier Compact 3.** So two of the five majors have essentially nowhere to express a character, and their mixes are small-sample noise rather than personality. `claimTerritory`'s greedy farthest-point spread predates this phase and rebalancing it was explicitly out of scope, but **note 4's payoff is capped by it**, and that is now a measured number rather than a suspicion. **This is the natural next item if faction character is to be worth more than a data key.**
- **⚑ ONE PRE-EXISTING STREAM COLLISION, RECORDED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED.** System seeds derive as `Rng(params.seed, kStreamContents + i)` with `kStreamContents = 4` and `kStreamClans = 5`, so **system 1's seed stream is the clan stream**. Deterministic and harmless — the two consume differently — but it is an accident rather than a design. Fixing it reshuffles every galaxy for nothing a player can see.

**Live proof (`sol.worldgen()`, seed 1701, the shipped defs).** **⚑ The probe generates its own counterfactual** — the same params with the rock rule off — so the A/B needs no second binary and no `git checkout dev`, which is the Phase 12 lesson applied to a non-visual change. **Coherence:** `80 systems, 3 with no rock; 124 stations; 0 extractor(s) sited without rock (rule off: 1 of 124)`. **Character**, columns agri/mine/refinery/factory, before → after: **Freight Guild** (44) `23 25 43 9` → `34 16 34 16`; **Helios Ascendancy** (18) `28 22 28 22` → `28 0 22 50`; **Frontier Compact** (3) `0 0 0 100` → `67 0 0 33`; **Solar Navy** (24) `21 4 33 42` → **unchanged**, as designed. Each moves the way its own description reads. **Presence:** a fresh game in Lyrioa goes **15 → 19 entities**, and `sol.contacts` shows **two Freighters and two Shuttles at 1–6 km** beside the three Interceptors, with the two coarse puppets still out at 379,322 km and 907,303 km. **They fly**: across ~2 s two of them closed 5 → 4 km and 6 → 5 km and the ordering reshuffled. `sim.pilots n=9`; the collision and avoidance sets go **n=11 → n=15**, which is correct — a civilian is a real ship you can fly into. All **11** suites green in ~81 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.

**Known gaps.** **Pirate clans have no `station_bias`** and fall through at 1.0 — their faction indices are `factionCount + clan index` and the clan count does not exist until the galaxy is generated, so a row for them cannot be authored in `factions.toml` at all. They hold ~30 stations at seed 1701, so this is not a rounding error; it needs a template-indexed table, not a faction-indexed one. **The territory imbalance above caps note 4's payoff** and is the phase's own recommended follow-up. **Civilians are scenery and stay scenery**: they carry no cargo, move no goods, and their deaths cost the economy nothing, so shooting one is consequence-free in a way a player may notice before the economy does. **Whether a civilian answers an 8s hail with a market tip is untested** — it is a `PilotRole::Trader` pilot, so it plausibly does, and nobody has pressed `Y` at one. **The ambient wing has no headless test**, per AGENTS §7: it needs defs, a registry and a loaded system, and its behaviour is Lua's. **The coherence rule's incidence is under 1% at the shipped tuning**, so it is insurance rather than a visible change. **No faction-specific hulls** — all seven factions still fly `sol.interceptor` as both patrol and raider, so a faction's *character* is now visible in what it builds and still invisible in what it flies.

### Phase 14 — A Number You Can Read: What A Dragged Point Writes Down ✅ (completed 2026-08-20)

**From playtest session 11 (2026-08-20), and it is the first note this project has ever received as a DIFF rather than as a sentence.** The user opened the Forge, dragged the shared apex of the cockpit's four cowl triangles, and saved — discharging the one thing the backlog had been owed since session 10 (*"moved things, didn't save"*). The write-back works. **What it wrote is the note**: an authored `p2 = [0.0, 0.26, -6.95]` came back as `p2 = [0.009169150493107736, 0.3069186387490481, -7.117375393866678]`.

**⚑ What the same diff PROVES, recorded first because it closes a standing gap rather than opening one.** Exactly **four lines changed**, all of them the one shared corner, in a 141-point file. `p0`, `p1`, every uv, every other part and every comment came back **byte-identical**. That is three separate E1/E2 mechanisms confirmed on a real human drag rather than on a test: the **relative write** (an untouched value is not rewritten as a float round trip of itself), **position-adjacency dedup** (one drag moved all four `flat_triangle` parts that name that apex, which is what makes a welded model editable at all), and the **comment-preserving writer**. **The write-back is no longer unverified.**

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **⚑⚑ THE WRITER IS NOT THE BUG, AND SAYING SO PRECISELY IS WHAT DECIDES THE PHASE.** `appendNumber` (`forge_doc.cpp:212`) already searches precisions 1..17 for the **shortest text that reads back as exactly this double**, and already refuses exponent forms because *"the round numbers an author is MOST likely to type are exactly the ones a naive shortest-form writer mangles."* Given a double that genuinely needs 16 digits it prints 16 digits, correctly. **The 17 digits are not a formatting failure — they are an honest report of a number that arrived carrying 17 digits.**
- **⚑⚑ THE NUMBER ARRIVES THAT WAY IN `forgeMovePoint`, WHICH ADDS A DOUBLE DELTA TO A STORED DOUBLE AND STORES THE FULL-PRECISION SUM** (`forge_doc.cpp:1498-1553`, all four write kinds). Nothing rounds anywhere on the path. The delta itself came from a mouse ray cast against **float** geometry, so digits 9 through 17 are the decimal expansion of a binary value carrying no information — the drag was never that precise and could not have been.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE ARGUMENT FOR FIXING IT IS ALREADY WRITTEN IN THIS FILE, ONE FUNCTION BELOW, APPLIED TO A DIFFERENT CASE.** `appendMeshNumber` (`forge_doc.cpp:235-241`) exists for exactly this reason on **baked** vertices: *"the extra digits a double writer emits are not precision — they are the decimal expansion of a binary value that carries no more information. At 960 vertices and eight numbers each, the difference is a file a person can scan against one they cannot."* **Seventh consecutive item where the reasoning the phase needs was already authored in the repo and simply not applied here.** The bake path solved this; the drag path never did.
- **⚑⚑ THE FIX CANNOT LIVE IN THE WRITER, AND THERE ARE TWO INDEPENDENT REASONS — EITHER ALONE IS DECISIVE.** (1) **The doc and the file would disagree.** The Forge holds the `ForgeDoc` in memory and saves after every edit; rounding only the text means the next drag adds its delta to the *unrounded* doc value, so an edit session accumulates at full precision while the file shows something else, and re-opening the file yields a different document than the one on screen. **That is the E1 lossy-round-trip defect exactly, arriving from a third direction.** (2) **The writer must never edit the author's own numbers.** `.forge` is hand-editable by design — that is the whole reason it is text and the whole reason the comment-preserving writer was built — so a writer that rewrites a value nobody dragged is the same class of unasked-for edit as dropping the comments was. **The rounding belongs at the MOVE, on the value stored in the document.**
- **⚑⚑ THE QUANTUM IS MEASURED RATHER THAN CHOSEN. Across the four authored assets — `cockpit`, `ship`, `station`, `gate` — every number has at most THREE decimal places**: 508 at one, 145 at two, 4 at three, **zero beyond**. So a **0.1 mm (1e-4 m) grid reproduces every authored value in the repo exactly, with a decimal place to spare**, and there is no shipped number it would disturb. The baked asteroid is not in that count and is handled separately below.
- **⚑ ONLY POSITIONS ARE AFFECTED, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `forgeMovePoint`'s four write kinds — `Vertex`, `BeamEnd`, `BoxCorner`, `BakedVertex` — write `from`/`to`/`center`/`size`/`p0..p2`/baked vertex positions and **nothing else**. `appendNumber`'s other callers (`smoothAngleDegrees`, scalars, uvs, revolve profiles at `:840`, `:890`, `:903`, `:915`) are never on a drag path, so uv coordinates and smoothing angles keep full precision and stay untouched by this phase.
- **⚑⚑ THE BOX IS THE ONE HARD CASE, AND IT IS A GENUINE THREE-WAY TRADE RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** For `Vertex`, `BeamEnd` and `BakedVertex` the parameter **is** the point, so rounding the parameter and rounding the point are the same act. For a box they are not: `center` and `size` encode eight corners, and the current solve puts **half** the delta into `center` and the whole of it into `size` so that the far corner's two halves *cancel* (`forge_doc.cpp:1528-1543`, and `aBoxCornerDragResizesTheBoxAndPinsTheOppositeCorner` at `forge_doc_tests.cpp:1336`). **Three properties are desirable and only two can hold at once**: the far corner stays pinned, the dragged corner lands on the grid, and both parameters land on the grid. Rounding `center` and `size` independently drifts the far corner by up to 0.75 of a quantum — ten orders of magnitude worse than today's few-ULP cancellation, on a property this repo asserts in a test and states in three comment blocks.
- **⚑ THE FORGE ALREADY HAS A METRIC GRID, WHICH IS WHY SNAPPING IS THE TOOL'S EXISTING LANGUAGE AND NOT A NEW IDEA.** `addGrid` (`tools/forge/src/main.cpp:98-121`) sizes its cell from the camera so it *"reads as a ruler at 0.1 m and at 100 m without the author touching anything"*, and the panel prints the live cell. An author is already looking at a ruler; the numbers they produce should be on one.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **Quantization happens in `forgeMovePoint`, on the value written into the `ForgeDoc`** — never in `appendNumber`, `appendMeshNumber` or `writeForge`, for the two reasons above. The writer stays a faithful report of the document. (2) **The quantum is a fixed `1e-4` metres**, a named constant beside the existing `kMinimumRun`. **It is deliberately NOT camera-adaptive and NOT a UI control**: a file whose contents depend on how far the author had zoomed in is not reproducible, and the measurement above shows one fixed grid already covers every value in the repo. (3) **The box derives its parameters from the quantized dragged corner and the unchanged far corner**, rather than quantizing `center` and `size` independently — so the pin stays exact-to-rounding, as it is today, and the dragged corner still lands on the grid. `center` consequently lands on a *half*-grid (5e-5), which is the honest price of the trade and is one extra digit at most. (4) **A drag whose quantized result equals the stored value is not written at all**, extending E2's zero-delta rule from *exactly zero* to *below the grid*. A 0.01 mm twitch now writes nothing instead of writing noise, which **strengthens** the fixed point E1 and E2 fought for rather than adding a second rule beside it. (5) **Baked vertices quantize on the same grid.** They already pass through `appendMeshNumber`'s float round trip, but that bounds them at nine digits rather than making them readable, and a dragged baked vertex is as much an authored edit as any other. Untouched baked vertices are not rewritten, so the asteroid's byte-for-byte proofs are unaffected. (6) **No change to the six committed assets.** This phase changes what a *future* drag writes; it does not reformat anything already in the repo.

**A. The grid, and the three kinds where the parameter is the point.** A `quantizePoint(BuildPoint, double)` helper in `forge_doc.cpp` rounds each component to the nearest multiple of the quantum. `forgeMovePoint`'s `Vertex`, `BeamEnd` and `BakedVertex` cases quantize the summed result before `set`. The no-op guard at `:1508` is extended: after quantization, a write whose result equals the value already stored is skipped, so the all-or-nothing validation above it still sees the move but the document is not touched. The beam's `kMinimumRun` collapse check runs against the **quantized** ends, so the refusal and the write agree about where the beam is.

**B. The box, with its far corner still pinned.** The `BoxCorner` case stops splitting the delta blind. It computes the dragged corner's current position from `center` and `size`, adds the local delta, **quantizes that corner**, then solves `size` componentwise from the quantized corner against the pinned opposite corner and sets `center` to their midpoint. The inside-out guard already at `:1453-1464` is evaluated against the solved `size`, so a drag that would cross the far corner is still refused before anything is written.

**Tests (`geometry.unit`, `forge_doc_tests`).** A dragged triangle vertex writes a value on the grid, and `writeForge` renders it in **four decimal places or fewer** — the assertion is on the emitted text, because the text is the thing the note was about. A drag of half a quantum writes **nothing at all** and leaves the document byte-identical (decision 4). A box corner drag lands the dragged corner on the grid **and** leaves the opposite corner where it was to within 1e-12, which is the existing pin assertion kept at its existing strength (decision 3). A beam end and a baked vertex both quantize. An authored value that is already on the grid survives a drag-and-drag-back unchanged. And the standing guard: `writeForge(parseForge(f)) == f` still holds byte for byte for all six committed assets, proving the writer was not touched. **The Forge's viewport still gets no suite** (AGENTS §7) — the drag arithmetic is all in `sol::assets` and is reachable headlessly, which is why this is testable at all.

**Exit.** Repeat the user's own edit live in `forge.exe`: drag the cockpit cowl apex, save, and `git diff` — the four changed lines read as short decimals rather than 17-digit doubles, and nothing else in the file moves. Drag a box corner in `station.forge` and confirm the opposite corner does not move. Click a point and release without dragging, and confirm `git status` reports **no change at all**. All 11 suites green, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings. **No save change, no sim change, no renderer change** — this phase touches the authoring path only.

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No author-facing grid control and no camera-adaptive quantum** — decision 2; if an author ever needs a coarser snap that is a UI item with its own verdict, not a silent dependency of the file format. **No reformatting of the six committed assets** — decision 6. **No change to `appendNumber`, `appendMeshNumber` or `writeForge`** — the writer is correct and the round-trip tests are the guard that says so. **No change to uv, smoothing-angle or revolve-profile precision.** **No axis or plane constraint on a drag** — snapping a free drag to a grid is not the same feature as constraining it to an axis, and the second is E4/E5 territory. **Nothing about `shipped::build*` or the transcription tests**, whose tension with an editable asset is recorded as a known gap below rather than resolved here.

**⚑ PHASE 14 IS COMPLETE** (`9febba9` spec, `461e894` A+B, `42c04d4` the rounding fix the drive found). `geometry.unit` **94 → 99**. **No save change, no sim change, no renderer change** — the authoring path only.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS A NEW SHAPE FOR THIS PROJECT: A LIVE DRIVE CAUGHT WHAT THE UNIT TESTS MISSED, TWICE IN A ROW, AND BOTH MISSES WERE ONE FACT.** `1.0 + 0.1568` is **not** the double nearest `1.1568` — adding a small number to a larger one shifts the exponent, so the sum lands a ULP off the decimal and `appendNumber`, faithfully reporting the double it was handed, spells all seventeen digits again. **Two clean decimals do not add to a clean decimal in binary.** The first attempt solved `center` and `size` back from a rounded corner and produced `center = 0.07840000000000003` beside a clean `size`; the second rounded the step instead and produced a clean `center` beside `size = [1.1567999999999996, …]`. **Same defect, opposite half, both times the value was RIGHT and unreadable.** The fix is to round the step going in **and** the result coming out.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE REASON THE TESTS MISSED IT IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: THE TEST PICKED A LUCKY ADDEND.** `1.0 + 0.3183` *does* land on the double nearest `1.3183`; `1.0 + 0.1568` does not. A unit test asserting the emitted **text** was already in place and still passed, because whether the noise appears depends on the specific value. **A rounding bug is not reproducible from an arbitrary example — the example has to be the one that failed**, which is why the numbers the Forge actually wrote are now pinned as their own test.
- **⚑⚑ THE SAME GRID HAS TO BE APPLIED TO THE *DELTA* FOR A DERIVED PARAMETER AND TO THE *RESULT* FOR A DIRECT ONE, AND THAT ASYMMETRY IS THE DESIGN.** `p0`/`from`/`to`/a baked vertex **are** the point, so rounding the result is exact and clean. A box's `center` and `size` are **derived** from two corners, so rounding the corner and solving back injects arithmetic noise into both — while rounding only the step leaves each one addition from a number the author wrote in decimal. **A box centre also needs its own half-grid** (0.05 mm), because a centre is the midpoint of two corners and rounding it to the coarser grid would move one of them.
- **⚑⚑ EXPRESSING THE GRID AS STEPS-PER-METRE RATHER THAN AS A STEP IS LOAD-BEARING, AND IT WAS CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED RATHER THAN AFTER.** The obvious `std::round(v / 1e-4) * 1e-4` **multiplies** by 1e-4, which is not representable in binary, so the result can land a ULP off the nearest decimal — the whole phase fails on that one line. Dividing by an exact power of ten yields the double nearest `n/10000`. **This is the same fact as the headline, met a third time**, and it is why the constant is `kGridStepsPerMetre = 10000.0`.
- **⚑ THE GRID IS A PROPERTY OF THE AUTHORED NUMBER, NOT OF THE VIEWPORT, AND ONE EXISTING TEST IS WHERE THAT SHOWS.** Snapping happens in the part's **own** frame, so under a parent scaled 2× the world landing sits up to ~1.03e-4 off the cursor (measured 4.4e-5). Snapping in **world** space instead would defeat the phase entirely: the inverse transform of a round world number is an irrational local one, which is precisely what this phase exists to stop writing.
- **⚑ THE ZERO-DELTA RULE GOT STRONGER RATHER THAN GAINING A NEIGHBOUR.** E2's guard refused a write whose delta was *exactly* zero; it now also refuses one whose quantized result equals the stored value. A hand that twitched by a few microns writes nothing. **The outer guard still earns its place separately**: it is what stops a click with no drag from snapping a point that was authored off the grid.
- **⚑ AND ONE ESTIMATE IN THE SPEC WAS WRONG IN THE EXPENSIVE DIRECTION.** The spec framed the box as a three-way trade and chose *solve against the pinned corner*, predicting the cost would be "one extra digit at most" in `center`. **That was the implementation the drive rejected** — the cost was not one digit, it was seventeen. The formulation that actually works is the one the spec described as what it was replacing, with the step quantized.

**Live proof (`forge.exe`, driven; the assets restored afterwards).** **⚑ The user's own edit, repeated:** dragging the cockpit's cowl apex now writes `p2 = [0.3616, -0.0296, -7.1164]` where the playtest's save wrote `[0.009169150493107736, 0.3069186387490481, -7.117375393866678]` — **exactly 4 insertions and 4 deletions**, the one shared corner across the four `cowl_*` triangles, every other line of a 141-point file untouched. **The box path, on `cube.forge`** (the asset that authors nothing, so a save writes precisely two lines): `center = [0.0784, -0.0562, -0.0391]`, `size = [1.1568, 0.8876, 0.9218]` — six numbers, all four decimals or fewer — while the tool's own report held `bounds min -0.500 -0.500 -0.500` **unchanged**, which is the pin surviving the rounding. **The no-op, with its positive control in the same run**: a click with no drag on the marker left the file **byte-identical by SHA256**, and a real drag *from the same pixel* changed it — so the negative is a refusal, not a miss. **99 `geometry.unit` tests, 11 suites green in 81.0 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.**

**Known gaps.** **A point authored OFF the grid is snapped onto it by any real drag** — deliberate, and invisible in this repo because no authored number has more than three decimals, but it means a future asset with finer authored detail would be quietly coarsened the first time someone drags it. **The quantum is fixed and has no author-facing control**, so an asset authored at a scale where 0.1 mm is coarse has no recourse but to edit the file by hand. **A box authored off-grid keeps its far corner exactly and stays off-grid**, so the two kinds disagree about what happens to an odd number — the direct kinds snap, the box does not. **Nothing rounds uv coordinates, smoothing angles or revolve profiles**, which keep full precision by design and would show the same seventeen digits if a future tool ever dragged one. **The Forge's viewport still has no suite** (AGENTS §7); everything above is asserted through `sol::assets` headlessly plus the drive.

**⚑ Known tension recorded before building, because it is the next question and not this phase's.** Five of the six shipped assets are pinned against a hand-written C++ recipe (`shipped_mesh_tests.cpp:91-103`, bounds plus four hashes each), so **any genuine Forge edit to a shipped asset breaks `geometry.unit` until someone hand-edits C++ and re-derives four hashes** — as the user's cowl drag did, at 93/94. That test earned its place: it is what allowed `gen_assets.ps1`'s mesh half to be deleted, and it proves the C++ port matches the PowerShell original. But the original is gone and the Forge can now edit these files, so the test may have become a ratchet against the tool it was built to enable. **Not resolved here** — it is a real decision with a real risk (deleting it removes the only thing pinning five shipped meshes) and it deserves its own checkpoint.

### Phase 15 — Pick The One You Mean: Reverse Cycling, A Set Target That Works, And A Map You Can Click ✅ (completed 2026-08-20)

**From playtest session 12 (2026-08-20), three notes, all on one seam — how a player picks the thing they mean.** Verbatim: *"when cycling through targets it would be useful to be able to go in the reverse direction when trying to pick a specific target." / "set target and autopilot buttons in system map dont seem to work." / "also should be able to click on the actual systems and points of interest in the galaxy/system maps to select things and not just the list on the side."* The board had been empty of player-found work for exactly one session.

**Diagnosis (read from the code as it stands, before any change).**

- **⚑⚑ NOTE 2 IS A REAL DEFECT AND THE WIRING IS A RED HERRING — IT IS ONE INDEX MEANING TWO DIFFERENT THINGS.** `executeMapAction` (`map_ui.cpp:638-647`) handles both kinds correctly and calls `world.selectTarget` / `engageAutopilot`. **The bug is what it is handed.** `state.selectedMarker` is a **row index into `panel.markers`**; `SpaceWorld::selectTarget` (`space_world.cpp:4997`) wants a **nav-target index into `m_targets`**. The local fill (`map_ui.cpp:529-537`) loops `i` over **every** nav target and `continue`s past the fogged ones, pushing a row only for the visible — so each undiscovered target ahead of the selection shifts the row index down by one.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE FAILURE IS SILENT, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY IT READS AS "DOESN'T WORK" RATHER THAN "PICKS THE WRONG THING".** `selectTarget` guards with `if (index < m_targets.size() && !navTargetVisible(index)) return false;` (`:5007`), so a shifted index landing on a fogged slot does **nothing at all** — no error, no selection, no feedback. When it happens to land on a visible slot it selects **the wrong object**, which is the worse half and the one nobody would report as a bug.
- **⚑⚑ THE ROW ALREADY KNOWS THE ANSWER AND THROWS IT AWAY.** The `markerRows.push_back` at `map_ui.cpp:603-612` sets `.targeted = i == targeted` — it has `i` in hand — and stores **`bookmarkId` but not `i`**.
- **⚑⚑ AND THIS PROJECT SOLVED THIS EXACT PROBLEM ONCE ALREADY AND DID NOT GENERALISE IT — THAT IS THE FINDING.** `Kind::DeleteBookmark`'s own comment (`map_ui.cpp:649-653`) records that the action used to read the id back off the nav-target slot, and that on a remote view *"that lookup would have deleted whichever bookmark happened to occupy that slot back home."* **8q's fix was to put a stable handle on the row — `bookmarkId` — and it stopped there.** Set Target and Autopilot were left on the row index. **Eighth consecutive item where the reasoning the phase needs was already authored in the repo and simply not applied here.**
- **⚑ PHASE 8z IS WHAT TURNED A LATENT MISMATCH INTO AN EVERYDAY ONE.** Before the fog every nav target was visible, so row index == nav index and the code was accidentally correct. Probed live this session: a fresh Lyrioa charts **1 of its 3 stations**, so rows are off by two almost immediately. **The defect is therefore NOT reproducible in a fully-surveyed system**, and any test has to fog something first.
- **⚑⚑ NOTE 1's OBVIOUS ANSWER IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE, AND THE SECOND REASON IS DECISIVE ON ITS OWN.** `Shift+T` is not expressible: `InputChord` (`input_bindings.hpp:26`) is `kind` + `code` — one key or one mouse button, **no modifier field** — so it would have to grow one, touching the by-name settings serialization, `chordName`, and the rebind capture UI. **And even if it existed it would be wrong here, because `LeftShift` IS Boost** (`input_actions.cpp:47`): every reverse-cycle press would fire the engines. **Two plain actions is not the cheap compromise, it is the correct design.**
- **⚑ THE ACTION TABLE MAKES TWO NEW ACTIONS GENUINELY CHEAP.** `kActions` is one row per action — id, group, label, default — behind a `static_assert(… == kActionCount)` whose comment is *"every action needs exactly one row"*, and the Controls screen is generated from that table. So each new action arrives rebindable and listed with no screen work. **Free keys, checked against all 35 rows: `J`, `K`, `L`, `N`, `P`, `U`** (`J` was freed when 8v deleted `Action::Jump`).
- **⚑ `cycleNavTarget()` AND `cycleContact()` BOTH HARDCODE `+1`** at `space_world.cpp:5030`/`:5044` and `:5128`, and neither takes a direction. `cycleContact` walks a **ranked** order (`contactOrder`) rather than the raw list, so its reverse must step the ranking, not the slot.
- **⚑ NOTE 3 IS GENUINELY UNBUILT, AND ITS CONSTRAINT IS THAT THE PRESS IS ALREADY SPOKEN FOR.** `updateMapView` (`map_screen.cpp:136`) handles wheel-zoom and drag-pan only; there is no marker hit-testing anywhere. **`input.mousePressed && inside` already starts a pan-drag** (`:161`), so a click-to-select has to be a *release* that never became a drag — `state.dragging` and `state.dragAnchor` already carry what that needs.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE HIT TEST MUST SHARE THE DRAW, NOT THE ANSWER.** A marker's map-space position is not a field — it is derived inside `drawSystemMap` by the `project` lambda at `map_screen.cpp:391`, which picks the orbital or playfield tier and then applies the magnifier. **Recomputing that in a separate pick function is the duplicate-expression trap Phase 13 recorded**; the pick belongs where `project` already is.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **`MapMarkerRow` carries the nav-target index it was built from**, exactly as 8q gave it `bookmarkId`, and Set Target / Autopilot pass **that** rather than the row index. Remote rows have no nav target and carry a `kNoIndex` sentinel; the buttons are already unreachable there because `viewIsCurrent` swaps them for Plot Route / Show Current, so the sentinel is a guard rather than a path. (2) **Reverse cycling is two new actions, not a modifier** — for both reasons above, the `LeftShift`-is-Boost collision being decisive. Defaults **`U` = Cycle Nav Target Back** and **`N` = Cycle Contact Back**, each a free key in the same physical neighbourhood as its forward key, and both rebindable from the Controls screen like every other row. (3) **Direction is a parameter, not a second function**: `cycleNavTarget(int step)` and `cycleContact(int step)` with `+1`/`-1`, so forward and back cannot drift apart. Backward wrap is `(slot + n - 1) % n` on `std::size_t` — never `slot - 1`. (4) **Reverse from a nav target lands on the TAIL of the contact order**, mirroring forward's documented landing on the head, so a back-press after a forward-press returns where you were. (5) **A click selects; it does not act.** Clicking a marker sets the same selection the list row sets — it does not target, autopilot or plot. The footer buttons stay the only things that act, so a stray click on a map can never engage an autopilot. (6) **The pick happens inside the draw**, next to `project`, and is reported back rather than recomputed. (7) **A click that moved is a drag, not a pick** — the release only selects if the cursor stayed within a few pixels of the press, so panning never re-selects.

**A. The handle: Set Target and Autopilot select what the player clicked.** `MapMarkerRow` gains a nav-index field. `map_ui.cpp`'s local fill sets it to `i` at push; `fillRemoteMarkers` sets the sentinel. `map_screen.cpp`'s two footer buttons read it off the selected row instead of passing `state.selectedMarker`. `executeMapAction` is unchanged — it was always correct. **The `.targeted` flag is switched to compare against the same field**, so the ring on the map and the thing Set Target picks cannot disagree.

**B. Reverse cycling.** `cycleNavTarget`/`cycleContact` take a step. Two rows join `kActions`; `kActionCount` and the `static_assert` follow. The two new actions dispatch beside the existing `T`/`C` handling in `main.cpp`. The fog walk in `cycleNavTarget` already loops over every slot looking for a visible one — it walks in the step's direction rather than always forward, so reverse skips undiscovered targets on the same terms.

**C. Clicking the map.** `drawGalaxyMap` and `drawSystemMap` each take the pending pick point and return the index they hit, nearest-wins within a screen-space radius, computed through the `project` they already use. `map_screen.cpp` decides a pick happened on mouse **release** inside the map when `state.dragging` never exceeded the drag threshold, then writes the result into `state.selectedSystem` or `state.selectedMarker` — the same fields the list rows write, so the list highlight, the footer detail line and the buttons all follow for free.

**Tests (`ui.unit` for the rows and the pick, `sim.unit` for the cycles).** The index fix is asserted the only way it can be: **build a marker list with a fogged target ahead of the selection and prove the row's nav index is not its row index**, then that the value the footer passes selects the intended target — a test that fails against today's code. Reverse cycling: a forward press then a back press returns the original selection, over both the nav list and the contact ranking; a back press from the head wraps to the tail; and **a back press walks past a fogged nav target** exactly as forward does. The pick: a point on a marker returns that marker, a point on empty space returns nothing, the nearest of two candidates wins, and a release that moved beyond the threshold returns nothing. **The map's draw still has no suite** (AGENTS §7) — what is tested is the pick arithmetic and the row plumbing, both reachable headlessly.

**Exit.** Live in `sol.exe`, in a **fresh** game where the fog is real: open `M`, System tab, select a station that is not the first row, press **Set Target**, and confirm the HUD's target readout names **that** station — the assertion is the name, not that something happened. Then **Autopilot** from the same row and confirm the ship flies to it. Press `T` twice and `U` once and confirm the selection returns. Click a marker on the system map and a system on the galaxy map and confirm each selects without acting. Drag-pan across a marker and confirm the selection does **not** change. All 11 suites green, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings. **No save change and no renderer change.**

**Out of scope (named so they are not half-built).** **No modifier support in `InputChord`** — decision 2; if chords ever need modifiers that is its own item with its own serialization question, not a silent dependency of this one. **No click-to-ACT** — decision 5; double-click-to-autopilot is a separate verdict. **No hover tooltip on map markers** — the list already has Phase 10's, and adding a second surface here is scope this note did not ask for. **No change to the contact RANKING itself**, only to which direction it is walked. **No reverse for `O`/`H`** — those select outright rather than cycling, so a direction is meaningless. **Nothing about the shipped-mesh transcription ratchet**, which bit again this session on a human's real Forge edit and still deserves its own checkpoint.

**⚑ PHASE 15 IS COMPLETE** (`ed2e7b0` spec, `0e69028` the debt slice + stage A, `420f94e` B, `c302e23` C). **Twelve suites now**: `game.unit` is new at **7**, `ui.unit` **87 → 91**. No save change, no renderer change.

**⚑ What building it changed about the plan.**

- **⚑⚑ THE HEADLINE IS A LINKAGE BOUNDARY, AND IT INVALIDATED THIS PHASE'S OWN TEST PLAN BEFORE A LINE OF IT WAS WRITTEN.** The spec above says *"`sim.unit` for the cycles"*. **`sim.unit` links `sol::sim` — `engine/sim` — and `cycleNavTarget` lives in `game/src/space_world.cpp`. It could never have seen it.** Checking properly found the general case: **every one of the eleven suites linked an `engine/` library and NOTHING linked `game/src`**, which `add_executable(sol_game)` held whole. So the map, the screens and the targeting code had no regression test **not through neglect but because no target could see them** — and that is exactly the structural cause recorded when `encodeMesh` sat in the cooker's `main.cpp` outside `sol_cooker_lib`. **Second sighting of the same shape, and the second time the fix was a library split rather than more diligence.** The user chose the debt slice over shipping around it, which is the fourth time this project has picked debt over a stage.
- **⚑⚑ AND THE DEFECT ITSELF WAS THE EIGHTH CONSECUTIVE ITEM WHERE THE REPO HAD ALREADY WRITTEN THE ANSWER DOWN.** 8q hit this exact bug from the delete side, recorded it in a comment that is still there (*"would have deleted whichever bookmark happened to occupy that slot back home"*), fixed it by putting a stable `bookmarkId` handle on the row — **and stopped**. Set Target and Autopilot were left on the row index for four phases. **The fix was to generalise a handle the codebase already had, not to invent one.**
- **⚑⚑ A TEST THAT PASSES ON THE BROKEN CODE IS THE REAL RISK HERE, AND IT TOOK TWO SEPARATE GUARDS.** Row index and nav slot **agree by accident** in a fully-surveyed system, so the obvious test passes against the defect. Hence a second test asserting the shift is **real** (`targets.size() > markers.size()`, and that the row number *would* have named something else). **Verified by reintroducing the bug**: the row index makes two of three tests fail, naming a hidden target and a wrong name. Separately, the contact-direction test was **vacuous on first writing** — this fixture calls `generateUniverse` without `initializeFactions`, so no ambient wing ever flies and `contactOrder` came back empty, and the test returned early while reading as a pass. It spawns its own ships now.
- **⚑ THE OBVIOUS BINDING WAS THE WRONG ONE, FOR A REASON THAT HAD NOTHING TO DO WITH COST.** `Shift+T` is unexpressible (`InputChord` is one key, no modifier field) — but **even with modifiers it would have been wrong, because `LeftShift` is Boost**, so every step backwards through a target list would light the engines. **Two plain actions is the correct design, not the cheap one.**
- **⚑ THE PICK HAD TO BE FED FROM THE DRAW, AND THAT PAID TWICE.** A marker's screen position is derived inside `drawSystemMap` by a local `project` lambda that picks the orbital or playfield tier and then magnifies; a separate pick pass would have been a second expression of it. Feeding `NearestPick` from the draw's own loop also **inherits the fog skip for free** — a dot the draw does not draw is a dot the pick cannot return — which is a rule nobody had to write twice.
- **⚑ AND ONE THING THE DRIVE SHOWED THAT NO TEST WOULD HAVE: A DRAG AT ZOOM 1.0 DOES NOTHING AT ALL.** `panLimit` clamps the pan slack to zero when the map is not zoomed, so the drag-is-not-a-click case is confirmed by a frame in which *nothing whatsoever* moved. Correct, and worth knowing before someone reads a future no-op drag frame as a broken pan.

**Live proof (`sol.exe`, driven twice, both runs identical, clean exit, 0 validation messages).** **Stage A, the defect:** the System tab lists `station Lyrioa Alpha / planet Lyrioa I / star Lyrioa`; clicking **row 2** and pressing **Set Target** reads back `SETTARGET Lyrioa I`. Under the old code row index 1 went to nav slot 1 — a **fogged station** — and `selectTarget` refused it in silence, which is precisely what the player reported. **Stage B:** `CYCLE0 Lyrioa I`, `T` → `Lyrioa`, `T` → `Lyrioa Alpha`, **`U` → `Lyrioa`** — the back-step lands exactly where the first forward step had been. **Stage C:** clicking the star's dot moved the list highlight, the marker ring and the footer detail (`390332 km` → `51.18 Mkm`) onto `Lyrioa` **while the target diamond stayed on `Lyrioa I`** — selecting without acting, decision 5 in one frame. A click on empty map space changed nothing; a **drag beginning on `Lyrioa Alpha`'s dot** left the selection on `Lyrioa`. **12 suites green in 82.1 s, `dev-gpu` 2/2, zero warnings.**

**Known gaps.** **The click radius is a fixed 14 px in virtual UI pixels**, so a very crowded playfield bubble at zoom 1 can still put two markers inside one grab — nearest wins, and zooming in is the answer the map already has. **A click cannot select something the map does not draw**, which is correct but means a fogged station is unreachable by mouse exactly as it is by cycle. **`game.unit` links the whole game library including the renderer-facing sources**; nothing tests them and nothing should (AGENTS §7), but they are now linked into a headless binary and a future static initializer touching a device would surface there first. **The fixture builds a galaxy without `initializeFactions`**, so its faction table is empty — fine for every rule under test here, and stated in the file so nobody reads `0 faction(s)` as a break.

### Phase 16 — An Asset You Can Edit: What The Shipped-Mesh Tests Should Assert Now ✅ (completed 2026-08-20)

**The checkpoint Phase 14 asked for and Phase 15 deferred, held 2026-08-20 after the ratchet bit a second time on a real human edit.** Playtest session 12 left a dirty `cockpit.forge` carrying a `bake` and a cowl-apex drag; `geometry.unit` ran 99 and failed 1. The edit was correct, the tool worked, and the test suite rejected it. **This phase decides what those tests are for now that the thing they were built to protect no longer exists.**

**Diagnosis (read from the code, and it moved the question twice).**

- **⚑ THERE ARE TWO FAMILIES IN `shipped_mesh_tests.cpp` AND ONLY ONE IS THE RATCHET.** `*ReproducesTheShippedMesh` (5) compares the **C++ recipe** in `shipped_meshes.hpp` against the embedded truth table and **never opens a `.forge` file** — it cannot break on an edit, and it did not. `the*ForgeSourceIsTheShippedMesh` (5) is the ratchet: `checkForgeSourceIsTheShippedMesh` compares the committed file to the recipe **vertex by vertex** *and then* runs the file's mesh through the hash table, which is why one edit produced four failures.
- **⚑⚑ THE RECIPE'S JOB IS DONE AND ITS COUNTERPARTY IS DELETED.** `shipped_meshes.hpp` (242 lines) exists to prove the C++ port of `gen_assets.ps1` landed on the same floats — its own header says so. **The script's mesh half and all five `.gltf` files were deleted at the D checkpoint.** So the two sides of Family 1 are now both frozen constants in this repo with nothing external to check against.
- **⚑⚑ BUT THE OBVIOUS MOVE — DELETE THE RATCHET — IS A TRAP, AND FINDING THAT IS WHY THIS CHECKPOINT WAS WORTH HOLDING. EVERY GEOMETRIC PROPERTY TEST IN THE SUITE IS POINTED AT THE RECIPE, NOT AT THE FILES.** `everyShippedMeshWeldsIntoAClosedManifoldSurface`, `weldingTheCubeFindsEightPointsUnderTwentyFourCorners` and `bakingAMeshAndReadingItBackIsTheSameMesh` all build from `shipped::build*()`. **Delete Family 2 on its own and the committed assets keep NO geometric validation at all** — a hole, an inverted face or a broken scale would reach the game unnoticed, because `cooker.unit`'s round trip is content-agnostic by design (it proves the pipeline is faithful to whatever the file says).
- **⚑ FAMILY 1 IS CHEAPER TO KEEP THAN TO REMOVE, AND ITS REAL VALUE IS NOT WHAT ITS NAME SAYS.** It exercises `addBox` ×17, `addFlatTriangle` ×14, `addBeam` ×10 and `addTorus` ×1, and pins their **exact float output**. `mesh_build_tests.cpp` already covers every primitive by *property* (box volume, beam ends, torus by Pappus, triangle winding, revolve border loops, degenerate refusal), so Family 1's distinct contribution is exactness, not coverage. **It never reads an asset, so it can never block an edit.** It stays, re-described as what it is: an exact-output pin on `MeshBuilder`.
- **⚑⚑ AND THERE IS A SECOND RATCHET NOBODY HAD NOTICED — IN A DIFFERENT SUITE, IN THE RIGHT SHAPE.** `forge.unit`'s `theCommittedMeshesAndTheirModelRowsStillDisagreeExactlyWhereTheyDid` already builds the committed `.forge` files and pins each one's radius **verdict and sign** against `game/data`'s `[[model]]` rows. That target already receives `SOL_MESH_SOURCE_DIR` **and** `SOL_MODEL_DATA_DIR`. It is softer than a hash — a boolean and a sign rather than bytes — and it is **already file-based**, which is the model this phase follows rather than replaces. `cockpit` is not in its list, which is why session 12's edit did not trip it.
- **⚑⚑ THE PATTERN THIS PHASE WANTS WAS ALREADY INVENTED IN THIS REPO, BY PHASE 12, FOR THE ONE ASSET THAT HAD NO SCRIPT TO TRANSCRIBE.** `gate_membrane` is pinned by `theGateMembraneFillsExactlyTheApertureTheCrossingTestUses` — **a relationship that matters** (the film fills the same 70 m the crossing test uses), not a hash. And `gate.forge` has never had an exact pin at all. **Two of seven assets have been freely editable the whole time and nothing has gone wrong.** Ninth consecutive item where the answer was already authored somewhere in the tree.
- **⚑ ONE ASSET IS NOT A CLOSED SOLID AND MUST BE EXCLUDED BY NAME.** `gate_membrane.forge` is a single `revolve` with profile `[[0,0],[70,0]]` — a flat disc whose centre collapses to a point. It is a film: it has a border loop by construction and asserting `isClosed()` on it would fail correctly.

**Decisions taken before any code.** (1) **The invariants move to the FILES; the exact match retires.** `the*ForgeSourceIsTheShippedMesh` stops comparing against the recipe and the hash table, and becomes a per-asset invariant check built from the committed `.forge`. (2) **Family 1 and `shipped_meshes.hpp` STAY, unchanged in code and re-described in comment** as an exact-output regression pin on `MeshBuilder` that deliberately reads no asset.
